As they approached they are sent a countdown timer (presumably to adversary arrival) and Heat gets a message on his secure Turing Army ops app, telling all who get it to head to the same location for a major strike against the AI and their allies! So they can assume they may be going up against the TA, and start wondering if the Null Sect is behind this, given their skill in manipulation. They are also sent an AR map, showing them a safe path through a maze of wrecked and rusted cars that block the approach to the partially buried low concrete bunkers that make up the facility. They pull the van into the largest one, and Perri greets them, explaining that they got a last minute warning of an impending attack and that the team are basically the physical defense. The overgrown bunkers should provide decent passive magical protection, and the Nexus has good matrix security, though she shows Heat quickly the path to the server room in a cavernous basement in case they need assistance there as well.

She passes over control of the Nexus’ drone fleet to Otters, who is quickly swaps a few weapons around between them and her own drones to optimize. She hides the van up in the mountain to be able to bring it and it’s HMG in behind the attackers as needed, and Ruxpin and Otters’ Lynx find a concealed sniper post at a decent range up the mountain. Aradia makes herself invisible and posts up on top of the tallest bunker, with the other Lynx and the 6 Doberman drones positioned at ambush points in the car maze, along with Heat and Echo. Otters parks her body inside in good cover within signal range, and they wait for the attack to commence. 4 black vans race in, crashing through the perimeter fence, and two of them start cautiously proceeding through the maze, with 5 heavily armored goons from each of the other vans following on foot, lobbing grenades over the tops to clear mines ahead of the vans.

The two emptied vans park across the entrance they’ve rammed into the fence, to protect their backs and maybe prevent escape attempts, which blocks Otters’ concealed van out as well, unless she shoots/rams through. The team tensely waits as the opps cautiously approach, until Aradia makes an executive decision and uses her magic pact to cast a massive area of Foreboding over one of the advancing groups. This causes the van to accelerate as the 5 behind it slow their advance, keeping to cover more, creating some opportunities. With the one van out front in the open, Echo and Ruxpin open up on with sniper rifle and shotgun, alongside Otters with one of the drones, making short work of it. 5 more goons pile out and race towards Echo and Heat but are also quickly gunned down by those two with assistance from Ruxpin.

Meanwhile, Otters has used missiles from the other Lynx as well as the pack of Dobermans to pin down and damage the other advancing goon squad behind the intact van. Heat races over to that side and gets blasted bad as goons from that van exit once it also gets wrecked. Aradia is frantically moving her Foreboding field around to effect the most targets while also making both Heat and Ruxpin invisible. Ruxpin is swapping positions between shots as well, and combined with the range he’s at, the goons seem unable to hit him. They do manage to take out all the Dobermans, and also hit Echo pretty hard before most of them get dropped as well.

With only 6 goons and two possibly empty vans remaining, the team gets another frantic call from Perri- the physical attack seems to have been a distraction! The main Nexus host is under attack from the Null Sect, and they need help. She tells Heat they are running the Silver Book program, so the team just needs to get downstairs to the servers and touchlink in. She also requests they call any hackers they know who can get there in time, and the team rounds up an impressive list of contacts to get people to turn out. Deciding to split the party, they leave Otters with her van and drones as well as Ruxpin outside to mop up, while Heat, Echo and Aradia head inside.

Aradia quickly heals Heat as well as she can using her magic pact and they race in, headed for the musty basement full of every age of stack, server, cable and connector, all hodge-podged together. They also see some thick cables running off into the distance, implying some exterior connections of some sort. With their allies summoned, they prepare to jack in and try to hold off the Null Sect while the gunfight continues outside!

The team takes an overnight biz class flight from SeaTac to Atlanta, with their fake Seattle job SIN’s getting them through customs without any particular trouble, though the social members of the team have to briefly explain away Ruxpin’s strange noise-generating phenomenon. Also, as a lifelong Detroiter, she seems to have resented the way that ARES treated and then exited the city during the previous year’s bug crisis.

The team has extensive intel on building security, layout, schedule etc, as well as Collins’ schedule, and gets to work reviewing these and formulating a plan. With a few days to wait for their job gear, they decide to take Atlanta’s modern public transit over to the area, a new development biz zone on the edge of Downtown. The massive influx of ARES personnel and money seems to be shifting the center of gravity in the city towards ARES, with no other major AAA competition visible. Otters also finds some reports on the boards suggesting that ARES may be trying to foment unrest by arming and funding both pro- and anti-corp forces in order to themselves gain further power and control, with some speculating that the corp may be trying to create their own version of Aztlan’s relationship with AZT, but in the CAS.

They also hear some reports of a major shootout and undercover AZT incursion into Texas, seemingly trying to recover a lost asset, and think this may be related to their extraction of McClure in Seattle, as Grey Cell is likely moving him around a lot. Scouting the building, they find that the dossier seems accurate, but also gain a few further useful details to incorporate into their plans. They decide to tackle the bugs first, since they don’t need most of their gear for that part of the job. Heat flirts it up in the on-site bar in the lower floors public mall and arcade area, and gets invited up to employee housing. He purposefully has a not very memorable but not bad time, and gets some good interior security and protocols intel.

The next day, Otters builds a custom drone in the shell of one of the cleaning robots the ARES building uses. She adds two manipulator arms, one with tools and one with a data tap, as well as a small smuggling compartment for the bugs. They wait til the late evening, and then Aradia makes the drone look like one of the building security roto drones with a spell, and invisibly levitates herself up to a drone port on the floor of the conference rooms, while timing her ascent to avoid the watcher spirit patrols, as well as masking her spellcraft. Otters and the rest act as spotters on the TacNet to keep Aradia from getting clipped by any other drones or aircabs on the way.

At the 43rd floor, Aradia flies the drone up to the drone port, and Otters uses the arms to crack it open and jack in. Heat uses the direct access to bypass the public host and hack into the nested security host, adding the drones spoof chip to the approved users list for the port, while heading from the patrolling IC and observant Spider in the system. After the drone enters, with Aradia dropping her spell and descending, Heat removes it’s ID from the security list, and adds it to the approved cleaning drones for that floor. He stays in just long enough to confirm this gives it access through the small cleaning ports in the doors to the conference rooms before bailing out as his GOD score is dangerously high.

Otters pilots the drone around, using it’s gecko-tipped arms to carefully hide the bugs in obscure locations. She then takes the drone over to the main trash chute, partially disassembles it, and drives it in along with it’s assorted parts, to cover their tracks. They head back to the airport coffin motel again to rest, and finalize their plan for the extraction and faux assassination. The next day, Heat sets up a fake escort website, and programs a disguise mask for Aradia to match a profile. Aradia calls Collins to get her to book her fake profile for the evening, which Collins does, and also coaches Collins on how to behave and what to wear to not raise suspicions.

Ruxpin and Otters head to the river, and find some of the massive Mimic Snakes Otters heard rumors of basking and digesting in the sun. They trap a few and Ruxpin tears them open, finding some partially digested human and animal parts, which they bag up and mix with some acid and quicklime from a hardware store. They buy a fancy duffel bag and stuff it all in there for Aradia to smuggle in, and head over to the trainyard to pick up the van at the Maesk transit and shipping hub. With night descending and the plan in motion, Otters finds a stealthy spot to park nearby, after dropping Aradia off with her duffel of “toys” and changing the van appearance.

Aradia heads in, using her fake ID and profile, and gets a guest pass at the lobby reception, getting ushered up to Collins’ floor and residence by building security providing lowkey guidance over the internal sensors. Collins answers the door in appropriate negligee and both vamp it up a bit. As Aradia enters she casts a Silence spell, as Collins warns subtly that there is no video surveillance but may be audio. They dump the bag of dissolving bits in the bathtub, with Collins looking grossed out but keeping it together. She also casts a sterilize spell on the pile, to further obscure traces, and then cuts herself and bleeds on it a bit for good measure.

They then set up a fake ritual circle, with symbols copied from Black Lodge stuff Aradia has seen, and some burnt reagents and melted candles and the like scattered around, to make things further confusing. Aradia cleanses astral space to make it clear that someone cleaned up after, without actually having anything to obscure, aside from her own spellcraft. She also gets Collins to change into her mask and outfit, and makes herself invisible, once again instructing Collins how to behave to sell the trick. Aradia also levitates herself to not set off pressure sensors. With that, Collins (disguised as Aradia’s fake persona) exits the room with Aradia floating along invisibly behind her.

They descend to street level, with Collins managing to coyly bluff her way past the receptionists, who seems surprised to see “her” leaving so soon after arriving, apparently used to the executive’s bacchanals lasting somewhat longer. She carefully walks with the appropriate amount of sauciness (Aradia is a little impressed), and enters the van as Otters pulls it around, and drives off into the Atlanta night, preparing to hot foot it out of town to Nashville as soon as she clears ARES’ sensor area around their HQ and office complex.

Otters arranges to send her some nuyen to dig further into Project ANIMUS, and then reports this to the team, a bit concerned. PPJ takes a closer look at Otters, alongside Heat, and they find two interesting things: there is a hardwired offline chipslot with a well-concealed flesh port cover, and there is a long-healed circular burn scar just at the nape of Otters’ neck, a little larger than a standard data jack port size.

With Otters excited about the prospect of using BTL’s again, Heat investigates the chip slot further, and finds it is loaded with a running BTL, labelled “NOREGRETS”, and before anyone else can respond, Heat yanks it out, recording Otters face flash with some unfamiliar emotions as Heat reminds her what she did to him as a child, before Otters quickly snatches the chip back and reinserts, deciding she doesn’t need BTL’s in general, but definitely needs this one. The regret fades, but the memory of those momentary emotions remains. Heat slow-motions, edits and replays the brief expressions on Otters’ face, which he finds a bit emotionally helpful. Otters, worried about what this may have triggered, asks StepMAMA to check their outgoing comms, and she reports that when the chip was removed, “Otters” sent a single transmission to the NE grid- NYC/DeeCee/Boston area somewhere, to a repeater that has now gone offline.

Heat wants to investigate further, but they don’t have a good lead to follow at the moment, until in a panic, Otters recalls that she also paid Peregrine to look into Project ANIMUS. She calls him up, and he says he has just recovered from a bad injury but seems to think he’s never been to Denver and doesn’t recall their dealing there. He does remembers the team and all the Detroit stuff, but still very suspicious. Otters sends him proof of their meeting over his denials, and he says he’ll have his tech people analyze as he seems to believe that the team must have been fooled by an imposter. With that settled for now, they head to Arcane, relieve Magnum and Goldsmoke, who report no new threats, and head inside to debrief.

Arcane is relieved by their report, though also unhappy to “ally” with the Black Lodge at an level, and annoyed that they know his secret. However, he provides his own notes and data to disprove him being the cause, and they team sends them off to the BL dropbox. They get a message back a short while later saying their proof is acceptable, and also offering work against GW, if the team is interested. The BL also pass along a warning to Arcane- Disians have begun to encroach at the Seelie Court, and their attempts to warn people there have been met with deaf ears- perhaps confirming that the BL is indeed behind the Shadow Tourmaline in the Great Hall. Arcane also confirms that if the BL tries to blackmail him with knowledge of his Fae nature, threat of exposure etc, he will let the team know. He pays them well for their work, and the offers to guide a team therapy session, as they seems a little stressed and on edge.

They agree, and Heat and Otters get into it first- Heat asking more of Otters than she is capable of/willing to give in terms of real emotional response and accountability. They do make a little progress though, especially in terms of how to communicate less condescendingly on Otters’ behalf, and how to perhaps take some small wins on Heat’s. PPJ goes next and explains that he will be taking a break from the team, wanting to focus on ‘hooding and working directly for good (by his definition) against what he views as the evil alliance that has emerged (Disians etc, along with GW’s tyranny and AZT’s astral sacrifices to a lesser degree). The team is a bit surprised, and they get into a long discussion about morals vs money, personal motivations and philosophies and the like, and actually learn a good amount about one another.

Heat tries to leave things on a good note with PPJ, who kind of brushes him off, but seems good with the team overall, just headed in a different direction after a number of recent group decisions didn’t go his way, in a way he finds baffling and amoral. The rest of the team agrees to aim to do good a little more often as well, alongside getting paid, and arrange a way for Otters or others to directly hire Heat for bonus pay to help on some of those jobs when he is reluctant. PPJ still makes his exit, but says he hopes to see them out on the streets and will keep in touch. Echo and Aradia have less personal business to open up to the group, though they also get into their motivations, goals, etc. Arcane declares it a good session, and then tries to talk them all into removing team votes to kill one another, which they roundly refuse, so he backs off. They all feel that they have gotten significantly closer to him in the process as well, along with saving his life.

They head back to the safehouse and rest, and wake to some disturbing news from Detroit- Peregrine was hit and killed by an out of control self-driving car, blamed on the still under-reconstruction Motor City grid. Worried, they check STL news as well, and see that Niki was also taken out in a similar accident, though noone on the shadow boards seems to have made the connection besides them. They call Fargus Sol and warn him about ANIMUS, giving him the relevant details as they have them, and warning him off investigating it further due to the danger. He also reports in on his investigation into Le Masque’s background- he is indeed French, and worked with the vamp merc corp Messerzahn during the AZT/AM war, Bravo Company and the 77th Indy Rangers both consider him a dangerous adversary. The team decides to wait and see what he offers next before deciding if they want to keep working with him.

Sol also tells them that Grey Cell is ready for the team’s extraction job in Seattle, finally having gotten the opening they need. He offers transit docs, smuggling assistance, job ID’s, and expenses, plus he has some ideas for muscle in town to hire on, since PPJ called and told him he had left the crew. Sol suggests they hire someone before leaving Denver, as a Seattle local would be easier for AZT to trace afterwards. He gives them a secure number to call when they get to Seattle for their on the ground handler as well. They set up a meeting with his top reco for muscle, a skilled ex-miltary sniper/brawler named Ruxpin, who’s new to both Denver and running, so won’t have much a trail for AZT to follow. They set a meeting for a park in Brighton, and the take care of some prep biz for the day.

Heat also calls Hype and fills him in on ANIMUS (leaving Otters out for now), aiming to get him interested, and maybe does a little too good of a job, as Hype seems hyped to be getting further into the “making memories” biz. But Heat figures he has enough power and corp cover to be able to safely look into it. He tells the team after, and they all reluctantly conceded it’s a good idea. They also see some local news that Laffy and Tessa did indeed hit Dexter Wright as he was driving through the Hub, but came under heavy fire from his ARES security detail, and it is unclear if they survived. One BL local leader down though! And hopefully not directly traceable to the team. They head out to the meeting with Ruxpin, finding a somewhat scarily overgrown town turned park, with a menacing and somewhat hypnotic aura amidst the rampant growth.

Ruxpin is a large human, very muscular, with some high end implants and a military bearing. He explains he is a sniper and close combat specialist, but didn’t bring his gear to the meeting, and when questioned further, runs off overland to his safehouse and returns with some high end scout/spec ops equipment, which Echo ID’s as being PCC issue, though de-flagged. Ruxpin explains he is recently out of the PCC military, and Echo confirms that won’t be a problem, keeping some of his pro-UCAS feelings to himself in the moment. Ruxpin also manages to avoid taking too much offense at Heat’s bumbling questions about the location and political situation of the PCC, mistaking it briefly for the CAS before being gently corrected. Overall Ruxpin seems a bit brusque but quite confident in his abilities. More worryingly, he seems to passively give off some noise, which Heat detects.

So they decide Ruxpin while travel in the team van, which will be loaded with all their team gear, as his noise emittance could be an issue on commercial flights, while the rest of the team takes a biz-class sub-orbital. The van will be shipped overland to a seaport in a sealed container (with air and supplies) and then literally shipped up to Seattle, so the team will have a few days on the ground to make plans ahead of it’s arrival, which lines up with the mission timeline. Aradia also notices that there may be an astral crossing nearby in the park, and after some investigating, determines it may be to the plane of Beautiful Death, a strange and dangerous plant realm. ChewGum was fascinated with it, so they review his notes, and Aradia buys a fresh Vault of Ages to mount in the van, and preserve some samples from the park to bring to Shiwase Daigou on her return to Seattle, to pay off an old favor.

With that, the team stocks up on necessary gear, and Otters gets her permits in order to bring Morticia on the plane as her emotional support bird. They take off, with Heat rampantly flirting with the flight attendants, who bring him extra champagne, blankets, and the like (though these do turn out to be available upon request to the others as well). Echo also decides to play a fun prank, and tells one of the flight attendants it’s Heat’s birthday, getting everyone in biz class to sing him happy birthday as they descend into Seattle and present him with a warn cookie with a glowing e-candle. He’s delighted until he realizes Echo set it up, having thought his flirting has gotten him extra perks. But the cookie’s still good.

They land, and Heat calls up Marli Bremerton and Althea, and makes plans to swing by their Downtown mansion, as they explain they can’t go out at the moment. Otters heads downtown to seek out some private cyber-surgery, using the info broker Rei Bluestone to find a reliable and discrete clinic. Aradia makes an appointment with Shiawase Daigou to prevent the plant samples upon their expected arrival, and Echo kicks around, getting a feel for the town and quietly resenting the free city movement. Heat arrives to his music hang to find a haunted and well-protected Victorian mansion, a bit dilapidated and quite out of place in it’s relatively tony Downtown neighborhood, though it seems it has kept the block around it from getting too far ahead with it’s odd vibes.

Heat enters, carefully ignoring the various ghostly manifestations, glimpses of spectral fire and the like, as he isn’t interested or impressed. He is however, delighted to see Marli and Althea again, though still confused at how Althea is Marlis’ niece as they look about the same age. They tell him the house’s spirits are restless at the moment and they can’t go out, plus they don’t do live appearances in town. Heat is disappointed, until they invite him to jam with them at the house, and Heat gets permission to invite Aradia along to check out the astral and maybe do some fun illusions alongside Althea. They also tell him they’ll have a special guest arriving after dark, and then they spend some time noodling until then. Aradia arrives and quickly determines that the ghostly manifestations are from a bad house fire in the the early 1900’s, which seems to have kept them there, though they seem appeased by music, dancing, illusions etc as it seems like it was maybe a speak-easy of sorts.

Shortly after dark, a cloaked figure enters, and Heat is shocked to recognize the legendary rocker Jetblack as he slips out of his disguise. He also doesn’t seem to have aged a day, much like his 2050’s musical peer Marli. Odd. They get into jamming, with Jetblack complimenting Heat on his set in Denver, which he says he streamed to catch Marli’s gig. He tells Heat to start jamming and he’ll try to keep up, doing some impressively fast tapping on his classic axe to match Heat’s hyperspeed beats. Marli floats over the top with some synths, and they seem to really be locking into something as Heat sees a familiar strange silhouette appear on a nearby vidscreen, The wild-haired dancing figure once again offers inspiration, and Heat accepts, receiving a small program from a Music Sprite, and feeling like their performance has reached a transcendent level. Heat also spots vamp fangs as Jetblack gets into and makes some “guitar face”, which explains his apparent youth, but is still baffled by Marli as she doesn’t bear obvious signs.

Aradia is content to listen along with Althea, occasionally contributing some illusions that seem to please the spectral audience. The musicians all explain that this is just for them and can’t be shared elsewhere, as both Jetblack and Marli value their privacy. They do however invite Heat along to a digital gig, explaining that they’ve both cultivated various matrix Idol/Idoru personas over the years to keep performing while keeping to themselves IRL. He accepts, and they suggest he spend a little time developing a persona and a new sound before the gig, as the exclusive 6th level of the digital Dante’s Inferno club Downtown (well, in the matrix, but also streamed into the physical club). Excited to start figuring this out, Heat and Aradia leave just after Jetblack, just as the sun is starting to come up. They take public transit out to Auburn to meet up with Otters and Echo, who have secured a safehouse through their local Grey Cell contact, who Otters is delighted to find is her old friend Moreau!

However, Moreau seems to have gone even further down the “biodrones are inherently unethical, but sadly irreversible” path, and is glad Otters hasn’t made more since Morticia, though he seems to regret his part in crafting her. He explains that he has some new friends who keep him more level-headed, and Otters realizes he is talking about more animals and is maybe still spending too much time with them, though he seems overall to be doing a lot better mentally. They arrange to meet at a secure hacker space as the Clone Zone mall after the team links up, and check out the safehouse and parking situation at the semi-abandoned Auburn Junction mall. Aradia is a little saddened to remember that this was where Tabasco’s safehouse, complete with racecar bed, was located, as she looks at the dragon-blasted ruin of that corner of the complex.

They all rest a bit and head to the meet, finding Moreau a little distant as he rhapsodizes about his explorations of a newly “discovered” plane, Tandora, also sometimes referred to as the “land of dinosaurs” which he says is misrepresentative, though not entirely untrue. They get him back on task, and he introduces them to his pair of hell-hound BFF"s, Stoker and Shelley, and tells them about some other members of his pack as well. He seems a little relieved to be talking to real live humans again, though, and gives them the mission briefing. Handing over a data chip with the specifics, he provides the overview. Their target, an AZT exec and board member named McClure, will secretly be in town to deal with some money management issues for the corp.

For background, Moreau explains that many corps had kept sizeable investments with Brackhaven Investments in case the former Governor regained power, especially if the Free City agenda failed or if the UCAS pushed back. But even with the ascension of the Arch-Conservatives in the post-Blackout election in the UCAS, Seattle remains free, and Corinne Potter remains popular and in charge (and seemingly not a despot, at least not openly), so the corps are starting to distance themselves from Brackhaven as he and his politics look like more of a liability than an asset in the new Seattle. However, as they don’t want to completely burn the contact, AZT is sending a high level exec to do the final negotiations on divestments and fund transfers. This is the first and best chance Grey Cell has encountered to extract McClure since he made contact with Ringmaster when the team last visited Zecorporatum.

Moreau suggests extracting him during the meeting, as most of his security will be a few floors away, so they might be able to avoid major combat if they’re careful. He also explains that due to compartmentalization, he doesn’t himself know the name of their inside source, he is just running this op. But the source may also need to be extracted from the meeting if they aren’t able to avoid attending and extract themselves, as this job will burn them for sure. Moreau tells them that when they are ready to go in, they are to make contact with a local runner named “the Chromed Accountant”, who will give them the source’s name and biometrics if they need to be extracted as well. Moreau says that they have arranged for a popular local street racing club to be holding a race Downtown near to the office at the time of the meeting to distract cops and provide some cover if the team wants to make use of that as well.

He suggests that the team pose as human supremacists when extracting McClure, as they can easily pass since they are currently an all-human team, which they all suddenly feel a bit weird about. But Moreau suggests that it’s very plausible that Brackhaven’s allies in the supremacist movement would take offense at the corps distancing themselves, and want to make an example of an exec if they can get their hands on one. So this could be good misdirection, especially if they feel like faking McClure’s death in the process. He assumes AZT will have ritual samples and use them to track/confirm death, and says that Grey Cell has a plan to get him out of the city, but won’t give the team details until they are out of the office tower with the target day of. The team agree, and plans to spend the next day and a half doing recon, prep, and planning until Ruxpin arrives with the gear and the van and it’s go time.

Meanwhile, Ruxpin cleans his gun, polishes his knucks, does push-ups, and lightly journals as he feels the container he is in shift from rail to sea on his long journey to Seattle.

Arcane's Bane

Arriving back at the safehouse after the Vishala interrogation, the team debriefs Aradia and ends up in a lengthy argument about what info to double sell when ethics are involved, with some of the team insisting that ethics don’t factor in (for or against), and after refusing to agree on base definitions of terms they take an inconclusive vote leaving noone particularly happy. Aradia catches the team up on what she’s been up to, and explains her revelation that the extermination of the Sisterhood of Ariadne was likely the fault of MCT/Dis rather than the Black Lodge, though the BL had infiltrated the group and was effectively running it in secret.

They tell Fargus Sol what they feel they can share without violating client confidentiality with “Le Masque” as they are now calling him. They also haven’t decided for sure how to respond to the revelation of his “true” identity, though Otters and the other Detroit survivors still on the team recall making a deal with AZT to do deep cover work for them in Denver in exchange for smuggling them and their gear in, and realize that Le Masque may have been so chill about it because he sort of thought they already knew. Regardless, PPJ doesn’t want to work with him further and some of the others are iffy but he did promise higher pay for the next job so TBD.

They do some research and find that he has a bad (or at least dark) rep in certain circles due to his use of Blood Magic on prisoners during the AZT/AM war from the early/mid-70’s, but don’t find conclusive stuff of him being explicitly worse than others involved. He has a rep for being dangerous and very skilled at illusion, disguise, accent etc, but isn’t known for betrayal, except when he’s on an infiltration mission against an opponent. So they ask Sol to also dig into Le Masque’s background a little further in exchange for tipping him to dig further into the Asintmah Alliance. He had heard of the group but doesn’t seem fully clued in, so they hope he can get there without having to compromise their norms, as he often seems to have better info than them anyway. Noone is willing to give Heat permission to share info on an equivalent basis with Horizon so they leave it there.

He reminds them they have an extraction coming up for Grey Cell after Otters inquires about work opportunities, and they say they will start getting ready soon and head for Seattle when they’re ready. Aradia also calls up and threatens Vishala’s former boss at MCT, Shinju Sho, who says they will regret not sharing intel as promised, but then Aradia covers her tracks by sending evidence that Sho was trying to hire the team on the side without company approval to her contact Carol Staples in Chicago. The team hopes this will effectively neutralize Sho, who had already suffered an embarrassing demotion due to Vishala, but time will tell. They rest, and upon awakening, receive the panicked call from Arcane- someone is trying to kill them!

They head over to his office as he says he is safe for now, but doesn’t want to debrief on the phone. Arriving, they look around, and seeing no immediate threats in any realm are allowed in. Arcane talks them through a series of escalating attacks on him in the last half day, which seem to have started as capture attempts and escalated to assassination. The team gets some leads from debriefing him, and decides to split into 3 groups- PPJ will stay onsite and guard Arcane, hiring on Magnum and Goldsmoke again to act as additional security, while Arcane spends some time astrally tracking the most recent attacker- a powerful summoned spirit.

Otters and Echo will leave the Hub as they both need gear to operate fully, and investigate the first attack, by the Warrens gang the Zombies. Heat and Aradia will stay in the Hub and track down the bounty hunter who made the second attack, having relatively easily tracked down the guy in question based on his particular appearance. Before they all split off, the team suggests that it’s time Arcane shared a little more about how he escaped his various attackers, survived his wounds, and generally seems to have some unusual magical abilities, and he explains that although he was raised for most of his youth as British gentry as advertised, his mother is the Queen of Roses, a powerful Wild Fae, and that he has access to a limited selection of her fae abilities.

He also tells them a bit about his runner past with Cat and others, and confirms that he is the expert he otherwise appears to be, as well, including baffling Echo by claiming, seemingly accurately, that he speaks and reads all known languages. Suitably impressed, the party splits. Echo insists on trying diplomacy first, and they stop by the Paradise Lost mall in the Warrens, and get a custom chainsaw glaive made for the leader of the Zombies at Rattler’s shop, after Otters thinks about the local gang situation and consults with PPJ.

Echo also gets some useful gossip on the current state of affairs in the Warrens and with the gang while drinking in Frantic’s bar while waiting for the glaive to be finished. They drive it over to Zombie’s turf, and Otters and Echo approach on foot, successfully presenting it as a gift rather than a challenge, and throw in a little additional cash to get the beleaguered gangers talking, while Otters keeps the van HMG concealed but ready. The leader Ohnee tells them they got hired for a simple kidnap job on “some nerd” confirming the target as Arcane, but that he hit them with a massive stunball (explaining their banged up state) and then a huge spirit showed up and pushed their warbus out of the road as he drove off in his dinky little roadster.

With a little prompting, Ohnee gets one of the gang’s ghoul members, sporting some fresh cyber-eyes, to share footage of the meet with the Johnson, and running the face through Mona Lisa, they ID him as one Dexter Wright, a local ARES middle manager. Odd. As their best guess so far is Null Sect, they warn the gangers to stay off grid for a while, which they don’t seem to view as an issue since they’re fairly low tech. Ohnee happily swings the chain glaive wildly about overhead as the runners make a careful exit. Meanwhile, Heat and Aradia have headed underground and made their way to the infamous smuggler bar 5×5, where their target, Midnight, is waiting.

After he confirms that the barkeep and other patrons have seen him with the runners, he takes them out of the bar, not giving Heat time to buy a drink, and explains that you don’t do biz in there- house rules. He is happy to tell them that he took a capture and deliver contract, dumped a ton of stick’n’shock into Arcane, and the slippery little fucker still escaped. Shortly after, the pay got upgraded but it switched to wetwork, so he dropped the contract- he doesn’t kill, as a rule. The team tries to warn him of the Null Sect threat, but he doesn’t seem to know what they’re talking about and doesn’t seem concerned. For some extra pay, he offers to look into who took the assassination contract, as he shows them on an encrypted app that it is open but “in progress”.

Heat impresses everyone by using his much-maligned Horizon contacts to get the name of the hirer of the wetwork contract- something called the “Mountain Edge Society”. Aradia has a faint memory, and manages to recall that she had heard a tip that a lot of local Black Lodge activity is run through that same company, so it looks like this may not be the Null Sect after all. Midnight gets back to them, and reports that the hitman is one “Boom-Boom” a somewhat psycho explosives and missiles-lover. PPJ and Goldsmoke get a little worried about a faint puff of smoke they see a half-block distant, and as they focus in they see a bald human with a stick of dynamite in his teeth lighting and pinching out the fuse. He sees them at the same time, and launches a missile, but PPJ manages to lightning bolt it in midair, detonating it early, very impressive.

He and Goldsmoke hustle back inside as they lose sight of their target and the ZDF quickly responds. As the team regathers and shares all their respective intel, Arcane reports that he managed to track the summoned spirit to a strip mall and industrial area on the NW edge of the Hub, but can’t get a specific lock- someone did some work to obscure their trail, astrally. Doing some research, Otters finds that ARES owns a large magnet school in the area, so they decide to start their search there. After Otters fails to smuggle their weapons into the Hub, they decide to just do recon for now, and scout out the school. Heat spots a nested high security host tied to a building hidden away in a windowless courtyard on the campus, with a single private entry road.

Otters does some surveillance with Morticia, and spots a hatch on the roof that look promising, so Aradia and PPJ go invisible and levitate in that direction, seeing that there is both a high force magical barrier inside the building, and seemingly an even higher force ritual inside it in progress. Heat, also invisible, sneaks up the access road, and hacks into one of the cars in the private lot, giving Otters access. However, he bungles the hack a bit and has to go aggro, which alerts a guard inside, who exits, and blasts Heat with an SMG before Otters pins the guard to the door with the hacked car, and Echo rushes up and executes him with his own gun, before Otters rams the door open and Echo uses the guards keycard and biometrics to gain access.

Up top, PPJ uses his strange magic hands to reach inside the roof hatch and open it, and he and Aradia slip inside. Both halves of the team are confronted with an opaque magical barrier, and PPJ and Aradia slip through above using Aradia’s special training, while Echo and Heat open fire from below. Inside, they find 6 cloaked mages (obviously BL from the symbology), who seem to have safely paused their ritual after the security alerts the team set off entering. One of them suggests they would be wise to stop firing on the magical barrier, while another of the mages works to fortify it. PPJ manages to restrain himself, leaving it to Aradia to make the call, though the team below continues firing until they hear otherwise….

Revelations and Revenge
10/7/2024, 4:24:41 PM

Otters aka Roadkill

Aradia spends some more time researching local Black Lodge activity, checking in with Laffy, Tessa and Arcane on their own research. No major leads but definitely some concerns about strange magical happenings around town, and Aradia gets a few leads to follow up on. She also reviews the team file about what happened with the Sisterhood of Ariadne after she temporarily left the group in Seattle, and is disturbed to find that MCT may have wiped out the magical order to get the mana picture of Dis that Aradia had taken on the moon, and given to them to study. Although Aunt Clara’s mysterious Black Lodge handler “JB” remains unidentified, Aradia decides her beef is less with the BL and more with MCT/Dis, so changes her goals there. She also makes contact with some matrix sex workers, including the Catholic kink collective Nasty Habits, currently based in Hong Kong’s grid, and hosts a party/debrief for the first batch of Denver SW’ers who’ve returned from the inaugural work trip to the Seelie Court.

The rest of the group meets up and plans to get the details on Vishala’s latest hideout from Le Guide. However, the conversation quickly devolves into a debate about the nature of sentience, initiated by Echo, which ends up with StepMAMA signing the team norms and agreeing to take a cut of jobs she participates in. She also reveals that she is what she refers to as an “emerged intelligence” or “EI” (as opposed to the more traditional “AI”). She doesn’t seem to fully understand how this occurred, agreeing that she began as the expert Agent program MAMA, which was damaged in Detroit and repaired by Heat, though it seems like between something that Heat did then (but doesn’t understand) and with Test having done some astral language repair on her post-Seelie Court, she became fully sapient.

They ask if there are others like her, and she confirms that she is in contact with some, but that their secret is not hers to share, but that the team does not have enmity with them (so not Null Sect, who the team surmises would dislike her form of life anyway). Reviewing the team file, they find that she had previously used similar terms when describing the matrix sanctuary that she helped to transfer the e-ghost known as The Seer too, back when they were researching glyphs early on in Denver. So it seems like whoever this contact of hers is/are, they have been background allies for some time, so the team goes along with it. Heat insists on some parameters around how the pay is split, and they settle that with everyone agreeing to chip in so that Heat gets what would be a full share if StepMAMA weren’t counted, which seems to mollify him, though he is as usual a little weird around money stuff, or at least with how the rest of the group seems to feel about it. Maybe they’re the weird ones….

About to call Le Guide, they again pause to get the promised info they gathered on the ops team in the last mission to Rat Queen to pay off their favor owed. She confirms that this is a no morals/anything for pay crew who contracts out with the runner Clockwork sometimes when he needs a bigger physical presence beyond his drones, and that they have been known to snatch and sell TM’s. The team decides to try to burn Clockwork on Jackpoint by posting what evidence they have of his Null Sect ties, but PPJ cleverly decides to do some research first after Otters can’t remember her password, as she rarely uses the forum. PPJ sees that Clockwork is a member and frequent poster, disliked by many but very active.

He seems to hold grudges, seek revenge where possible, and hate TM’s/e-life forms. Not wanting him to know their street names/handles, they forgo posting on JP for now, and Heat shares the info with Perri at the Nexus instead, which she appreciates and says she’ll dig into further. The team finally contacts Le Guide to get things rolling, and gets the brief on her new hideout- a shipping container assemblage in an industrial park in Stapleton. She seems to have entered (looking injured) and not left in a few days, and the place is guarded by some gangers and a trio of cybered-up mercs. There doesn’t seem to be onsite magical defenses externally, but there are some angry spirits around.

With all that, the team decides to research it further themselves, and heads out. PPJ projects over, and confirms that there are more corrupted spirits, not as far gone as the ones at the ice rink, but along a similar path. They are circling the area but not approaching, and other spirits are diverting around them and the area. PPJ confirms no exterior magical defenses, but sees some small mana ebb areas, where perhaps some of Vishala’s weapons were detonated, perhaps explaining why the spirits aren’t approaching closer at the moment. Heat checks out the matrix and notices that once again, their exterior defenses and sensors are fried, and the patrolling gangers are operating with comms off, using handheld walkies to communicate when needed. There are signs that the Null Sect may have attacked the site in the matrix recently, though Heat can’t spot any around.

Otters does a surveillance pass with Morticia, and gets the guard’s patrol pattern down. The gangers seem to have fortified most of the entrances and only use a few to come and go, with the bodyguards popping out from time to time to check in on the exterior patrols, but staying inside themselves. Otters and PPJ ID the gangers as the Smooth Criminals, a human supremacist, mostly ex-military gang, that got kicked out of the Warrens during the Reclamation and is now based in the somewhat lawless Colorado Springs. It seems like the bulk of the gang is on-site, and has decent equipment and tactics, though hampered by reduced comms ability. Echo is able to ID the 3 mercs as siblings named Grim, Arbalest and Animal, ex-UCAS spec ops who went over to the merc/runner side of things post-Reclamation. They don’t have affiliation with the gang other than the military connection, and aren’t known to be human supremacists, though they are regarded as being another money over morals crew. They are much better equipped.

Doing some further digging via Mr. K and other contacts, Echo finds out that they recently ordered 6 doberman combat drones and got them delivered to their current location, but no sign of them outside. Otters also spots 3 Gomatia paracritters patrolling the 6 exterior halls of the structure, which the team knows Vishala has used before. They start making a plan, and head nearer to the site. PPJ makes Heat invisible, while keeping an eye on the corrupted spirits in astral. Heat sneaks up through the gap in the patrols that Otters spotted, and reaches in through one of the unmanned fire ports, slipping one of Otters’ stealthed snake drones inside. It slithers in, spotting a patrolling Gomatia, as well as more gangers managing hardened fire points inside. Well-set up defenses, but everyone is using walkies and speaking out loud, seemingly worried that their matrix shielding isn’t foolproof.

Otters overhears Vishala making a series of hurried and increasingly urgent calls, though she can only hear Vishala’s side, which seems to be mostly frustrated responses and growing anger and stress. Otters creeps the drone forward, and spots two brain-fried riggers, Hack and Iceman, who look to have fallen victim to lethal biofeedback (Null Sect?). She also spots 6 crates for the dobermans, one partially opened. She crawls the drone inside, also spotting the 3 cyber-merc’s and Vishala, who is wearing brand new combat armor with the face guard flipped up so she can smoke while she tries to fine a safe harbor and/or ride out of town, but it seems like she’s been burned by a lot of her contacts by now.

Otters slips the drone into the open crate, and uses the built-in data tap to get Heat access via the drone’s port. Heat hacks it and wakes it up, and PPJ also makes Echo invisible, who slips up alongside Heat and touches him to use his touchlink. Noticing that their matrix activity has attracted some possible NS attention, StepMAMA advises them to go to LOS burst comms only, so Otters drops drone control and sneaks the van a little closer. Echo and Heat use the doberman’s onboard comms to send a private message to it’s nominal owners, the cyber-mercs, offering more pay and a way out if they sell out Vishala. After a tense and terse negotiation, they agree to a (high) fee on the proviso that Vishala never has the chance to tell anyone, and that the team won’t either.

They agree, and Otters sends another snake in on autopilot, this one loaded with a Gamma Scopolamine dart. This sneaks up to Vishala and darts her in her face, the combat armor responding just too slow and closing up after she is injected and paralyzed, slicing her latest cig in half. The mercs load her up in one of the scattered crates they had set up for cover, and grab her three armored cases as well and start hauling them out. Echo and Heat slip around front and meet the van, which Otters changes the appearance of and pulls up to the door with comms off. Based on the blood up to the elbow and retracting cyber-spur on one of the mercs, it seems the gangers on door duty gave them some guff. They quickly load up the crates in the van, get their payment, and then all start threading suppressors onto their weapons as they say they’re going to stay here and clean up, seemingly doing some proactive reputation management. The team doesn’t expect they will hear of the Smooth Criminals again….

They open all the crates as they drive off, as PPJ notices some of the angry spirits starting to follow them. Remembering Creep’s tactics from the ice rink, he chucks one of the grenades he finds out the back, which luckily doesn’t have the detonator inserted yet, so seems low risk to any civilians who may encounter it, while still distracting the spirits and allowing the team to make their escape. They call Le Guide, who gives them directions to a nearby safehouse. When they arrive, they park in a rundown farmhouse, and head down into a bunker, concealed under a hatch in the thick roots of an old tree near the barn. They’ve seen that the crates, as well as the spirit grenades, also contain some one shot matrix chips of some sort, and a data drive. With the jammer hood on Vishala, they’re confident she can’t have called for help, so they muscle her down the stairs and fine LG finishing washing down what can only be described as a kill room- a fully tiled cube, with a single restraint chair over a floor drain, and a long hose attachment on one wall.

He asks if they want him to take lead, and they volunteer to do so, as well as to kill her after, which he seems perfectly happy with. Heat works on cracking the data drive, defusing data bombs and decrypting files as the team pulls off Vishala’s armor and restrains her in her stylish biz suit. Waiting for the paralytic to wear off before they start questioning her, PPJ cuts her a little with his knife, but everyone else isn’t on board for torture so he quickly stops. He does use an illusion spell to make both Echo and Heat look and sound exactly like her for the interrogation though, which should get weird. Heat finishes getting all the data with some lucky hacking, and is pretty horrified at what he uncovers.

It seems the grenades use fragments of a tortured spirit to power them., and that though this initial batch it toxic, and thus opens micro-tears to the toxic planes, Vishala believes other effects could be achieved through other means of spirit torture. She goes into great detail about how to summon, force manifestation, and then fetter the spirit to allow for this lengthy and awful process to occur, and has plans included for how to further mechanize the whole thing. The team starts to think maybe a little torture is ok after all, in this case. The chips are about as bad- inspired by the SpriteLOKs from Detroit, they contain tormented Sprite fragments, and can destroy other Sprites very effectively, or create Dissonant pool openings if used in Foundation hosts. Both very dangerous, and the team is a little worried that Le Guide took both cases with him when he left the bunker to leave them to it.

The research and note data is also bad- it looks like a lot of stolen research data from AZT and ARES dealing with portals, magitech, etc, as well as the Detroit Scar, the Yellowstone Anomaly, and the Antarctica Anomaly, amongst other things. She seems to have been selling her discoveries to MCT, while also receiving funding from the mysterious group “The Asintmah Alliance”, whose ranks she seems to have wished to join, viewing this research as her path in. However, she seems frustrated at their slowness to accept her and grant her powers, raving about how she hasn’t even received one crystal yet, unlike some base runners (PPJ??). She also apparently got tricked by Le Guide into buying the Null Sect “Clear” that the team had captured, and found that it was an even more efficient way to make the chips than regular Sprites, and set about trying to capture more- this seems to be why her matrix defenses were under such heavy attack. So LG has been working to bring her down as well as hiring the team to assist.

With some key intel, they start questioning her and make a deal- they will kill her quick if she gives them some intel, as she seems to delight in telling them they don’t even know who they are working for, but that she doesn’t want to be used in one of his twisted rituals. She reveals what she thinks is a big conspiracy to them- Dis, MCT and the Null Sect are working together! Except they already know. She does seem disturbed by the two perfect copies of her interrogating her, and quickly spills more. That trio was working with AZT, but has lately been starting to cut them out, and everyone is starting to regret involving the NS, as they are too alien to understand their goals or sufficiently control them.

She also gives some further background, suggesting that Disians may have been in touch with some on Earth (knowingly or not) for longer than the team thought, as they seem to have originated the Blood Crystal tech that the Black Lodge has been distro-ing. PPJ is a little concerned, as are the team. When pushed further, she also confirms that she sought to join the AA, though she knows little of them, because she thought their crystals and other higher secrets would finally grant her some level of magical ability, as well as power in general. She doesn’t know who the members are, as they sought her out, but says she believes the leadership is known as the “Sinister 7”. They apparently use old-school, over-manufactured tech to make themselves hard to hack or magic, and seem to be continuing some of the agenda of the Freedom Network. She says that she believes the rewards include being made a “Faustean”, and seems disgusted that she thinks PPJ has been made one and doesn’t deserve it.

PPJ reaffirms to the team that this all new to him, but they are more worried now. Vishala also enjoys mockingly telling them that LG has tricked them just as she did- he is actually an AZT agent known as “MASQUE”, ans she suggests they look into what he got up to during the AZT/AM war not too long ago. The team is a little worried, so Otters checks that the hatch is still unlocked (yes) while the others ask if she has any final words. As she starts down a path of self-justification about her rags to riches story, PPJ stabs her in the brain, quickly killing her. They exit, and LG waiting. He smoothly takes the lead, and says that they heard some things in there that don’t sound great (it seems he was recording) but that he hopes his dealings with them are sufficient to prove his rep and well-meaning. He does seem to be telling the truth, but they don’t fully agree to continue working with him.

They negotiate pay, coming out a little behind what they paid the mercs in total, but LG promises them extra pay on the next job, once he has a chance to dig into all the data Heat recovered, as well as the intel they got direct from her. Feeling a little unnerved by everything, they decide to return to the safehouse and rest up for now. They awaken in the morning to a frantic call from Arcane- someone is trying to kill him! And he wants to know who and why, ASAP. They agree to look into it, and head out.

Foundation Rescue
9/30/2024, 4:13:01 PM
Runners
Heat
Echo

While the rest of the team starts gearing up to take on Vishala’s stronghold before she can leave town, and Aradia does some research into matrix-based sex work and also local Black Lodge activities, Heat gets an emergency call from Louis Belaran of the ASPS. He tells Heat that he needs a hacker to go somewhere he can’t, and offers a sizeable payday for it. When Heat expresses his interest, Belaran fills him in: the French troll decker SIM-Eon has gotten link-locked, with his consciousness trapped somewhere deep in the matrix. Belaran thinks he must be in a Foundation or similar since he occasionally takes damage but seems unable to communicate, and everyone he has consulted says it is unsafe to merely disconnect him.

As SIM-Eon was investigating alone and ran into trouble, Belaran advises bringing a friend in, and Heat recruits Echo to watch his back, and also test out how the new runner and teammate does in the matrix. Belaran tips them to the last 3 places that SIM-Eon sent expense account receipts from as places to start their investigation- it seems Belaran was acting as the fixer for this gig, with SIM-Eon seeking some kind of valuable paydata for an unnamed Johnson. The two runners decide to post up at their safehouse and begin investigating in the matrix.

First they check out the VR version of Dante’s Inferno, the famous Seattle multi-level nightclub. They mingle a bit and spot a few avatars that look likely to be info brokers. They also see that some life-streamers are broadcasting VR feeds of the IRL version of the club to the matrix version (and vice versa), so it seems like a pretty immersive experience, and Heat already wants to come back when he has more time. They choose an info broker, and approach a blue-skinned 4 armed woman, Rei Bluestone, who seems to also be a cook or gourmand of some sort, at least according to her avatar.

After confirming that she does actually run a food cart and catering biz, they contribute to a “custom item” fund which she explains she uses to subsidize her efforts to feed the unhoused population Downtown, and gives them the info they are seeking. They’ll need to get further club access to go a few levels down to the high energy dance floor, and seek a hard partier named Sirena, who Bluestone has recently seen speaking to SIM-Eon’s avatar. Heat calls up Johnny Silver and gets a passcode to give to the VR bouncers, which works, though Heat doesn’t quite manage to connect over a shared love of the outlaw tunes.

They get onto the dance floor and circulate, with Echo keeping an eye out for an surveillance while Heat approaches Sirena. Buying her some drugs and other party favors via her publicly shared bar tab code, he gets her to take a break from frenetically dancing and manages to just hold her attention long enough to get the intel. She was an exec assistance at NeoNET before their collapse, who seems to have gotten a juicy payoff from her boss as the company was collapsing, and says that SIM-Eon wanted to know about a specific Foundation Host that her boss was in charge of shutting down. As far as she knows, everything got deleted, but she was out of there, not wanting to get caught up in the collapse.

Doing some quick research, they find that her boss died shortly after her final encounter with him a few years back, while link-locked in the matrix- something is up with that Host! Also they find some rumors that various NeoNET hosts that weren’t absorbed by other corps in the feeding frenzy during and after their collapse have either drifted off into the Wild Matrix or become home to Null Sect intrusions, some info about which is in the team file. They decide to check Hong Kong next, and use the password provided by Belaran to gain access to a private VR hacker bar Host, sculpted like a pirate ship.

They spot some interesting characters around once again, though this crowd seems to have more scrap and home-built stuff than the high end tech/TM abilities that Echo and Heat are using. They approach one of the fancier icons who again seems to be some sort of fixer or info broker, based on the frequent stream of messengers approaching her. She introduces herself as The Rat Queen, explaining her avatar of a roiling mass of rats inside a trench coat and hat. They make a deal with her to get info in exchange for seeking some revenge for her, as needed.

She says the SIM-Eon came through recently, looking to buy some intel that an up and coming decker named Blip had posted for sale, but that noone has seen Blip since and it having been a few days people are starting to worry. She tells them she sent SIM-Eon to a brothel where Blip did maintenance work and upkeep, as it seems like it was an all-VR operation, called “Nasty Habits”. Echo is delighted to arrive at that private Host, via another passcode, and find a delightfully sinful Catholic-kink themed joint, with some good looking house rules. They forward the info to Aradia for her investigation into VR sex work. Making contact with one of the priest avatars, they don’t take advantage of the confessional glory holes, but Heat does make note to return later.

The priest fills them in on Blip’s situation once they make clear they are there to help, but also warns the team that another team recently came through looking for Blip, shortly after SIM-Eon’s visit. He tips them off to Blip’s last known safehouse location, and tells them to let the Sisterhood (seemingly the org behind the brothel) what happened, as they all view him as somewhat of a little brother. Contracting a small local team through TRQ, the team arranges for them to go seek out Blip’s last known safehouse, in a houseboat shanytown, and report in on any leads. With that in motion, they head to the third location Belaran provided, the VR Host of the cathedral at Notre Dame. They arrive and find it hyper-realistic and very publicly open.

They spot various generic pilgrim and clergy avatars milling around, many of which, many of which don’t look especially familiar with VR matrix navigation. Heat also spots some security IC in the form of Hunchback of Notre Dame characters flitting about in the shadows and rafters. He calls Perri and asks if there’s anything weird known about the Host, wondering why SIM-Eon came here, but she says the only thing she knows is that many Catholics on their deathbeds or in hospice spend a lot of time in VR visiting the very high quality replicas that the Church has made of many of their holy sites, so they could look for someone who’s been logged in a while and might have seen something. She also warns them to be careful of the Catholics though, as they have their own agendas.

Heat does a quick matrix search, and finds someone who has been logged on since SIM-Eon was here, and they chat up a dying French man after Echo spends a few hours learning French with their adept abilities. He confirms that he saw their friend, seemingly being warned off by some other runners, who match the description of the other team from Nasty Habits. He says that SIM-Eon rapidly left without seeming to give them what they want, and they seemed angry but seemed to be trying to track him. With this, they get a little concerned and call Belaran to confirm that SIM-Eon’s bod is in a safe place, and Belaran confirms it is, saying that SIM-Eon did too good a job covering his tracks, this being part of why he needed them to backtrace his investigation to find out where he is trapped.

While heading back to their own bods, they get a message from TRQ, reporting bad news- Blip’s half-eaten corpse was found in his trashed safehouse- seems like local ghouls and/or vermin took the opportunity to chow down after someone shot him in the head a few days back, and locals report seeing the opps team in the area, up to no good. After Heat is unable to dig up anything about this team in the Denver matrix, they report what they know about them to TRQ, who confirms they are a low-morals, high-violence crew that sometimes bases out of HK. She also passes along what data the TM’s at the pirate ship VR bar were able to salvage from Blip’s trashed deck.

They find a message from Blip to SIM-Eon about selling the location of something valuable in the Wild Matrix, and finally also get the location of the lost NeoNET host. Feeling like the opps team may also be coming after them at some point as they keep digging, they decide to recruit some backup. Echo calls in a favor with his fixer Eddie Mustang Voss, and gets a secure hacking safehouse in the Hub, with some decent onsite protection from the attached bar, as well as the general district, but not a lot of good escape routes. They get Otters and PPJ to watch their backs from the bar, as well as hiring two runners, a Dwarf named Goldsmoke who seems like cocky muscle, and a nervous but focussed hacker named Magnum, through Stiletto, to help support Otters and PPJ.

Heading up the safehouse, they get set up, with Echo deciding to use one of Heat’s strange Silver Book abilities to be able to access Foundation levels as needed. They do a decent job navigating the strange broken physics of the Wild Matrix, way off the edge of the map of the pubic/known matrix, with only the massive hovering Host icons of the megacorps visible from their distance. They find a collapsed NeoNET Host as promised, and easily find the Foundation access, it being the only somewhat vibrant remaining part. They dive in, and find themselves atop one of a number of fragmented floating islands, covered in odd religious statuary, altars, buildings, etc, all of which are collapsed, actively collapsing or in disarray. They seem to be at an anchored portal, themed like a sacrificial altar, and see that the rest of the Host seems populated by iconography from a wide variety of religions and pantheons.

They decide to check some of the smaller islands first before heading to the largest remaining one, which is the only one that looks to have any kind of active movement. Echo almost mis-times their jump, not expecting a strange zero-g void between the islands, and neither of them are sure what would happen if they fell in, though they see bits crumbling off the edges of the islands and de-rezzing as they tumble into the void, so they imagine nothing good. Echo launchers their Agent, Paris, to assist. Finding that all the smaller island nodes’ functions seem broken, they head for the largest one, which upsettingly looks to be the Null node. Echo also recognizes the strange gold fog over everything as a blown apart and fragmented NeoNET logo in their trademark color.

Reaching the largest island, they find strange mythical beasts menacingly circling an overgrown temple, with a sacrificial altar out front, as well as a cage containing a very injured SIM-Eon. Seeing rusty stains on the altar, they think it has been used before, perhaps explaining the NN exec’s link-locked death when he tried to shut down this Host. They resculpt their avatars to try to blend in, and then both try to behave like the local monsters, with pretty good success. Echo starts circling with the other avatars and manages to fake clumsily knock one of them into the void, to see what would happen, and is once again to see it seemingly suffering as it de-rezzes.

Heat approaches the altar, and manages to knock it over, spilling forth what looks like the paydata they seek but also coming under attack from a hidden avatar that looks like some kind of odd steampunk golem. Heat is badly hurt, and Echo sends Paris to assist. The other monster avatars also start closing in on the fight around the altar, distracting them enough for Echo to grab the file and flee as Heat retreats from combat, breaking open SIM-Eon’s cage and freeing him and helping him jump to an adjacent island. The steampunk golem reaches into the open Null node under the altar and pulls forth a large vine, which starts whipping about it and knocking monstrous avatars into the void, as well as firing at the retreating team again, injuring Echo as well and nearly KO-ing Heat, though the runners do make good their escape amidst the chaos.

Their only hint as to who that other avatar may have been is that they really seem to hate Heat as a TM, so likely either Null or Null ally. They quickly retreat, helping SIM-Eon along with Paris also assisting, and Echo does an efficient job navigating them out of the Wild Matrix back to the public one, where SIM-Eon gratefully takes his leave back to his bod, and the team also drops out. They get a report that the bar came under attack from the opps team, but between bar and Hub security they were a bit under-equipped and the team there managed to drive them off with no casualties on either side, though also choosing not to pursue.

The team pays off their protectors as promised and passes the paydata along to Belaran, who thanks them for completing both halves of the mission and pays up as promised. With a solid payday in their pockets, they head back to the team safehouse to rest up and get ready to hit Vishala asap. PPJ, during some downtime, also take a meeting with Belaran, and briefs him on the situation with The Veil/Hearts of Zebulon/AZT’s astral experiments on the Shattered Lands hyperplane. Belaran agrees to get the ASPS involved with those groups and look for opportunities to interfere and help stabilize the Mile High Realm further. He also explains that the ASPS is looking to establish a spirit sanctuary somewhere, and seems to think this could be a good opportunity, potentially. He agrees to tell PPJ if they plan a kinetic response at any point, but talks him into holding off for now, saying they need to do some recon and intel gathering.

Deepfakes and Paydays
9/16/2024, 12:15:42 PM
Runners
Otters aka Roadkill
Pepper Jack
Heat
Aradia
and the introduction of: Echo

The team, returned from the Mile High Realm and worried for their safehouse, all rest up in their various secondary locations. Heat has a restless night, still feeling complicated but unarticulated feelings about the different departures of Creep and The Detective. PepperJack has the strangest dreams by far though- he feels the calling of a pack of wolves from outside the light of his mental “campfire”, and feeling they are offering rather than threatening, he approaches, intrigued. It seems the howling of the pack is drowning out some other lone, distant howls, which he ignores for now. As he moves amongst the wolves, many lick his injured hand and he finds that it has healed from the Void Rock damage, at least in the dream.

Wanting to run and hunt with the wolf pack, he slips his human skin, noticing the careful seams that remind him of the costumed Lone Wolf he saw in the MHR, and freeing the wolf inside. He runs with the pack, cavorting and enjoying himself, and on awakening, accepts Wolf’s offer of kinship and a path to power through cooperation, and goes through a paradigm shift from Lone Wolf to Wolf as a Mentor. Feeling newly aligned with “pack” while still able/required to assert his dominance against foes, he prepares to face the day, excited to notice that his hand has healed IRL as well.

The team meets up and decides they need to make some calls and get warning out about the Void Rock and what they saw of the AZT/MCT portal experiments on the metaplanes. They tip off Fargus Sol, who says he will spread the word amongst Grey Cell and their allies. He tells the team that from what he knows, this substance can only be mined on “dead planes” who have lost their magic, whether through magic cycles like the Material Plane, Scourges by the Horrors, or other means. However, this should prevent travel to and from that land, as they ought to be disconnected from the astral sea. The team recollects seeing some dead and empty bug tunnels through the rift in the sky over Detroit, and Otters re-shares the drone footage. They speculate a bit about if this could be one of the places Void Rock could be mined.

PPJ is also able to retrieve some more memories from Dis, hearing the distant howl of a wolf pack in his mind as he does so, and recalling that there were empty bug tunnels under the city, partially built into but largely empty, that he fled through to reach Kadath when he left. He also recalls that the pieces he was attaching to metahumans when working in the surgical suite mostly seemed to come from paracritters, though some may have been from other metaplanar beings he vaguely remembers that don’t feel familiar otherwise. Also, many of the metahumans he helped operate on had various tattoos and insignia suggesting they were members of the missing III Corps. He shares all this with the group, and they all spend some time discussing what this might mean.

They also discuss what implications this has for the Dis Trader that Aradia and others met on the moon, when they found the wreckage of the trapped and murdered first expedition to the Seelie Court. Thinking back, PPJ confirms that whatever mysterious figure helped him flee, it seemed to only become possible after the team used the artifact on the moon to allow the Trader to return home through a magical portal/door that revealed a sprawling, chaotic city before closing behind him. Between this and the strange Planar Convocation that occurred during the Blackouts, Sol speculates that Dis may only be recently connected and accessible, but what that means for their level of connection he does not know- it seems like some of them must have been able to access other planes much earlier at least.

Sol seems concerned, and says he may need to put some things in motion a little sooner than he hoped. He tells the team to get in contact with a local extraction expert, and test them out to see if they’re a good fit. If so, Sol will be in touch soon with details about an extraction the team will need to pull in Seattle. He explains that there are two factions within AZT, and that a whistleblower may be able to give some much needed leverage to Grey Cell to influence the internal operations of the corp. The team remembers that they passed a coded message from someone to Ringmaster after receiving it on Zecorporatum, and Sol confirms this was the extraction target making initial contact.

He says they’ve done everything they can to confirm that the target is real and sincere, but warns the team it could still be a trap, especially if the corp finds out about the target’s plans. For now, he says to test out the extraction expert and get gear ready. He sends some payment for the intel, and also tells them how to use the set of 7’s that Creep seems to have someone gifted to the team. They talk about the strange phenomena of ShadowCreep, and Sol shares some suppositions about what could have happened there, as the team recalls other past members and contacts that have appeared as limited astral manifestations as well. They head to Aradia’s Hub safehouse to do the Tarot ritual in her magical lodge, and also set a meeting with Echo, the extraction expert, for that evening, at Club Olympus. They debate trying out the same lateral thinking test that Silk used on them but decide against it.

The ritual is a success, with each of them holding one of the 7’s., chosen somewhat randomly, and feeling new powers suffuse them as the cards dissolve into their bodies. Otters feels a little luckier, Aradia feels a little sneakier, Heat gets 100 shares of some company called “Sky Brides”, and PPJ is shocked as 2 new arms burst forth from his torso. Interestingly, he now has 3 magic hands, though his previously injured hand still can not pass through things- when he thinks on it, he feels a sense of disappointment from his “passenger” about sharing so much intel with Fargus Sol, and abandoning Lone Wolf, but no further punishment is forthcoming beyond that so- net gain?

The team heads to the meet, with PPJ stopping to buy some nice new streetwear, as well as getting his armor tailored for his additional arms. They arrive at the club and meet up with Echo with no trouble, and have a momentarily confusing conversation before realizing that as well as testing Echo out and recruiting them for an extraction job, Echo is also feeling them out and aiming to recruit them for another extraction job that they need a crew for. They all agree to do some work together in town and then if all goes well, work together on the two travel jobs- both “willing” extractions, so hopefully low drama.

Heat also does some research on Echo, finding that they are ex-UCAS military intelligence, a veteran of Colloton’s campaigns in Seattle (freeing the Arcology from DEUS) and DeeCee (putting down the New Revolution coup attempt), and then transitioning into serving in her administration in a similar role. Echo then apparently blew some kind of attempt to leverage the Black Lodge to keep St Louis in the UCAS and got tangled up in trying to protect the city during the Blackouts. This wrecked their rep and they appear to have taken the fall for the busted op, with the government severing ties. Echo confirms this story when questioned, though seems a bit reluctant to discuss what is obviously a bit of a raw area. They do confirm that they are not BL-affiliated, and were merely aiming to use them in an “enemy of my enemy” way, against the Sea Dragon and Ghostwalker’s “Free City” advocacy.

Heat checks in with Hype about Horizon’s official policy on Free Cities, and finding that Horizon doesn’t care so long as they’re free to operate, and that they like Denver especially since AZT is banned, giving them more market share. Heat then doesn’t agree with Echo’s stance that Denver should be brought back into the UCAS fold, but does so politely. It seems Echo remains a UCAS patriot and is looking for something in Denver that could bring them back into Colloton’s good graces, even though she is out of power now, having lost the presidency to a far right candidate running on a “fear” platform following the Blackouts, ARES’ move, the bugs in Detroit, III Corps disappearing etc. Echo still strongly believes that Colloton is the hero this nation needs, and gives the team some context and history- her resistance to the corps and defending of lower-case R republican values/democracy/national supremacy over the corps.

The team is interested in this viewpoint, not having encountered a lot of people with these philosophies of late, but can’t dive in deeper, as they get a frantic message from a wounded and battle-damaged Stiletto, telling them she is under attack and will pay well for a rescue. They offer to cut Echo in, and they negotiate and counter on pay, with Echo ultimately impressed with their skills after getting tag-teamed by Heat and Aradia, and agreeing to the same cut as the rest of the crew. They race for Stapleton, grabbing PPJ’s ARMor (ha) from the tailor on the way. Doing some quick research, they figure out that the GPS tag Stiletto sent is ruined site of the big battle between the ZDF and the defenders of the Nexus a few years ago, which PPJ remembers well, having been one of the defenders.

He briefs the team on what he remembers of the site, including the escape tunnels underneath and the one exit point from them that he knows of. However, based on matrix chatter, it seems that the blasted ruins have been left to grow over by the ZDF, after initial excavations didn’t find any survivors or intact servers, and uncovering the sealed tunnels showed them how the Nexus made their escape. Echo is intrigued to hear that the Nexus is still operational, as the official story is that they were destroyed in this battle.
