This checks with the team’s experience of them in Denver- hiding out while focused on the matrix side of things. They reach the site, with Morticia scouting overheard.

Although the official Nexus memorial is at the other abandoned site in Co Springs, it looks some informal memorials have been tagged here, showing a lighting-wreathed Nocturna, and bearing lyrics from Johnny Silver’s rebel anthem “Guns of Chrome”. They start doing some investigation, and quickly spot some groundwolf nests in the vicinity. Using Otter’s knowledge, they recognize that there are unarmored ambush predators, who attack from their hidden burrows. As they fear fire, PPJ uses some well-placed fire grenades to keep them well underground while the team searches the area. They find a damaged old truck partially hidden in semi-collapsed ambulance bay.

Otters and Heat stay in the van, with Heat detecting some failing signals inside the hospital building. The team follows some tracks and signs of combat in, noticing that there was some big magic used, with PPJ and Aradia confirming that some of the magical signatures look like Stiletto at least. They continue, and find 4 broken corpses- Heat spotted their fading piezoelectric signals. It looks like they were all killed with magic and/or knife wounds, and PPJ uses Death Replay on one of them to confirm. Echo is a little surprised by the use of Blood magic, but PPJ asserts it is merely Death magic and Echo goes with it. Otters has the Mule drone along, which starts looking for more tracks with Echo, spotting that someone made false tracks down one tunnel, and then backtracked and obscured their footprints down another path.

PPJ reports that it looks like an injured Stiletto did indeed win the fight, but that she was wearing different armor and looked a bit different that in the call they got. Worried about a trap, they call Mr. K- a Johnson and fixer both the team and Echo have worked with, and ask if he’s heard from Stiletto recently. He gets her on the call using a different number than she had called the team from (which Heat hasn’t been able to reach since), and she seems very surprised that they are there, and that they are saying she requested their presence. She does come out from her hiding place further in the complex, warily, with her assensing the team and them assensing here to confirm they all are who they appear to be.

With that out of the way, they assume it’s a trap of some sort, and Otters and Heat take a panicked look around outside, barely managing to get the van out of the AOE of the initial grenade, magic and sniper attack. Heat conjures up a Machine Sprite to help Otters defend the van, which she does a miraculous job of for quite some time, though still taking some hits, with it eventually getting knocked out by machine pistol fire from two street sams after they are forced to abandon their grenadier position due to Heat using some well-placed suppressive fire. Otters manages to take out one of the same with the van HMG as it dies and grinds to a halt.

Inside, the team plus Stiletto end up in a close quarters fight with two adepts, who some recognize as two of The Auditor’s bodyguards from Denver/LA. With Echo providing some critical shotgun assistance, they manage to take down the two adepts with no casualties, with big aid from Aradia cleverly using illusions of Stiletto trying to escape to draw them out of cover. She also heals Stiletto to get her back in the fight, and boosts both her and Echo’s speed. Otters brings the Mule back to the entrance as the rest of the team also approaches in cover. After some more clever illusions and volleys of fire back and forth, the team manages to take down the other sam and the mage, though the sniper may have escaped- they aren’t sure.

With the gunfire momentarily halted, they hustle Stiletto into the Valkyrie module while Heat and Otters desperately try to rip parts off of her shot-up truck to get the van running again, with the help of another Machine sprite, and some leadership guidance from Echo and Aradia. PPJ thinks about the vehicle magic that he saw and learned on the MHR, and thinks he might be able to do something- he tries out a new spell, and manages to get the van sputtering back to life, with the rest of the group doing some hurried fixes to get it moving. With it still badly damaged, PPJ adds some spell protection as well, and Otters gets Morticia up in the air again to act as long-range scanner.

Echo confirms that the likely plans was to flush the team into the complex with this ambush attack, driving them into the tunnels, and then have kill zones set up at the exits. Using PPJ’s memory, Otters checks the likeliest locations for these kill teams, and spots 3 off road vehicles speeding towards them while one remains guarding the tunnel exit area. Heat launches some grenades into the hospital to obfuscate their escape path, sending the unstable structure further into full collapse. Otters races off, using Mortitica’s eye in the sky to take a stealthy path north away from the complex, headed up to Brighton where Stiletto says she knows a street doc and a safe place they can hide out and debrief.

With some impressive driving, Otters manages to slip the net the enemy vehicles are trying to make around the zone, and they race north to Horse Trot Ranch, where they head into one of the secure cabins, with covered parking, and Aradia having changed the van’s appearance with another spell. They head inside to rest as the veterinarian Raw Dog shows up to tend to their wounds. He is delighted to see some of the team again after they warned him he was in danger during the money cow job, and gets to work. Stiletto also pays some staff mechanics to start patching the van up, after ensuring Otters of their discretion. They debrief, and realize they need to make some calls, warning other contacts of potential traps.

They start with The Collector and Silk, who thank them for the warning and confirm they have already picked up on some “intrusion attempts”. Agreeing with the team’s suggestion that they need a better way to avoid more deepfake tricks, TC proposes using a one-time pad code encryption system, after confirming some of the team is familiar from Detroit or other past activities. He says he will set it up and start distributing the pads throughout their growing network of allies. He says once this is done, it may be time to organize a meeting between all allies to plan their response, but that this will take some time to set up. Heat next calls Perri, who is less worried about fakes and encryption with all the Nexus’ Technomancers, and as Creep didn’t have much intel on them anyway.

Heat also reports in all their findings on GW from the MHR, closing out that job, and much to his own dismay convinces himself and the team to trade for a favor instead of money, with Perri agreeing to use the funds she had set aside to pay TM’s at the Nexus to help TC with his encryption and secure comms effort. Lastly, they call Le Guide, who is saddened to hear of Creep’s death (though quickly moves on), and after receiving their bad news, says he has some good news to cheer them up: he finally has managed to track down Vishala! He says she’s hiding out for the moment, but he assumes she is probably aiming to leave town, so they shouldn’t linger too long.

They explain they need to rest and repair and get ready, so he says he’ keep an eye on her for now, and they agree to be in touch soon, planning to negotiate pay on the tail end based on what they are able to recover from her defended base. Le Guide requests her alive if possible, for interrogation, as well as capturing whatever data and tech they can and destroying anything left behind. They agree, but for now, rest up. In the morning, all feeling much better from Raw Dog’s ministrations, they negotiate with Stiletto for pay (less than offered in the deepfake but some, plus some favors) and then set a meeting with Alexis Glimmerscale to sell her all the GW info from the MHR, Seelie Court, and Endless Archive. Stiletto also tells them she will likely have more work for them soon, as her investigation is obviously getting somewhere since they tried to kill her.

They head over to their church HQ in Stapleton, and meet up. After a lengthy and intense debrief, Glimmerscale seems thrilled with the amount of detail they’ve uncovered, and pays extra for intel on the corps who are actually causing most of the rifts, which the team provides. She doesn’t currently seem interested in hiring them to try to breach the hidden 5th area of the MHR, though she confirms PPJ"s assumption that it is likely the mountain’s interior. Heat negotiates a big payday for all they found, with Echo’s assistance, and they split it up appropriately, before Glimmerscale asks if they found anything about Rex Coll. Thinking back to their chat with Nadja Daviar at the Seelie Court, they realize they actually might have.

Daviar had broached the question of “what happened to all the live bounties brought into the Draco Foundation over the years?” and the team knows that Coll has long overseen that department before ascending to his current leadership role. With PPJ’s recovered memory of implanting paracritter and other parts onto metahumans on Dis, they wonder if Coll may perhaps be in league with the Disians, or some faction of them, and share this with Glimmerscale, who seems very worried, and says she needs to talk to some people about making some moves and plans, and confirms she will be keeping some of the information they sold her to herself for now while she does so. With another promise of future work once things are in place, they head out to gear up for going after Vishala.

Void Rocking Out
9/9/2024, 10:51:18 AM
Runners
Otters aka Roadkill
Pepper Jack
Heat
Aradia
the Retirement from the Shadows of: The Detective
One Final Ghostly Appearance from:: ShadowCreep

While crossing Iron Horse Nation, PPJ has had a growing awareness of some conflict in his mind. He focuses, and can sense that there are two Wolves fighting- seemingly more traditional Wolf, the mentor, as well as his variant, Lone Wolf. Strangely, Lone Wolf seems to be someone inside a very realistic wolf costume, made of the wolf’s skin and carefully sewn. However, PPJ spots a few seams and a few times where the figure doesn’t quite move correctly. He can hear the snapping of other Lone Wolves supporting one side, and the howling of the packs supporting the other, but not sure what it means decides not to participate. He tells the team, and they wonder if this has something to do with what happened to Lone Wolf while they were at the Seelie Court, when PPJ provided his strange mental presence access in exchange for the return of his powers. Knowing that Mentor Spirits can both be trapped and corrupted, Otters is a little concerned, but PPJ brushes it off for now.

Carefully crossing the twisted bridge, covered in barbed wire and tank traps, the team makes their way into the City of Steel. They encounter a border checkpoint, more heavily fortified than what PPJ recalls from his prior visit some years ago, and see both a large collection of mirror-masked and trench-coated guards, and many large screens displaying a closeup of a bloodshot, unblinking eye, staring at them as they approach. After some back and forth with the guards, Heat agrees to check his weapon, surrendering it into their secure lockup in return for a chit, while Aradia is allowed to keep her knife focus, as it has a legit magical purpose and isn’t a large blade.

They are all split into separate lines, and ushered forward for what seems largely like a customs interview. Luckily, they just have time to concoct a shared, largely true story, and all manage to talk their way through without any trouble, with assistance from having a package to deliver to Mystics and Magicks. Heat almost gets in trouble by referencing working for the “Big Man”, forgetting for a moment that Ghostwalker would surely never work with an AZT subsib, but manages to course correct and bribes the guards with some reagents to get a little further access to the city. They get approved to travel to 3 of the 7 districts, and head into the first.

Otters just barely spots a strange, small shadow that sneaks through the large border gates into the checkpoint after them. She sees it follow Heat, carefully staying right behind him and out of sight of the border agents, following him through the line. Otters tries to share some pictures with them team, but can’t get a clear shot without being too obvious. However, the shadow looks distinctly like Creep’s silhouette. Strange! They team chats about it after clearing the checkpoint, and a thin line of shadow turns sideways and reveals itself to be shaped like Creep. It gives many of the team a hug, but seems to only communicate gesturally.

Finding themselves in a chaotic street market amidst a shantytown, with buildings of all architectural periods towering around them, they approach a cabby for some local intel. He accepts some payment from Otters, and explains that they are in Mish-Mosh, the chaotic district that those not approved for other districts all end up in. It seems like a kind of a mashup of all the leftover places of Denver- the Warrens, the Barrens, various other neighborhoods caught between things. Not seeing anything they need at the market, they get a ride to the Sun Sector, where the cabby tells them M&M is located. Hilariously, this is only a brief walk across the market square. Also, the cabs seem to be free, but willing to take advantage of out of towners, so they plan not to pay in future.

The team heads into Sun Sector, with those that didn’t fit in the first cab walking over to join and quickly catching up. PPJ wants to hang back, not feeling good about further assisting AZT, especially since they didn’t yet help the Hearts of Zebulon, but ShadowCreep pushes him in, so he staggers after the group. They find M&M easily amidst the dingy, overcast district. Despite the general downer atmosphere, the people of the district have done a lot to make the place nicer- warming paint tones and the like. The team can see a large step pyramid at the end of the district where it abuts the mountain ridge that wraps the city. They can also see a lot of Sun/Path of the Sun iconography, so this definitely seems like another AZT affiliated area.

However, since they know GW wouldn’t tolerate their presence in astral Denver if he had a choice, this either means he is so distracted that he doesn’t know, or he doesn’t have a choice, implying that though The Authority that oversees the city may be a manifestation of his authoritarian tendencies, it isn’t actually GW himself. PPJ remembers that the Sun Sector was present the last time he was here, though it was gated off and not accessible- it seems like GW’s “Great Reclamation” has opened the borders in the City of Steel as well, even to districts he would prefer not to include. The wristbands they were all given on entry seem the only restriction to travel, with The Authority’s guards casually scanning their color bands at entrances to different districts.

Entering M&M, the team find some friendly shop keeps, who seem to be material plane metahumans residing here. Heat and Aradia hand over the package, after PPJ quickly assenses it and recognizes trace blood magic. He’s still not happy about handing this over to AZT but, out-voted, goes along with the team decision for now. Heat, taking a guess, asks the clerk why they’re receiving Blood Crystals, and the clerk seems surprised, though definitely does know what Heat is talking about. He hurries into the back to unpack it and get it into the safe, but Heat gets the impression that though the clerks seem somewhat in the know about AZT’s activities in the Shattered Lands, this was likely a package of reagents, foci, or similar and not actually Blood Crystals.

However, this is good intel to be careful what specific info they share here. Heat negotiates for pay for the courier journey, and the other clerk recognizes Aradia, having been told to expect her, though they refuse to engage in the sexual banter requested by Simonetta Chandra, despite Aradia trying to get it going- she quickly drops it when the clerk seems uncomfortable. Heat gets them a spot to stay for the night, and an opportunity to do some work in exchange for AZT forging more access bands for them to get them into 6 of the 7 districts. PPJ really wants to get into the 7th district, but the clerks explain that (wink, wink) even if they COULD do that, it would require a major, no questions asked, service from the team and a little time to put together.

They decide to do the smaller job first, check out the 6 districts, and then see if they want to pursue that further. They get job intel, and head next door to a small bunk room where they all rest up for the night. Otters spends a little time reading about magical theory in a book from the shop, but is disappointed that she still can’t learn to cast spells. The next day, the team heads back out to Iron Horse Nation, collecting Heat’s weapons at the exit. Crossing the bridge, they find the 3 iconic smugglers awaiting them once more, and this time take the multi-sidecar motorcycle. Telling him they need a ride, he takes them to an overlook, where some figures are huddled below at a makeshift camp, seemingly planning something.

PPJ can detect they are fellow followers of Lone Wolf, roughly split between metaplanar manifestations and sapient spirits and/or material plane denizens. They make some various plans to steal some vehicles, and at Otters’ suggestion decide to leave not witnesses, so Otters concocts a poison made of TD’s stash of opiates mixed with Heat’s Gamma Scopolamine, which ShadowCreep delivers into the food and drink at the camp. Once the GS paralytic effect hits, the team strikes, killing all 6 of the people below with guns and spells. Aradia summons an Earth spirit to bury them, who seems not to like this Mad Max-y place due to all it’s pollutants, while Otters welds together two of the dead smugglers vehicles into one supercar that can carry the whole team, equipped with a flamethrower and HMG.

Heat gets Aradia to magically disguise the two of them like wealthy investors, and Otters drives them straight into the nearest town, parking in front of the bank and sheriff’s office. PPJ, invisible, sneaks into the sheriff’s office as the sheriff comes out in heavy welded together armor to see what’s going on. Metal shutters are dropping over doors and windows all over town while SC spots and points out some deputies with rifles atop some nearby roofs. Heat fast talks the sheriff while Aradia casts an illusion to make a large satchel of gold, and they convince him to bring them into the bank to make a deposit. They meet an elderly teller, who slowly starts counting out the illusory coins, while SC turns sideways and slips through the bars, sneaking around to check out the downstairs vault.

SC finds an assortment of goods locked up on shelves in one of the cells- it seems like the basement doubles as the town jail. Also, it isn’t a full vault, merely a large safe. He turns sideways to try to slip through the gap alongside the safe door, and succeeds, though he notices himself starting to fade as he does and pulls back, but not before seeing a strange rock chunk on a pedestal in the safe room. He heads back upstairs and gestures to the team to try to warn them a bit, but they don’t quite get it, so Heat convinces the sheriff to take Aradia down to examine the vault before they commit to the deposit, while Heat distracts the teller, who Creep has spotted has.a shotgun mounted under the counter, and warns Heat of.

Meanwhile, PPJ hasn’t found much in the sheriffs office except a rolodex style calendar, which he peruses a bit, finding that the sheriff has been threatened by the Disians into cooperating in some way. Having heard all the stuff next door in the bank, he slips in there and follows Aradia through the barred door after the sheriff, with Aradia providing a critical distraction to keep him from getting spotted. They head downstairs, with SC joining them again. Aradia converses with the sheriff a bit about why the goods are stored in a cell and not in the safe, and the sheriff fails to bluff past it, not yet noticing that the illusory gold is starting to flicker and fade. SC also start to fade again, slowly.

PPJ uses his magic hand ability, and reaches through and unlocks the safe door, but then experiences a shocking pain as his hand withers and shrivels, barely pulling it back in time before it solidifies. As the safe door swings open, PPJ and Aradia’s spells all fail, their foci and fetishes deactivate, their reagents crumble and neutralize, and SC starts to fade faster. The sheriff tries to warn Aradia against casting a spell, but she tries anyway, and gets hit with the drain but no other effect (the same happens to PPJ as he tries to slag the sheriff’s gun from behind him). With the sheriff now onto them, and clutching his withered hand, PPJ flees up the stairs. Aradia tries to bluff that she wasn’t involved in all the scheming but the sheriff isn’t quite buying it.

Luckily SC has spotted some dynamite in the lockup, and slips in, grabs some, lights it low on the fuse, and shoves it in the back of the sheriff’s armor. This basically blows him in half, also wrecking Aradia’s armor but freeing her from being trapped down here. She also flees upstairs, fearing whatever anti-magic effect is down here. SC stays and continues to fade. Heat rushes down after dodging a shotgun blast and taking out the aged bank teller in response. Outside, the deputies have gotten nervous and started shooting at the welded together buggy car, but Otters starts spinning it, kicking up dust, while TD fires off the flamethrower to discourage them. PPJ quickly takes the deputies out with targeted lightning bolts as he dashes out to the car.

Downstairs, Heat scoops up the strange rock, overcoming a feeling of mundanity and malaise to do so, and then hugs SC a final time as SC disappears into vapor. He dashes back upstairs expecting to make a quick escape, but finds that the team has taken out all visible opposition, as the townspeople hide in fear. They wrap the rock in PPJ’s duster, and tie it 20 meters behind the car, dragging it along as they drive off, just out of range of it’s effects. The mages are disturbed and pissed about what just happened, and Otters is stressed about drastically changing the balance of power by providing this to AZT, concerned it could easily be weaponized. As she sneak drives away to an isolated location, the team gradually talks it through and recognizes that AZT already has a dragon-killing nano-weapon, and ARES already has FAB-III, and other corps have other anti-magic weapons. So while this is surely a powerful artifact, it doesn’t seem likely to trigger a total shift in global power structures.

Thus reassured, they dig a hole and bury it, noting that as with the safe room, the earth packed around it seems to somewhat blunt it’s effect. Thinking about it, the closest thing they know of are astral voids, aka Fovae, but they’ve never heard of those being transportable before, though the rock seems to work in the same fashion. They head back to the City of Steel, having an easier time entering as they have already done the security interview and have their wristbands. They reach M&M, and Aradia heads in first, pissed off, but is slightly confused when the clerks eagerly agree to replace all her lost reagents and only want more details on what occurred. The team debriefs, and provides the location where they buried it to the clerks, who tell them that it sounds like this is the rumored substance known as “void rock” of Fovaeum. They seem very excited to get their hands on some, and quickly agree to upgrade the team’s bands, put them up another night, and at Heat’s insistence, provide some of the local food.

They go back to the bunkhouse to rest, and find a sad stew and old bread awaiting them. Rejecting this, they eat their packed rations instead, and sleep, with a quick convo before where Heat realizes he may have cause SC to fully fade by hugging him with the void rock, but then convinces himself that SC is obviously now living in the void rock, so it’s fine. Unable to sleep, Heat patches some damaged armor and waits for morning. When the rest of the team arises, they find that their upgraded bands have been delivered as promised, and head out to check out the other districts. They find various representations of modern Denver throughout- a tech sector on the rise, various Native American Nations on the borders, the lingering legacy of the divide of the UCAS and CAS, etc, but nothing truly stands out until they reach a heavy security checkpoint into the 7th district.

Unable to pass, they look past it and see the wrecked Tower of the Broken Heart, which looks to have once been three stories, with various atriums and balconies, but to have collapsed in on itself when it split down the middle. It appears composed of hearts made of stone, all cracked, broken, and dripping thick black blood. Various figures are studying it and seemingly attempting various stabilizing rituals- these seem more like material plane mages, and the team surmises this may be another location some of the team’s GW has been sending in have wound up. Not seeing a good way to sneak through or get more intel for now, they decide to call it good on the investigation, they decide to return home, and perhaps return for further work if they can get paid for it. Comparing the state of the tower to his memory of his past visit, PPJ confirms that much of this damage is new, likely from when he helped destroy Zebulon a few years back.

The Detective also announces that they’ll be leaving, retiring from the shadows after an enjoyable dalliance with the team. They will return to the Seelie Court for now to report on the threat of the void rock, as well as warning of the MCT/Disian/AZT conspiracy where they can. They will finish some investigations there, and then when they feel ready they plan to return to Motor City, and pick up their old life there- get a new apartment, a new tub, and reconnect with all their old contacts and clients, at least those who survived Bugs and Blackouts. The team is sad to see them go, but this does all make sense, except to Heat, who seems to have a very difficult time saying goodbye. He instructs StepMAMA to start programming a chatbot using Creep and TD’s chatlogs, and after assuring StepMAMA that he will also seek therapy/help IRL she agrees and gets to work. TD takes off to make their own way back to the Seelie Court.

The remaining four head back to M&M, with Heat retrieving his weapon from the entrance now that they have the upgraded clearance, and all step through a portal back to a rural area just south of Denver. Otters, delighted to be back online, calls the van and drives it down to them. Worried about what MCT might have gained from Creep’s capture, and warned by StepMAMA of some attempts to use Creep’s credentials, but not his internals, to log in to the TacNET (which she blocked), the team decides to proceed cautiously, and Otters does a thorough scan of the safehouse while PPJ projects over to check it out astrally. Not seeing any threats or evidence of intrusion, they do however spot 4 Tarot cards on Creep’s bedroll under his usual resting place, and see that is the full set of 7’s.

They decide to risk a trip to the safehouse to retrieve the cards and their gear, leaving only the Roseanne bot there to continue surveillance. Otters and Aradia head to their private Hub safehouses, while Heat and PPJ stick together and head into the Warrens, sheltering at Dracula’s coffin motel in the Paradise Lost Mall. They rest up and prepare to sell their GW paydata to Perri and the COTD, and to figure out next steps for work, new team-mates, and safehouses, as well as determining who to carefully warn about the void rock.

Creeps Last Ride
9/2/2024, 2:15:40 PM
Runners
Otters aka Roadkill
Pepper Jack
Heat
Aradia
the Brief Return of: The Detective
and a Sad Goodbye for: Creep

Meanwhile in the Seelie Court: The Detective received a message from Arcane, along with the first batch of visitors from The Order of the Garter. Following up on this, The Detective decided to head to the Mile High Realm to temporarily rejoin the team to finish out the Ghostwalker investigation, before tendering their resignation and staying on at the Seelie Court full-time. They booked passage on one of the sand-ships that sails the astral seas, and got delivered to the Black Canyon, seemingly waking up aboard an odd train as it emerges from cavernous tunnel in a mountainside.

Spotting Iva Linus Hathaway, TD tries to get their attention, but they seem a little vague and distant (kind of like ThinkTank in the Library of Alexandria), though they do fill TD in on payment options and where they can get off. Having heard that the team planned to start their search in the Shattered Lands, they decide to get off there, and pay a small amount of life experience, as well the unique memory of their bathtub getting blown up by a missile, for the transit. They both hear and feel the train respond as they make their payment, sensing that the contributions are also it’’s fuel.

As the team had fed one of these astral trains Iva’s Soul Coin as payment in Detroit, this could explains why this ghostly version of her is acting as a porter here also. Regardless, TD, stays aboard and avoids the temptations and lures of the dark forest on either side, wary of the numerous eyes gazing out from it’s depths. Some of the other passengers are not so steadfast, and jump from the train, to their peril. TD eventually arrives at the lone platform at the edge of the Shattered Lands, dust and windblown and seeming largely abandoned.

The last stretch of track is a wild almost loop-de-loop rising over a great void, apparently GW’s attempt to keep the hyper-planes from drifting further apart. TD also makes some quick conversation with a group of mages and spirits that looks like one of the team’s GW has been sending into the Rifts for some time now. They seem to be taking measurements and performing experiments aboard the train, but seem a little fuzzy on exactly how long they’ve been there, and don’t seem to actively be doing any harm or directly causing Rifts at the moment.

TD continues on foot into the Shattered Lands, hearing the distinctive boom of Creep’s hand cannon and heading that way, eventually spotting the geodesic dome in the distance. At the dome, Heat has called the retreat and lays down some covering fire while falling back behind some low hills and hummocks to avoid return fire from the drones, 3 of which emerge from the open structure. He does succeed in getting most of the team to begin retreating, and in keeping the injured APEP Rift Jumpers from flanking them. Otters, being first to flee, is also first to spot TD approaching, and they are able to provide some critical leadership to PPJ and Aradia to get them up and moving away from the dome.

PPJ is also on fire, as the Rift Jumper he had been grappling with immolated shortly after their death, which TD helps him put out, just before it would have knocked him out. Aradia gets taken down by a spell from whatever mage is targeting them, but Heat overrides her Auto-Injector to get her up and moving quicker, and they all retreat out of line of sight. Unfortunately, Creep also gets taken down by a spell, which finally defeats his amazing ability to dodge, and gets dragged inside by the drones, as they and the Rift Jumpers retreat back in and the dome seals itself up.

With no signal from Creep, and having seen him flatline on the TacNet from the combo of magic and drone fire, they keep falling back, aware that if they are to attempt a rescue they need to rest and regroup. They spot a lone cabin in the distance, and head there, hoping for lowkey shelter for the night. The denizens seems like planar manifestations of “frontier people” in a kind of generic, hardscrabble way, but are friendly and welcoming enough. The team notices that while outside the cabin, there is a permanent midday sun, while inside there’s snow and cold outdoors. Stange. They make a deal to rent the odd collection of mounts out back of the cabin the next day in exchange for help clearing some fields, and spend a few hours healing and patching armor, before huddling under the table to sleep.

They chat with the settlers the next day while they quickly clear the fields, and get warned of the dangers of both the dome and the town on the plane, especially for outsiders like them. It seems like the RIft Jumpers and some cultists in town engage in trade of some sort, often including live captives. The dome also seems to get off-plane visitors using anachronistic craft, and does also seem to sometimes host parties. Regardless, they realize they are not properly equipped to try to rescue Creep at the moment. They catch TD up on the state of the investigation, and Denver more generally, and TD does the same re: their investigations and the Seelie Court.

With their work done early, they take the mounts (a skeletal biomechanical clydesdale, an armor-plated and horned rhino-horse, and a small drone-like mule). Otters proves a dab hand at animal handling, even without the ability to rig any of these. After some tense discussion and interpersonal dynamic navigation, they decide to go to the town despite the warnings, with Otters and PPJ watching the mounts outside of town, while the rest of the team sneaks in in disguise to see what’s up and try to get some info. Aradia and TD disguise themselves and Heat, and they walk into town, finding a classic, iconic “old west” town, basically straight out of a movie. They notice the townspeople seem split about half and half between looking approving and disapproving towards their cult disguises.

The townspeople also seem split between more planar representations like the farmers- kind of ill-defined and generic, and more more independent, fully defined spirits. They also see some non-spirit denizens, who mostly seem to be affiliated with the cult. PPJ and Otters, doing some bird drone surveillance via Morticia, report that this cult is almost surely a metaplanar representation of the Path of the Sun, the official state church of AZT, though this version seems a bit inaccurate, and definitely ahistorical to the region and time represented. This gives some good tips on how to play it though, so they make a quick plan and head towards the church, but not before spotting PPJ trying to sneak into town, not in disguise.

His vindictiveness and hatred of Blood Magic got the best of him, and he snuck off from Otters while she was in full VR in the drone, and headed in. He almost instantly gets spotted by the sheriff as an outsider, and a classic high noon standoff takes place on main street. PPJ subtly waves Heat off when he offers assistance, and the other 3 hang back and blend in with the townfolk who have cleared the street. PPJ tries to intimidate the sheriff, but ends up getting it reversed on him, and heads to the saloon to drown his sorrows, with some mocking laughter following him in.

Luckily the bartender claims he is famously neutral, and when PPJ offers reagents in exchange for info, proves to be quite useful as a source of general info. Even more interestingly, someone casually bumps into PPJ while passing by and tells him if he opposes the cult, to go to the general store and ask for “the Heart”. He heads out into the street to do so. Meanwhile, the other 3 have blagged their way into the church, the newest-looking building in town (PPJ found out that the AZT faction is definitely not original to the town, but also not brand new), having intimidated the two young acolytes outside, who seem like local converts.

Inside, they have a little more trouble, as they don’t know the right passphrases (or language), but manage to con their way through, explaining that they are Earth visitors to the Mystics and Magicks branch in the City of Steel, and being entrusted with a sealed package to deliver back to M&M. Heat also holds his hand in the magnified, burning-hot sunlight in the center of the church, until his skin blisters, both to prove his faith and because he needs to feel something punitive, as he blames himself a bit for what happened to Creep, but as the team’s “leader” can’t let on to anyone that that’s going on.

They also see that church uses magnifying lenses for their sacrifices, explaining that the sun will purify everything. They get the feeling that although some of the cultists seem like true believers, the material plane leaders are aware that they are to some extent running a scam, though they do seem faithful in their way. The team, between conversation and assensing, gets the feeling that this church and the sacrifices are designed to gradually alter the mana of the local area, and maybe eventually the whole plane, which GW surely won’t be happy about, if he isn’t aware already. They send a warning message to PPJ that the cult seems to specifically target outsiders.

He receives this as he is walking into the general store, so decides to first get some local duds, which he does, replacing his burnt coat. He then asks for “the Heart”, and is ushered into the back, and shown down a stair into the basement, hidden under a false barrel. He heads down and finds some local denizes waiting, these seem more of the free spirit type. After doing some initial establishment of bona fides, PPJ shares what the team knows about the situation with the MHR, the Veil, GW, and the cult. In exchange, the spirits explain that they are members of “The Hearts of Zebulon”, the astral group that the team knows is allied with the Veil, with each group generally tending to their side of the divide.

The HoZ explain that they want GW and other material plane dwellers to stop meddling in their plane and affairs and leave them be, confirming that the meddling is what is causing the Rifts. However, GW seems to only be responsibly for around 1/3rd of the Rifts with his current activity, trying to keep the planes from drifting further. He has been maintaining the increasingly tenuous transit connections between the planes to keep them all loosely affiliated, and though the HoZ say he is certainly dangerous they don’t view him as the current largest threat.

That honor goes to a group they refer to as “The Dissonants”- this seems to be the alliance of MCT and the Disians (“baldies”) that the team has been running up against. The Null Sect don’t seem to be present here at least, but interestingly, the HoZ confirm the farmer’s intel that these Dissonants engage in trade with the AZT cultists, especially of living metahumans. This could explain what’s been happening to all the people who have been going missing through Rifts of late, including many Veil members, as well as some of what’s been happening to all the team’s GW has been sending in and not getting back. The HoZ also explain that they think the sacrifices that the cult performs, as well as gradually aspecting the local mana, are also powering up some strange crystals that they’ve seen them trading to the Rift Jumpers.

Recognizing Blood Magic when he hears of it, PPJ follow this topic further, and the HoZ confirm that the crystals seem to be used to both power and stabilize further Rifts, along with who knows what else, which is deeply worrying. Speculating together, they guess that these Dissonants are using GW"s distraction, bad reputation for caring about civilian safety, and the pre-existing presence of Rifts due to his past with Zebulon as cover for their own Rift experiments, and they guess that the Dissonants are using some random Rifts to sow chaos while also obscuring their true agenda, whatever it is.

With that, PPJ vows to help them somehow, with them providing some tips as to how he could do so. They sneak him out of town, and he rejoins Otters, as the rest of the team also heads out and meets up with them, with the package from the church for M&M in tow. They decide not to open the package, as it has some anti-tamper seals, but also decide not to help the HoZ locally right now by attacking the church, which PPJ is really pushing for. Heat wants to finish the main job and get paid, Aradia doesn’t want to alienate valuable allies at AZT, and Otters, though initially excited about helping locals, is quickly convinced to go the other way with no regret. TD stays out of it, as they are exiting the team shortly and don’t want to make big decisions for the group.

They head “north” on the mounts, reaching a perilous, crumbling land bridge towards a welded together metal wall, crowned with spikes and towers of scrap- the crossing into the Iron Horse Nation. Three atompunk looking figures yell down at them, as they dismount, and send the mounts back towards the farmer. The figures offer a number of different challenges for the team to complete to be allowed to travel on, with them deciding to all stick together and participate in some kind of car combat race known as the “Wreckoning”. They are ushered in, and Otters quickly gets very pumped when she realizes she can rig the scrap built war rig they are provided to use in the contest.

The limited rules are explained, and the team members all choose a station to man in the rig, with TD ending up as the gunner, being the best of many mediocre options, as Otters is busy driving and Heat is doing the hacking station. They kick it off by launching smoke and then slewing to the side, with Otters going offroad and taking a hard route, trying to sneak to the finish line while avoiding other combatants. Aradia assists with a distracting illusory car in the main scrum. They manage to reach the track exit first, but having been told that they will earn little respect if they deal no damage, they set up an ambush, and wreck the first other rig to reach the end with a combo of gunfire, spellcraft and ramming.

Otters guns it out of the track in reverse, and the team are told they have free travel of the plane, though they are on their own as to providing personal security etc. It seems very post-apoc, everyone for themselves vibes, though there are hints that are more established communities, the team is told that there are run by boring, crusty sheriffs trying to establish “order” instead of embracing the churn of the rest of the plane. The team decides to bypass all of this for now, and takes a ride from one of the atompunks on his gyrocopter. He flies them up to the crossing to the City of Steel- another tenuous and twisted bridge, but this one of modern alloys, covered in tank traps and barbed wire, and leading to a high security checkpoint.

The team prepares to cross, planning to rest again once they get inside, as they aren’t feeling good about sleeping securely in the current plane. Meanwhile, Creep awakens in captivity, and finds himself interrogated and Mind Probed by one of the baldies. As he is paralyzed by Gamma Scopolamine, he doesn’t have a ton of options, and he refuses the offer to go over to the other side. Sensing that he may not get a better chance to take his own way out, he uses his internal router to eject all his limbs, and then vents all his air from his internal tanks. As he loses consciousness, he senses a growing burning in his chest- seemingly Thisbe Sorrel being good to her word re: her ritual to deny his body to the Shadow spirits whenever he died.

As he fades into nothing, his last hope is that all the vented oxygen will create some kind of explosion, maybe taking out the baldy or at least doing some damage, but he feels good that at least he didn’t betray his friends….

Geodesic Glory Hole
8/26/2024, 9:55:27 PM
Runners
Creep
Otters aka Roadkill
Pepper Jack
Heat
Aradia

The team rests up from the previous couple of jobs and waits to hear back from Le Guide on Vishala’s whereabouts. They get a call from The Collector and Silk, inquiring if they are down to do another job of making connections between different groups in town. After pausing to do some largely inconclusive but vaguely positive digging on Silk, they get back to them and Heat negotiates for pay. They are told it is once again an info gathering job, this time looking into the ASPS. TC and Silk want the team to find out if two local ASPS members, Hypnalia and Louis Belaran are: free of Black Lodge association; not a on a suicidal revenge quest against Ghostwalker; and generally trustworthy/open to allies.

As the team already has a decent rapport with Belaran from helping to rescue Ersatz, the Refugee, they figure this sounds very doable, and agree. After trying to convince Belaran to meet in person, and finding him too busy, they take up his suggestion of an astral meet, and decide to try to do both interviews at the same time, in case anything sketchy arises, so the ASPS members can’t compare notes. PPJ and Aradia project over to the agreed upon meeting spot and find Belaran’s projection waiting. They have a friendly convo, and find that he seems to check all the boxes as required.

They also find out that he is more of a global fixer/floater for the ASPS, traveling to problem areas to help troubleshoot. He gives them a little further update on the situation in Paris, and France in general, and also provides some tantalizing details about Ersatz’s strange powers. Belaran also passes along Ersatz’s thanks to the team for helping to rescue him from the Black Lodge and others who didn’t have his best interests at heart, so it seems like they made a good call trusting Belaran then and feel able to continue doing so for now.

The rest of the team does some research and calls up some contacts, and eventually tracks Hypnalia down to the Viper’s Den bar, the former home of Harlequin’s resistance against GW, in the Aurora Warrens. Only PPJ has been there before IRL, but weirdly, most of the team recently visited a strange version of the bar in the Reflecting Pool, when Chiron assisted them on their dive there. They drive over, and Creep ID’s members of two gangs hanging out- the Vipers street gang, and the Blockade matrix gang. He’s heard rumors they’ve teamed up recently, and as the team enters the bar they do so that both gangs seem to be tag-teaming some surreptitious security, as well as outnumbering the civilian patrons.

A small group of mages at the back are maintaining some illusions for the patrons’ enjoyment while also conversing amongst themselves, and Heat approaches, announcing that they are seeking Hypnalia.After some slightly tense rep checking, the team is invited to sit for a chat, and does so. Hypnalia seems a lot more guarded and cautious than Belaran, possibly due to the fact that she seems charged with actually sticking around and getting this chapter up and running.

However, she does confirm that she despises the Black Lodge, and though she also doesn’t seem fond of GW she says that she is too busy trying to help the denizens of Denver deal with magical threats to pursue revenge in either case, and that any of the gangers (who seem allied with the ASPS here- one faction providing magic, one matrix, one physical) were dumb enough to openly oppose GW they would not still be around. After some dancing around it and hinting at stuff, Heat confirms that the team is basically in the same boat.

Confirming that Hypnalia’s rep for ruthlessness, but not cruelty, seems accurate, and having fulfilled the other requirements, they feel out her interest in allies, since she seems more locally in charge than Belaran. She requires them to ID who they are working for, and feeling that will assist mission success, they do so, gaining Hypnalia’s approval, at least for now. She warns them in return that Cat, the fixer, is more affiliated with GW than she lets on, and tells the team to make sure they know what kind of work they are doing for and what info they share. With that done, they report back in to TC and SIlk and get paid. Still not having heard back from Le Guide, they decide to head back to the metaplanes, specifically the Mile High Realm, to finish their investigation into “what GW is up to” for the Children of the Dragon and Alexis Glimmerscale.

They do some research and gear up, refreshing on the nature of the 4 known and one rumored connected hyper-planes, and also on how to go about traveling to, from and between them. They decide to pay Silver Streak to smuggle their gear over to Arcane’s and get portaled in again, aiming to land in the Shattered Lands, which PPJ is so excited about that he adds a new stylish duster to his costume store western wear. The smuggling and portal go off without a hitch, though they also tip Arcane off to start keeping an eye out for dark magics in town, telling him they’ll pay for any intel he passes along. They also hear from Glimmerscale that a Catholic banisher/exorcist from Boston was approved to enter the city to take care of the Shadow spirits possessing the family in Sunshine Estates, so that’s good.

They enter the Shattered Lands, and with the biodrone aloft get a pretty good luck at the place. There’s a feeling of endless mountains rising to the “west” and grassy plain descending into infinity to the “east”. To the “south” they see a rickety train track extending off over an open void from a dusty, ill-used platform at the plane’s edge- this seems to be the broken connection the Black Canyon and it’s creepy forests, though it’s possible the track would still carry them over if they followed it. To the “north”, they start to see some oil spills and tire tracks, as well as some smoke clouds, suggesting that a crossing to the Iron Horse Nation is somewhere that way. They also see various communities scattered around, seemingly the plane’s representation of different periods of history and people from around what is the modern FRFZ.

However, the team becomes fixated on a large geodesic dome, which squats anachronistically a few hours walk away to the “east”. Having heard rumors that an MCT exec has settled in the MHR and occasionally hosts interesting parties, and is also obsessed with the 6th World Tarot, they decide to head there first. Some of them are near blinded by the aggressive reflections from the dome as they approach but they manage to jury-rig some solutions and reach it with no trouble. Heat and Creep knock on the outside, and receive responses on a small screen. After affirming that they have Tarot, and flashing a card to prove it, the team threatens to leave when they refuse to hand the card over in exchange for entry.

This is when the ambush is sprung. Only Aradia and Creep notice in time, and the rest of the team comes under fire from what Aradia recognizes as an APEP Consortium rift jumper team. This also explains the odd perfect circles she has seen near the dome. There are also some vehicle tracks, both of local carts and wagons and more advanced craft. PPJ is snared in the initial attack and reeled in towards one of the attackers as the other team-mates dodge. A massive firefight breaks out. The opponents seems quite skilled and hard to hit, but between Otters with a Mule drone, Creep, Heat, and distracting magic from Aradia and PPJ, they manage to take one of the rift jumpers out and injure two more.

However, Otter’s drone is destroyed when she is forced to use it to defend her prone body, and PPJ’s fancy armor is also wrecked. Also, someone starts countering their spells to various degrees. Heat launches a grenade inside the dome as a crack starts to open, and blows something up as it deflects along an inner wall. Then the team comes under suppressing fire from a drone with a mounted mini-gun. They flee and take cover, with the rift jumper team also having fallen back into cover in response to the team’s attacks. After refusing an offer to throw down their weapons and Tarot in order to be allowed to leave, Heat calls a tactical retreat and they begin trying to figure out how they are going to get out of there….

Blackout Investigation Part 2
8/19/2024, 11:42:05 AM
Runners
Creep
Otters aka Roadkill
Pepper Jack
Heat
Aradia

The team hurriedly makes good their escape from the vicinity of the ice rink in Elbert, with PPJ making Heat invisible alongside Aradia who did so for herself. They sneak back to the truck, joined by Creep with the experiment fragments, and after finishing off the LoneStar support drones, Otters pulls the Mules back as well, and repositions the van. Once everyone is loaded up, they leave town, with some slightly off-target shotgun blasts booming out behind them. Otters take a deeply off-road route through mechanized farmlands, with Heat spoofing various sensors that they pass to avoid detection. They reach more normal road grids with a new van skin, spoof chip, plate, and magical look, and reenter normal traffic.

Feeling like they lost any potential pursuit, they decide to take a look at the recovered fragments Creep has in his leg. Assessing them they can see that they aren’t alchemical, but do appear related to spirit summoning and binding, as well as some other mechanism they are having a hard time ID-ing. They also can tell that possessing the fragments or being in their presence is slowly aspecting everyone’s background count, with Creep already showing lingering signs, so they probably want to offload these quickly. They decide to keep doing evasive driving for the moment while Aradia uses Psychometry to further analyze.

She touches the fragments and gets pulled back in to some vibes- it seems like some techs set them up in an area in the center of a large ritual circle, which Aradia recognizes bits of from Creep’s footage from inside the rink. The sigils of the circle seem related to summoning and binding 6 different “standard” forms of spirit, from some of the closer planes. However, the grenades seem to torment them and corrupt them by opening micro-rifts to a toxic plane (likely Miggon or near to there), though the explosion and corruption seems to have blown bigger than expected, based on them abandoning the site immediately after they blew the doors off.

Piecing this together with Creep’s intel and what they know of Vishala’s interests and experiments, they determine that what she is doing is two-fold. First, she seems to be testing her new “corruption grenades” to see how quickly they can corrupt spirits and turn them toxic, and also how that effect changes related to the power of the summoned spirit (since Creep saw 6 spirits of varied Force). Secondly, the experiment also seems to relate to the blimp experiments that caused the local blackouts- seeing how quickly they could force a change in background count in an area, in this case by trapping and tormenting/corrupting bound spirits.

Since this is all quite nefarious and they are all now carrying a small amount of toxic taint to their auras, they call up Le Guide, and arrange to meet him at a nearby wildlife refuge in Stapleton. Otters does a little research, and realizes the refuge is a former USA chemical weapons testing site, so given the rampant growth in the decades sense and the likely lingering toxic traces, it seems like a great place to do a magical item handover without getting spotted. They head there, meet up with LG, and hand over the fragments. He has brought some kind of shielded bag to carry them in, and pays up, gladly paying some extra when Heat explains that the job wasn’t quite a milk run after all, despite his previous assurance that it would be.

Heat and Aradia burn the SINs they gave to LS, and Aradia replaces her own, while Heat gets a new one with team funds, to avoid going into further debt with another team-mate. LG says he will be in touch about next steps once has time to analyze the fragments and search around town some more, and the team agrees they are still interested in pursuing this matter. They mostly head back to the safehouse to rest, though Heat and Aradia split off to head into the Hub to check out the new Basilisk brothel. Arriving, they find that some of the advertised statues made of misbehaving patrons are parked out front, with digital signs around their necks showing how long they have left to remain calcified, seemingly based on their infraction, some of which are fairly obvious from the poses in which they are frozen. Heat peruses the digital service menu as they enter, and tells Aradia to call him if she needs help, heading off to have some fun, at a rate which he can afford.

Aradia chats it up with a Lamia bartender, seeing that the staff also includes, Nagas, various Shifters and a variety of Changelings, including one of the new, rare Garuda types. There is also a hooded basilisk in a comfortable enclosure off to the side, as well as more statues scattered about the interior. Aradia accepts a “Blow Me” mixed drink, with some poisonous extractions from a rare fish (or at least the synthesized equivalent) adding a little frisson, after the bartender recommends it as a compliment to Aradia’s novacoke high. Aradia passes along info on the Order of the Garter, and the bartender agrees to distro it to the workers here, explaining that they are all independent contractors, with the owners providing the space, security, and client screening resources.

Satisfied with their outing, Heat and Aradia head back to the safehouse and crash out. The next day, they get some news: the ZDF, with a quiet Horizon assist, has raided the Shedim outpost in the Englewood Barrens, and taken down their smuggling op. So sounds like PPJ is even with Doublewide. Horizon seems to have done some careful PR as well, though not quite in the direction the team wanted- the news stories all hint that the dark magics in the area are likely leftover from AZT’s time occupying it, and also clear Knight Errant of the rumors of involvement in area residents disappearing by showing evidence that some of the missing people turned out to have been grabbed by Shedim.

As to whether either of these claims is partially or fully true, who knows. Ghostwalker also takes some subtle hits for being distracted enough to let Shedim flourish, but it isn’t the official narrative, and seems consigned to fringe forums. Also, an A-rated Japanese corp called Komatsu was apparently destroyed in a bungled data steal raid after they apparently developed technology to keep metaplanar portals open and stable. Rumors in the shadows seem unclear who was behind the raid, or how Komatsu managed such a discovery.

Heat gets a worried message from one of the 3 guards they paid for info on Vishala, telling him that he and one of the others had to flee town and hide out after their 3rd colleague was killed by a group which tagged “Omega Dawn” at the murder scene. He had apparently been talking positively about trying to set up nonlethal arrangements with runners to reduce the risk to guards, and word reached the corporate death squad, who targeted him. The surviving guards ask for Heat to tell them if they’re clear to return safely sometime.

Heat calls up Johnny Silver to warn him of the risks to the ShadChaps, after PPJ relays the tale of his run-in with the OD in Boston- when he was kidnapped and tossed into the Temple of Terror and being interrogated (unsuccessfully, due to a fortuitous magical mind wipe from the team’s allies). Talking with Johnny, Heat gets the background- the OD is a cross-corporate, ultra-secret cell-based terror group, mostly comprised of corpsec who has lost friend, coworkers, family, etc to runner activities. They are officially renounced by all of the corps, though everyone assumes they must have some reasonably high-level supporters, as they always seem to have top-notch gear and be able to get around easily.

Heat also calls up Hype to warn him, and Hype seems not overly worried about his personal safety, though he appreciates the heads up. He asks for an update on the ShadChaps, and is disappointed Heat has allowed himself to get pushed out of day to day involvement, telling him to get back involved and make sure he picks the right allies as the ShadChaps are getting established locally- there are some organizers who will assure longterm success, and some who won’t. Heat says he gets it, though he assures the team afterwards that he means that in a different way than Hype does. They also get a call from Le Guide, offering a side job. He makes clear that it’s a favor for a contact of his, and not a job he’s personally invested in, but the French Touch corp collective needs a runner on the run extracted back to France. The team says they’ll look into it.

The runners name is SIM-Eon, a troll decker, and they quickly find posts from him on both Jackpoint and The Nexus, suggesting he’s a well-connected runner in the French scene, and has become one of the go-to “local experts” on Paris and it’s surroundings for the international runner community. He seems generally well-liked, so the team mostly balks at doing an extraction, with many wanting to instead help him out. Creep wants to remain loyal to Le Guide, with the rest of the team leaning partway or fully towards assisting SIM-Eon to various degrees. First Heat calls Perri, and gets more info.

Apparently, SIM-Eon pulled a job and needed to lie low for a bit afterword, but it was big enough to attract corp attention in a bad way. Perri is worried because the enforcer of the French cyber-democracy, and massive Expert System named “Marianne” has apparently been broadening her reach, doing open-source intel gathering and some light hacking around the world to track people who have caught her interest but escaped the reach of her physical sensors. Worried that SIM-Eon will accidentally get tagged in the background of some random tourists snap-shots or something, the Nexus have him laying real low right now.

Also, there’s another interested party- GW himself apparently heard that the runner was in town, and wants him brought in- GW has a long running interest in Marianne, and may want a captive in-house expert on both that system and how to avoid or subvert it, especially if he is looking at setting up his own version of it in Denver, as he is rumored to be. This pushes most of the team over the edge into wanting to actively help SE. Creep still prefers loyalty to Le Guide, so they compromise a bit, and call LG, telling him that SE is friends of friends so they can’t take the job, and can’t recommend other runners to take it, as they’d be in the same position.

LG says he understands and appreciates the caution, explaining that he will still have to track down some local contractors to look into the matter, but will avoid sticking his neck out for it or expecting success, but must still appease his contacts and keep them happy. Feeling that he legit isn’t bothered by this turn of events, the team puts SE in touch with Arcane about arranging some alchemical disguises to avoided detection, while Heat puts together some high quality forgeries showing evidence that SE has been spotted in Seattle, St. Louis, New Orleans and elsewhere, hoping to hint that he is hiding out in various other locales, all of which happen to be under the Sea Dragon’s matronage.

They get a “thanks!” message passed to them through the Nexus from SE, and an assurance that they now have a solid French connection, if it becomes useful. They next hear from Ringmaster, and catch up a bit about events in Denver and Detroit/Motor City. Ringmaster wants them to find some kind of amoral dirt on Seader-Krupp, in order to tempt the Iron Cavalry merc corp (who the team recently encountered at the Horse Play brothel) away from SK employ. Ringmaster says he has lined up a contract for them through Evo, to ensure pay continuity, but explains that the International Mercenary Association is a stickler about breaking contracts, so they’ll need something good to get the IC away from their longterm employer.

He says he can’t tell them more at the moment about why this needs to happen, but ensures them that it will be for the good of metahumanity in the long run. They agree to look into it, but don’t have any current leads. The Collector and Silk call them up, and ask them to take on another investigative job for them. The team agrees, and gets an info packet with two areas to investigate, especially for astral weirdness, both corresponding to local blackouts they had previously investigated. TC and Silk also mention to the team that they know the team had previously looked into the blackouts for Peregrine, but warn that he is too closely tied to SK to be able to trust that he will do the best thing for the people of Denver, and not instead use what info they find to make a move against GW, since Lofwyr is beefing with him. The team agrees to keep the info exclusive, especially since Ringmaster just subtly warned them that SK is up to something potentially nefarious.

They head to the first area, stocking up on some gardening and surveying supplies and appropriate uniforms, and gaining access to the human supremacist Archer Heights neighborhood, bordering the Aurora Warrens, the site of the former eugenics clinic. They spend a while taking measurements and doing some public area lawn care, with Heat’s quickly forged business website holding up to casual scrutiny. Aradia and PPJ begin analyzing the local mana, which is definitely off in some way, and find a small fear-based background count. It seems like the strange magic they had previously detected, which in retrospect seems Disian, is absent.

Heat can also see that the large Dissonance pool previously present has shrunk in a normal fashion, as it seems it hasn’t been tended to, so that at least is good news. Otters keep an eye out for threats approaching, but sees none, and Creep spots a scared child watching them from a nearby house, which looks to have a suspicious amount of recently added locks added to it’s entrances. They decide to look into it more, with Creep sneaking up and seeing the child mouth “help me” before being snatched back into what can only be magical darkness. He starts picking a lock to get in, as PPJ projects in, planning to manifest to get a look around.

PPJ checks for auras in the house, and finds 3: the child, and 2 adults, who seems to be Shadow spirits possessing living humans, and who are masking their auras. Seeing that they’ve been spotted, one of them races to attack PPJ, who flees to his body quickly after being injured, for once rejecting astral combat, wisely. He preps his astral armor spell in case they’re going back in as the team debates strategy. Creep gets the door open just as PPJ’s astral form retreats and hears some creepy, gravelly whispers from inside before the door slams shut in a gust of dark wind in his face. Proper haunted house.

Realizing that they don’t have the right tools and skills to banish the spirits without harming the hosts, who are likely the girl’s parents, they decide to call in some backup, and just focus on the data, since that’s what they’re getting paid for. After some debate, they contact Alexis Glimmerscale at the Draco Foundation, who seems surprised they’re calling for a favor on another job without ever reporting in on the job they were doing for her. Aradia blows her mind with a progress report on all the info they uncovered on Ghostwalker at the Seelie Court, and says they’ll get around to finishing the job soon, and Glimmerscale agrees to coordinate with the ASPS to bring some licensed banishers to town.

She says they’ll maybe also have to coordinate with the Catholics to get some banishers in who will be approved by GW/ZDF border security, since the FRFZ is a safe haven for spirits and all, but says she’ll handle it. She asks for more info on what they’re investigating, and the team says they’ll give their Johnsons her number in case they want to coordinate info. PPJ and Aradia finish assensing the area, but decide to also analyze the other blackout area before drawing any conclusions. The team calls up Silk and TC as they drive, report in on their findings so far, and put them in touch with Glimmerscale- they once again seem excited to be expanding their pool of allies and say they will coordinate with her to try to save the family.

Heading way south to Colorado Springs, the team uses the same disguises to enter the Sunshine Estates private neighborhood there, which they know is a front for a secret AZT spec ops deep cover team, as well as being a functional gated community.

PPJ and Aradia start assensing again, with Heat confirming the same depleting Dissonance effect here, Otters spotting a few undercover AZT security people around town but not on alert, and Creep eventually noticing a blood trail leading into a corner store, with a snapped dog leash out front. They pause to investigate, heading inside to find a badly injured man bleeding out on the floor, while some customers try to stabilize him and the shopkeep yells about who’s gonna pay for the mess. Aradia steps in and magically heals the injured man, though his lower legs are nowhere to be found, and Creep pays off the shopkeep to shut him up. Everyone seems very surprised and impressed that a gardening crew has a magical healer on staff so they decide not to stick around too long, but do get a quick tale of what happened.

Apparently the man was out walking his dog, when a massive armored bear came out of nowhere and bit off his legs after chomping on him. His dog leapt to his defense, getting the bear to let go of the man, before it grabbed the dog and ambled off somewhere to eat, seemingly planning to return for the man. Doing some magical analysis, they get the feeling a rift had recently opened and closed in the near area, and assume the bear isn’t from her. Otters quickly tracks it via blood trail to a nearby alley where she sees it finish swallowing the hindquarters of a large dog.

The bear is tusked and does seem to be wearing armor, so the team speculates this a fighting bear from some astral arena, and likely a plasma. Given justified fear of the massive beasts, including from PPJ and other past team-mates’ direct encounters, they decide to pass this problem off as well. Aradia calls up Aguila, the local AZT covert ops team lead (and Blood practitioner) and manages to subtly insult him again, though also offering him the chance to take down a major threat in his neighborhood. She also passes along one of the sample experimental bioweapons that Simonetta Chandra gave her, and tells Aguila to report the results into Chandra to score some further points in the corp.

Aguila accepts, and PPJ and Aradia finish their astral investigation before the team quickly leaves the area. They get a call from Chandra a short while later, saying that she got an encoded message passed to her from the corp ops division, reporting that the bear was not a shifter, so the experimental weapon only got half a field test, but proved quite damaging, and also that an image was included, of Aguila holding up a bloody collar, seemingly taken from the bear, with a sigil of a massive sword crossed over a broken stone ring. Doing some thinking about the symbol, the team thinks this bear likely came from the metaplane of War/Battle, specifically from it’s sole city, known as Coliseum, where endless bouts between varied combatants take place.

Putting this together with what they found in Archer Heights, as well as the larger astral investigation, they put together that the blimp experiments there seem ongoing, in a way. The fear of blackouts was enough to tip the local mood and manasphere over a precipice, to where they established a fear-based background count, which attracted more rifts and more dark spirits, which created more fear, etc in what seems to be a slow-moving but potentially self-perpetuating cycle of astral worsening. Contrasted with what they figured out about Vishala’s experiments, it seems like MCT is pursuing at least two paths to triggering large scale mana shifts, one passive and slower (the blimps, blackouts, fear route) and one faster and more active (the spirit corruption).

This is very worrying, for obvious reasons, so PPJ and Heat tag-team another impressive presentation to the Johnsons, who once again say they need some time to analyze, but seem impressed, and happily pay the increased rate that Heat head negotiated for. The team rests up and prepares for whatever comes next.

Rural Ice Rinks
8/12/2024, 10:58:28 AM
