The team, returned from the Reflecting Pool, brief Heat on what they found there and chat for a while before Chiron gets bored of driving them around and drops them back off at their van- he hadn’t wanted to stay stationary after their return just in case, as the smugglers had left and taken their advanced warning systems with them. They start heading back to town, but Otters decides to make a stop near their safehouse at a uniform supply shop to freshen up her duds, and then Heat and Acadia convince everyone to go clothes shopping at a fancier nearby mall instead.

Otters and Creep both get some new formal wear for appropriate occasions, and PPJ updates his “ronin” cloak with a low-sling old west style bullet belt and a piratical eyepatch, both from a costume supply store. Although the others aren’t 100% about this look, they head out in their new outfits to meet again with The Collector and Silk at Club Olympus, in the Hub. They have once again rented out a private room in the interior of the central mountain, and Creep makes a point of stating that they dressed their best to try to change the Johnsons’ impression of the team from the previous meeting, which seems to amuse Silk. Aradia has helped PPJ out a bit and made his look more unified while still being very “him”.

PPJ takes the lead on presenting the info, doing a kind of classic “red string” conspiracy theory board in AR, though Heat also jumps in to clean and clear everything up a bit. With the two of them they cogently present the info they got in the Reflecting Pool and the Johnsons are appropriately disturbed. After confirming that the team is fully briefed on the dangers of the Null Sect, they confer for a while about what this could mean, and offer the team more work in the future once they have time to process this and figure out next steps.

Heat asks if they want to drink and party a bit since they’re in the club, and they demur, saying the info requires some immediate thought and analysis. They do arrange to pay the team a little extra to broker a potential intro to Stiletto, so they can hopefully collaborate, as it seems their formerly parallel investigations are intersecting. Next up, the team stops by the new Centaur-run brothel, Horse Play. Aradia has already been white-listed it seems, due to her work with the Order of the Garter, so they have an easy time getting in, past the muscular Centaur bouncer.

Creep recognizes that much of the security staff as well as many of the patrons seem to be members of the Iron Cavalry, a merc corp sometimes derogatorily referred to as “The Freak Show”, a sobriquet that they have embraced. It is made up primarily of Centaurs, shape-shifters, and other metasapients, and tends to get some unique work opportunities due to it’s unique composition. What they are doing on a deployment in Denver is a good question. Perhaps due to their presence, there are also a fair number of other off-duty mercs in the bar, outnumbering the civvie’s there.

The team makes themselves at home, with Heat bemused when Creep has a successful social interaction with the bartender, when he asks for an “Alpha CENTAUR-y” and the bartender chortles and says that he’s renaming the bar’s cosmos as that from now on. Creep is delighted. Aradia converses with some of the staff about the potential safety risks of drastically different size mammals copulating and they explain to her some of the various methods and means they have of addressing that.

Since the place seems legit and up to her standards, Aradia asks the bartender to spread around some invites to TOoTG amongst the employees, which he agrees to, as he says that everyone here is “grass fed and free range”- seemingly making a little joke of his own. But it seems to fit with the general do no harm ethos of the owner’s other business around the corner, the popular vegan restaurant “Feed Trough”. PPJ partakes of some of the services offered after getting chatted up by some employees who seem to perhaps correctly identify his costume outfit as some form of fetish wear.

With that complete, they head back to the safehouse, where Aradia is shocked to find a new Tarot card on her pillow, right where she laid her head to go to sleep in the Reflecting Pool- it is a variant version of The Fool (which they’ve previously seen in the 6th World Tarot as “the Bastard”) but seemingly from one of the other two strange new decks they’ve encountered in the meta planes. The team reviews drone and other sensor footage around the safehouse and don’t see anyone dropping it off, so it seems to just have manifested, as the Tarot is sometimes wont to do.

Aradia stays up to magically analyze it a bit, trying some assensing and also psychometry. It seems to have some of the same powers and possibilities of the 6WT- both useful as a tool of divination and potentially summoning, but also for a variety of different ritual or artifact purposes. However, the magic behind it seems a bit different, and Aradia feels the closest thing she’s seen to it is the strange magic from the baldies, entities they have now identified as Disians. The team analyzes and speculates about the card for a while more, before tabling it for now and deciding not to use it at the moment, as Heat is the one pushing it and he finally comes down from his evening’s high and loses interest.

They crash out and rest up. Awakening the next day, they find a waiting message from Le Guide (they have decided to start calling him this due to his French connections and to avoid confusion with “The Collector” etc). He apparently has a new lead for them on Vishala, and wants them to get to it soon. However, PPJ has something he feels he needs to deal with first, and gets the team on board to help him do recon on a trafficking org that’s operating out of Englewood Barrens, in order to pay off his debt to Doublewide, the strange underground fixer that he met on his way back from Zecorporatum.

The team mostly agrees to help, with Heat insisting on being paid, but then getting flustered when Otters offers him too much money in a way his pride can’t handle, and eventually settles on knocking off a small portion of his remaining debt to PPJ instead. They prepare to head out as Heat does a matrix look and Otters does a drone flyby. Heat sees very little matrix activity in the Barrens, and the team briefly discusses trying to get a side job to get connectivity up there before Heat realizes that the area is likely an informal sacrifice zone, used as an example of GW’s wrath, as it used to be AZT turf, and the lack of reconstruction and level of destruction there seems intentional.

They decide to just do that job and get out. With a little research on forums, they also find out that there is a large tent city in a park that is the main population center, alongside scattered smaller groups squatting in the ruins, and the only business in the area is a multi-block Knight Errant live fire training facility, that they got on the cheap after GW’s “Reclamation”. There are rumors of people going missing semi-frequently in the Barrens, and some of the darker rumors suggest that KE may be kidnapping stragglers from “live targets”, though this is far from confirmed.

On the drone flyby, Otters sees that the nursing home that the smugglers are using as a base is fairly well-maintained for the area, with boarded up exterior ground floors and a nice looking interior courtyard. However, all the elderly people she see’s there are shockingly spry, moving about with a purpose, seemingly unattended by any staff, though there are people of a variety of ages alongside them from time to time. Worried that some of the rumors of “dark spirits” in the area could suggest a Shedim infiltration, Otters heads back to the safehouse with the VTOL and picks up PPJ, to do some assensing. PPJ masks his aura quite well, luckily, and they do another surveillance pass seemingly undetected amidst the various flyover traffic above the Barrens. PPJ confirms Shedim, partially by spotting that all the natural growth in their immediate area have died, and the verdant appearance is provided by fake potted plants.

Otters is also able to spot some healing surgical scars on various of the people they observe, including what look to be major organ and limb swaps and the like. The team speculates that Shedim would be perfect for an organ-legging group as allies/employees, as they can keep the bodies “alive” past the point of death and rapidly heal wounds, and this gives the Shedim a relatively stable “home” for a time as well. Disturbing! Not wanting to deal with it themselves, they come up with an easier alt plan, and bandy back and forth who to take it to, before eventually settling on utilizing Heat’s connections in Horizon.

As this is a major get, it should boost his contact Hype’s ranking in the corp, which is a nice side benefit, as he will now owe Heat some favors in exchange. They pass along the intel on the facility to Hype, with Heat emphasizing that it getting dealt with is the top most important item, and that the team wants it spun such that it can be used as anti-GW propaganda- his neglect is allowing Shedim and organ traffickers to flourish! This could also explain where all the missing people in the area have been ending up. Hype says to leave it in his capable hands, and seems excited about the opportunity.

With that done, PPJ hopes it will get quickly wrapped up and word will get back to Doublewide so that they’ll be square. Heat also settles up his info debt to Prophetess and the Followers of the Ancient Way for using their Resonance Well at the Temple. He debates feeding them some of the info the team found out about GW and the dangers he poses in his request to reinstantiate Zebulon, but decides against it to how close Prophetess is to the dragon, and instead gives them a detailed update on the political situation at the Seelie Court, which they seem delighted by, as current info on that plane is hard to come by on the matrix.

The team finally calls Le Guide back, and gets the update: following their leads, he had sent spirits searching around town for an overlap of corrupted mana with Dissonance build-up, and found a spot where it looks like Vishala set up after setting up the team and fleeing her previous lab. She seems to have also vacated this locale after something blew up, big, there. He wants them to get down to the rural district of Elbert and check it out. Heat talks the pay up a decent amount, and they do another remote recon.

There is a bit of matrix activity in the immediate area, including a LoneStar presence, who seem to have closed off the long-shut ice rink building where the explosion took place, for further investigation. Otters confirms there is a small LS presence on site, it what looks like a dilapidated farm town, long collapsed for the most part, with a few small businesses and inhabited house clinging on. The team heads over, with Otters sneaking the van around to avoid being spotted by LS. Creep sneaks over the ice rink on foot, PPJ hangs back in the van to do some astral recon, and Aradia and Heat head into town on foot, with fake ID’s and fancy duds on, posing as interested investors.

Heat and PPJ confirm Dissonance and toxic magic, with Aradia jumping in to confirm PPJ’s findings- there is also an increasing mana ebb starting at a short distance out from the edge of the ice rink, and strengthening inward. Creep sneaks around the back of the building and starts picking one of the ancient padlocks there. Aradia and Heat chat up the lone worker at the dingy restaurant where they also find two of the cops hogging the coffee and pie. Conning them into believing that they are agents of the owners of various building in town, they insist on seeing the damage for themselves, and one of the cops begrudgingly agrees to take them on a drive through the damaged areas in his cruiser.

He points out the blast marks emitting from the doorway of the ice rink and explains that LS nailed up the plywood over it til they can get some proper arcana-investigators out here to figure out what’s going on in there. He then starts probing them on what building they own exactly, and they disastrously confirm that they are owners of the ice rink, amongst others. He locks the doors and seems thrilled that he’s got them detained, as the cops will now finally have someone to question! They’ve had no luck finding suspects, owners or anything else so far and he seems to think this will look great for him in his report that he managed to corral these two.

Heat protests as the cop starts calling back into base to report their SINs, and then squelches his signal, as well as to keep him from contacting the other LS cops and drones in the vicinity. The cop seems to recognize he’s being hacked, and tases Heat, injuring him, before Aradia casts an agony spell on the cop and Heat starts to draw his gun, at which point the cop tases Heat again, frying his armor. Otters and PPJ reposition with the van and some LOS, with flanking stealth drones, to provide support as needed. Aradia also gets a silence spell going as the cop turns on his sirens and starts racing towards his allies, seeking assistance. The drones start to follow the silent speeding car as the other cops take cover and draw their weapons, detecting that something is wrong.

Meanwhile, Creep has easily cracked the rusted padlock and gained access to the rink. It looks to be in a bad state of disrepair from decades of neglect, before the recent wreckage of the explosion. Creep spots a few toxic spirits milling about the rink in a circular motion, spiraling in towards the center, and trying to grab at something, but coming away empty-handed before spiraling further back out and then trying again. After watching their movements for a while, he thinks he gets a feel for their speed and patterns, and cautiously makes his approach towards the center, presumably helped by his low essence and Gnomish resistance to magic, though he gets a bad, skin-crawly feeling as he closes in.

Outside, Aradia has a bright idea and uses an illusion to make it look like some toxic magic is occurring that could be causing all the chaos in the cop car, which spooks the driver so much that he dives out at full speed. PPJ decides to make his moves as he can the other cops shouting that they will open fire if the car doesn’t stop, as Heat and Aradia duck and brace for impact. PPJ unleashes a targeted anti-gun acid spell, and melts all three of the cops’ weapons, which they frantically drop, not knowing that the limited spell can’t actually harm them. Distracted, they are then all bowled over and sent flying as the speeding car slams into the sideways cop car they were taking cover behind.

Otters uses this opportunity to open up on the half dozen support drones present, distracting them from Heat and Aradia, who, though injured, are managing to escape the wreck, with Aradia turning invisible. Between the Mules and the van, Otters easily accounts for the LS drones, which all seem to be running on local dog-brain mode and can’t deal with a skilled rigger and a massive amount of firepower. Heat debates frying the cops’ electronics to try to save his and Aradia’s SINs but as they may have already been reported decides flight is probably the better option, and he and Aradia head for the van.

Back inside, Creep has got close enough to the center to see that items there are fragments of about a dozen grenades, which look like the custom-fillable kind, all detonated. He cautiously approaches and grabs a few fragments, at which the spirits all turn to him and start to pursue. He flees, chucking the fragments as he goes. Seeing that the spirits chase the fragments, he dashes back to the center and grabs another handful of fragments, tucking a few into his leg compartment as he races for the exit, and throwing more off to the sides to distract the pursuing spirits. He manages to make good his escape, and starts sneaking back towards the van as well. Now they just need to exfiltrate from this super rural area without getting caught!!

Zero Factor

The team goes to report to The Guide while Heat continues to seek info in order to have something with which to pay Prophetess for use of the Resonance Well at the Temple of the Ancient Way. They also report that they are continuing some other investigations with the court detective Ashokasundari Rai for the time being and will keep in touch as able. For the JoM: it was apparently a gift to dragon kind from the Seelie Court, using 2nd World magic, early in the 4th World. Thisbe Sorrel seems to have been involved in the crafting of it, and it seems to have been made using knowledge from the Silver Books, back when they were all collected in one place as “The Seed”. It seems like it is also a hyper-powered artifact version of the ritual used to create the Coimeadai, as it literally holds memories, though Courtiers believe that living storage is better, as it keeps them “fresher”. TD speculates that mere mortal (or even immortal) minds also couldn’t handle the separation, access, and reabsorption of feelings and info on the scale of the JoM, with that ability perhaps limited to dragons.

The JoM also seems to be the origin of the Seelie Court’s “Moonlight Book” which one of the Queen’s courtly retinue uses to hold the history of the Court, recording the Morning Hearings and other happenings. This suggests that if the team is unable to get the JoM, they may be able to access the Book, if they can get ahold of it somehow, to use in the ritual to free Harly instead. TD also reports that there are some rumors of losses over time with stored memories, as if repeatedly splitting parts of your mind may have some costs that the Court hasn’t or doesn’t wish to acknowledge. Also, TD thinks the ritual was similar to the one used to split Harly from Gwyplaine (likely why Thisbe Sorrel said the JoM was necessary to rejoin them), and they believe others could use that same or a similar ritual (more powerful than the Coimeadai one) to effectively make themselves litches, creating living phylacteries that might perpetuate their existence. Worrying!

With that, they’re free to get to work on the next gig for The Collector. They call him up and set up a meet for that evening at the Mt. Olympus club downtown. They arrive and find that he has booked them a private room in the massive central mountain that takes up much of the club, as well as providing support for various tables, small dance floors and mini bars on it’s ledges. They head inside and find TC along with a woman they don’t know, who TC introduces as SIlk, saying that the two of them are working together on this gig. The team goes along, not sensing any danger or deception.

Silk mostly hangs back and watches as TC lays out the mission: the team will head into the Reflecting Pool, a strange crossing place between the Foundation and Resonance space, where they will be able to operatre somewhat normally, as opposed to the Foundation, though magic, drones, etc won’t really work. However TC says that it is intended as an investigative mission, and should not put them in any direct danger, partially due to the strange nature of the Reflecting Pool as a place. He offers a commesurate rate, and Aradia is unable to convince him to increase it, though he is very polite about the fact.

As they are preparing to leave, Silk says she has one more thing, and holds up a heavy decanter, telling them to throw it through a window she points to. With Creep’s encouragement, PPJ does, shattering the window and sending the decanter sailing out into the dance area beyond. Aradia detects that Silk seems to have made some judgement of them as a crew based on this but isn’t sure what, and Otters has to explain as they are exiting that this was a likely a lateral thinking test to see how they would respond- some may have refused, some may have opened the window, some may have asked questions, and that their team has likely been categorized as being useful for certain sorts of tasks based on their response.

Not liking the prospect of being pigeon-holed, the team decide to try to do a really good job in the hopes of showing that they contain multitudes, and call up Chiron, the contact they were given who will help them access the Reflecting Pool, and teach Heat some of his techniques to he can help watch over the rest of the team while they’re under. After getting a location to meet him, they set a time for the next day so they can rest. They head back to the safehouse and crash out. The next morning, they take a leisurely drive out to Front Range to meet Chiron, and find a somewhat paranoid Technomancer awaiting them in a rarely used public forest road parking area with good foliage cover.

He has a multi-van welded together custom RV, and explains that this location is a good spot for a variety of reasons he doesn’t go into, but which Aradia confirms include some astral cover from all the overgrowth in the area. However, there are another pair of higher tech RV’s parked across the small lot, emitting a lot of signals, that Chiron seems concerned about. He asks the team to figure out what’s up with them before they proceed. Otters initially proposes just moving, while PPJ wants to go straight to killing whoever is in the RV’s to get them out of the way, with the encouragement of Lone Wolf. Aradia suggests scaring them off with illusions to get them to relocate.

Creep sneaks up to take a listen, and hears some computer and tech sounds but no conversation, and Aradia and PPJ confirm that there are two people in each RV who seem pretty focused on whatever they are up to. Otters, after doing some signal mapping, figures they have upwards of a dozen drones under their control and a well-defended network, and decides to just try talking to them. She approaches, digitally, and says hello, wondering if they would mind moving on. Aradia jumps on the call to apply some intimidation as well. The riggers who respond, Pitch and Roll, push back a bit but seem very willing to ignore the team if the team leaves them be, claiming they are in the middle of a “drone race”.

Quickly figuring out that they’re doing some kind of smuggling run, the team instead suggests a deal- if Pitch and Roll bring any heat down on the area from the ZDF or others, they will warn the team so Chiron can move his RV, for which Otters offers a token sum. They agree, seemingly eager not to further antagonize Aradia, who has done a good job of spooking them, as well as it not really costing them anything since they already have drones and sensors up. At PPJ’s insistence, the team confirms that there is no metahuman trafficking going on, and once that’s settled they get back to biz.

Chiron turns out to be a bit of a conspiracy theorist/Lone Gunmen type, which Creep bonds with him over a bit. He explains to Heat who arrives late on his moto about how the process will work, and suggests the other team members get comfy on the various bean bag chairs, armchairs, or bunk bed scattered around the custom RV’s interior. They all take up places on (or under in Creep’s case) various objects, and Chiron tells them all they have to do is fall asleep, and the same to return. He starts a countdown, while using Heat’s strange new Silver Book abilities to keep the team all together and push them in.

They awake in what looks like their safehouse, though it seems like non-memorable spaces between more memorable or important points is compressed and vague. So hallways and floors and such take almost not time to traverse, but all the personally relevant signifiers are still there. They emerge from their rooms and find each other a bit altered- it seems like each of them has become somewhat more their “true self” in terms of how the others on the team perceive them. They also find that they are maybe just a bit better at their best skills and a bit worse at their worse ones, so whatever powers this place seems to effect them internally to. Otters also feels like her Software skills may come in useful in shaping or manipulating the reality around them.

Appearance-wise, Creep looks like a dead-eyed 90’s child star, with the kind of threatening stare that would likely lead to a lot of worried parent-teacher conferences. His face lacks a mouth, though a disturbing orifice gapes on the side of his leg, as Otters has always pictured his feeding port. Otters looks almost saint-like, surrounded by small animals, birds, and the like, though with a fixed rictus grin and a hollow interior- outwardly focused with little inside. PPJ has a strange combination of dress, wearing too small “IOND MagicScouts” uniform shorts on his adult body, while also appearing child-like and small while swimming in his overlarge ronin-mage poncho, while a Wolf tail springs from the back of his shorts. He also carries a blood crystal blade, which glows faintly and drips gore. He looks like a dangerous nerd.

Aradia seems the most put-together, as the others all appear to see her as the adult in the room, looking a bit older and more pro, but with a strong “witchy dominatrix” vibe. After taking a moment to kind of mentally deal with all of that, they step out the safehouse door and find themselves emerging into a drive in motel parking lot instead of the expected hallway. The van is there, though it also appears different- Otters mostly highlights the upgrades, while Creep has focused on the seating, and the mages have interpreted it more metaphorically, so it is a strange blend of all of those. They all spend a moment conferring and confirm that they do seem to share a perceptual reality, which it seems they are all contributing to.

Creep and Otters see a sort of caricature of Chiron waving to them from the motel office, so they go over and find him watching an old flatvid cassette about how dragons caused the Blackouts, but he gives them some tips of places around town they could start their investigation. They also have a few spots they think might be useful to take a gander at to get a feel for this odd place. First they head to the Aurora Warrens and the Jester’s Nest, the bar where much of Harlequin’s resistance to GW back in the day was organized. The drive is a little strange- it’s much quicker than expected, and populated mostly with what each of them seem to think of as the main landmarks between the Warrens and the Safehouse, and not much else.

On arriving, they find a large brawl spilling out of the bar, with a dozen or so people on each side as other patrons watch. As they look closer, they see some faces they recognize- some Chrome Domes, some of the Windsor Crew from Detroit, and some others, including some of Harlequin’s “Diamond Eye” gangers that PPJ recognizes from back in the day. Everyone present seems to bear grievous injuries related to how they died in “real life” but all seem to be alternating drinking and fighting. Aradia heads inside and converses with the bartender, who seems to be a representation of Gwynplaine, the mad clown, and who suggests it’s time for a new master in Denver, whatever chaos it may bring!

The brawl outside seems to be between different factions of anti-authoritarians, or to represent such as least. One side seems to want an alliance with some “new power” in town, whatever the cost, and the other side doesn’t want to swap one dictatorship for another. The sides don’t seem entirely analogous to where the individuals involved would necessarily have fallen irl, but the team gets a little more info talking to one of the Candyland Ghouls on the sidelines, whose neck is still leaking from an Order of St George arrow wound. The ghoul seems to be on the “maybe not worth it” side, confirming that he’s concerned what this “new factions” agenda may be.

Next they head to the Paradise Lost mall, once again finding the distance compressed to only “important” points in between, and find it populated by various former teammates and runners, who left the game one way or another- dead, captured or retired. Otters chats to a decapitated Eion Smash in the weapons store, though a talking dog with a brain jar on it’s back (Wat?) warns him to stay out of Eion’s reach and bite range. The stores also seem jumbled around and on top of each other and all odd sizes, as it the team’s collective memory can’t quite agree on which ones matter most and what order they are actually in. They see 3 Card working the bar, Butter wrenching on cars, Aleiron tending to a strip club, and Bubby running the magic shop.

PPJ, delighted to see his spirit friend again, tries to converse but once again finds that he seems to merely be a shallow reflection of the team’s knowledge and thoughts, with some additional info creeping in. Overall, some of the runners (especially Eion) seem excited about the prospect of more sales amidst more violence while other (Wat, Bubby) seem more worried about the consequences to the people of Denver, and also make note that GW has done some good for a lot of populations that are endangered elsewhere- Free Spirits and talking animals being amongst them. They all agree that his neo-feudal, neo-fascist supporters loudly proclaiming his divinity are not helping matters, but don’t trust this new faction in town, though some also view them as generally good for biz- more chaos equals more customers and more jobs.

They head next to the Hub ZDF precinct, having to leave the van and crawl through narrow opening in a massive fortified wall, apparently their feeling of the protected Hub border. They find patrols rushing out the doors, being destroyed by or destroying strange shadow forms, and then respawning inside the precinct house and doing it all again. The shadows seem to be coming from some corp towers not too far distant in the district. They head inside the precinct house, with no resistance or security trying to stop them, and find it populated by some other former contacts, team-mates and the like, including Smoach and Myrryr, Locke, ThinkTank and other investigative types, while various faces they recognize as cops, corpsec, and syndicate and ganger security all repeatedly rush to their deaths “on patrol”.

Conferring with the investigators, TT summons them all to a conference room, where they all slap down large piles of files in front of the team. They are combating this shadowy threat, but can barely keep up, and can’t keep it contained, so need to them to go to the corp offices and find out what’s going on. To help focus their investigation, they share some rumors that MCT got some kinda sweet deal when GW was rebuilding Denver, probably partially to further stick it to Lofwyr and SK when MCT took their top spot on the Corp Court/AAA ranking. They suggest starting there. The team also all see that the investigators have personal files on all the team members, though disappointingly they only seem to contain secrets the team members themselves know, not unknown things to themselves.

They head out again, aiming downtown for the corp core. Approaching the MCT office, they find it bristling with defenses physical, magical, and digital0 seemingly their mental representation of their fear of MCT’s famed “Zero Zones”. However, as with the precinct house, they once again just easily walk inside with no resistance. They see the personnel here all stick to the sides of corridors, walk with their heads down, and avoid eye contact while scurrying around carrying files and data cores and the like in some inscrutable dance. Some have strange devices screwed to their skulls, and all seem to have strange wispy shadows trailing them. As the team makes their way up the office tower, they realize that all the “employees” here seem to be TM’s that have gone missing- from Seattle, from Detroit, members of the 100 etc.

They see figures they know to have been associated with TM kidnapping and experimentation seemingly acting as managers, PAX, Danielle DeLaMar, Alice Lewis, and some Freedom Network members amongst them. As they ascend to the top floor where DeLaMar oversees the building through a literal glass ceiling, one of the workers briefly joins them in the elevator. PPJ recognizes him as an investigative reports, “AA”, who showed up at a baseball game in Boston and gave the team the original paper copy of the Null document that they have since shared around. He warns them that he heard people in tech department talking about a deal MCT made with the Null Sect, and says nothing good can come from this. He suggests they check out a literal back alley bar behind the office, and then exits, saying more loudly “wrong floor”.

They head to the back alley, and walk down it towards a sign with three glowing neon zeroes with slashes through them – a null set before noticing that the alley has closed behind them. Seeing various of the corp’s “employees” entering he bar with files, they follow, and find a dim, low-ceilinged interior, overhung with a choking dense smoke. They see the corp types dropping off their files at various scattered tables, before dissolving into the floor as the shadows at their backs joins the overhead haze. Around the bar, they see various entities they have associated with the Null Sect Freedom Network members, Blackout Boyz, and the like. As they listen to everyone inside engage in what sounds like inane banter, Aradia is reminded of their surveillance mission on the Auditor, and they are also surprised to see some of the mysterious baldies they’ve been investigating in Denver, as well as the the known TM-experimenter and (former?) MCT employee Bishek Tam, whose name had arisen during their first job in town, the investigation into the lab under the money cow meat processing plant.

They approach Tam, and the bar kind of morphs to put him behind the main bar instead of alone in a corner as they walk up. He welcomes them to Zero Factor, and offers them drinks with a sly wink. Aradia accepts and he summons down some shadow from the floating malaise, which Aradia manages to sleight of hand out of the shot glass without actually consuming, not sure what it would do. While this occurs, the rest of the team sees that whenever files are delivered, whoever they are given to does a frantically rapid series of calculations and notations before the files themselves sink into the tables and vanish. Aradia tries some different tactics- etiquette and intimidation both don’t work, with the Tam representation countering in kind- hinting at what the Null know of the team, and replaying past attacks against them in his eyes, as if reflected as he watches them irl.

Without anything to offer and not wanting to make a deal with the Null anyway, Aradia has a little think, and realizes conning will likely be the best way to get info- she has detected a little of the “real world naivete” that is common to AI’s and e-life forms in general, and since they seem to have scripts for responding to both banter and threats, some careful deception will be most likely to be successful. She embarks on this path, and finally starts getting somewhere. It seems like something called “Project IXION” is a joint venture involving the Null, the Freedom Network, and the Ordo Maximus (possibly though the Black Lodge) to make more realistic anthrodrones for the Null to be able to move about “meatspace” more undetectably. With some further inquiry, Aradia detects that Penelope Beans’ hyper-realistic drone form in Detroit was likely an early test of these realistic anthroforms.

She inquires if this means the Null can do magic, and manages to fish out that they have other allies for that, at which point Tam’s eyes betray the barest glance in the direction of the baldies. Aradia “buys” drinks for the house, and the team heads over to that table, with the table turning into a dim corner booth with non-specific anti-surveillance gear as they approach, seemingly their collective vibe for meeting an info source at a dive bar. They slide into the expanded booth, and begin conversing. Aradia quickly determines that this bar itself is some kind of Null construct inserted into the Reflecting Pool, whether by the Null or by the team’s knowledge of Null activities or both. However, it doesn’t seem like even with all the manatech flowing around town that the Null have figured out how to do magic (though Vishala’s corruption grenades are likely one of the payments MCT is offering them).

These figures seems to instead represent a third party, the magic power involved, and once again detecting a faint flick of the eyes during a conversation full of lies and elisions, Aradia notices them glance at PPJ and determines that the magic portion of this allied trio are from the plane of Dis, where PPJ spent some of his mysterious time away, and performed strange surgeries on others of “The Returned”, before escaping (or being let loose?) through Kadath. However, these seem to be representations of Disians, rather than Disians themselves, and Aradia repeatedly bumps against the limits of what the Null (or the team) actually know about them. She does however confirm that what MCT is getting from the deal is essentially the quantum computing-like capabilities of the Null, which MCT apparently needs to power the Disian’s “devices”. Knowing that the baldies have access to previously unseen magic, and that they are engaged in metahuman trafficking into Denver, the team is pretty worried about what this may mean…

Feeling they’ve got enough info for now, and noticing Null vines starting to grow through the floor and entwine their legs, they decide it’s time to exit, and beat a hasty retreat to the safehouse, finding themselves once again surprisingly unopposed, though they feel the Null were starting to rapidly catch on to their conning strategies towards the end of their convo. They have a brief confusing time before figuring out how to exit the trick alley that the bar is in, and then a quick walk and drive back to the safehouse, where they easily fall asleep (glad Heat isn’t along with his insomnia as Chiron told them they all needed to sleep at the same time), and awaken in Chiron’s van, still in the forested mountain parkland in Front Range. The smugglers have left with no trouble, seemingly, and it looks like they successfully got the paydata the Johnsons wanted, but what does this all mean for the future of Denver….

Revenge is a Dish Best Researched Extensively
7/29/2024, 9:59:42 PM
Runners
Creep
Otters aka Roadkill
Heat
Aradia

Pepperjack decides to spend some solo time working on new magical abilities. Creep stays behind to hang out in Roseanne and binge Party of 5 flatbeds. The rest of the team head south to Co. Springs to go to a hard-scrabble biker bar in the former CAS sector, where Heat’s show it taking place, another Ceol Productions showcase, though Johnny Silver is loathe to spill on who the surprise headliner is. Aradia makes contact with the mysterious “George” who was interested in The Order of the Garter, and invites him to meet her there. He seems a little archaic in his speech patterns but not overtly threatening.

Upon arrival, the team finds a mostly older crowd, a mix of go-ganger types and aging rockers, wearing a lot of vintage 50’s and 60’s era band merch. A few members of Glock .50 open, doing some solo and combined performances, with Heat up second. He plays his new Fae-inspired composition, but also reads the crowd and drops some old Shield Wall breakdowns in, for the many fans of the “classics” in attendance. He gets a burst of inspiration, and sees a small Data Sprite of Music trying to get his attention.

He finds himself newly able to focus on multiple things at once, and accepts a packet from the Music Sprite, and inserts it into his performance. It seems like the final component that pushes it over the edge into something sublime, and he as usual also has a chaotic visual feed accompanying him, showing sped up scenes of Seelie forests and the like. He is steaming from overclocking his cyberlimbs for his performance, and feels that he really got the crowd on his side, though due to his hyper-speed style, many seem surprised at the short set time.

He also catches a brief flash of a glowing neon outlined figure with wild flyaway hair in his visual feed, but no-one else seems to notice it, and he finds that it didn’t get recorded on his internals. He looks in the Resonance and sees that it seems somehow connected to the Music Sprite that showed up. Otters checks the RCC for any attempted intrusions but gives the all clear. Up last in the surprise event: a rare live appearance of the legendary guitarist Blitz (formerly of Blitzkrieg) accompanying Marli Bremerton, a notoriously reclusive Seattle-based synthwave composer, who wears a full body covering, and leaves the stage performance to her younger accompanist, who does a magical lights show along to the music.

This seems like what most of the old heads in the crowd came for, and people are amped. Heat talks to them after the show, and Althea, the younger mage, explains she is Marli’s niece/publicist/manager/caretaker, and that Marli doesn’t like to speak to “outsiders” but gives Heat a physical address in Seattle if he wants to mail them any demos, or any old hard copy media recordings he finds, as Marli is apparently a serious collector. Blitz also offers Heat some career help and mentorship, if he ever finds himself seeking a different style than Johnny Silver, though he admits he has less resources, he also has less people looking to kill him.

Meanwhile, Aradia has met up with George, who is dressed in a medieval/modern fashion hybrid, that looks manufactured and not homemade. They converse for a while, and Aradia quickly determines that he is a member of the Order of St. George, so treads cautiously. He does legitimately seem interested in her group, but is also pretty free, though quiet, in discussing his hatred of dragons and their persecution of metahumanity. He seems to believe that TOoTG could be a good way to get info and keep an eye on Denver without exposing themselves as dragon-hunters, and that TOoSG could act as additional security and assistance if TOoTG wishes to expand beyond Denver in the future.

Aradia says she will take the offer to the next group meeting, but says that regardless it’s good to have another friendly contact, since they both seem opposed to Ghostwalker’s workings here, even if their potential alliance goes no further. She takes her leave, and the group head back to the safehouse, with Heat reporting in on his strange visual sighting. No-one recognizes the description, and it didn’t seem like a threat so….. ?

They take the next few days off so Heat can Submerge again with the Nexus, gaining new TM powers, and Aradia can do another group initiation with TOoTG, gaining the ability to conceal her spells as well as her magic. She also discusses the offer of TOoSG, which most of the group rejects out of hand, not wanting to go against a dragon, especially since GW taking over Denver has been good for at least some of them, and ensured some consistency in legality, protection, enforcement, etc between all the sectors.

Alli also helps Aradia spread the word about the risk of Null infiltration of rental bots and bods, for those members who either themselves do or know someone who does that kind of work, or works in those environs. The group also seems excited about the possibility of setting up work trips to the Seelie Court, so Aradia proceeds with that, making a deal between Arcane on this end to open portals for pay, and Lord Blackbane on the other end to open the return portals to Arcane’s shop, in exchange for a cut of the profit in some form, as well as the ability to personally visit the material plane more easily. Boutrous the Boisterous will handle security for the bookings on the far end, which Blackbane and his manor staff will help TOoTG members manage logistically.

With that all done, the team gets back into looking into Vishala and her betrayal. They pay for some intel on JackPoint, and get a list of local MCT facilities, including subsidiaries, as well as some known employees at each location. Some of the spots seems pretty creepy, including one Zero Zone that only has armed drone transit in and out. They do locate Vishala’s old boss, who is in town as promised, working at MCT’s downtown local HQ tower. They decide to do a little more background work before approaching him, and check out some of the sites they had raided for Vishala, as well as the lab she had been running.

They find the lab closed, with for rent signs up. However, they do notice some similar astral and Dissonance contamination there as at the first site they raided for her, and also remember they stole a powerful summoning focus for her from the ZDF vault, and worry what she may have planned. They track down some of the former guards at the facility, who they remember worked through a temp agency. By searching MeFeeds and other public socials and job boards, they track a few of them down to a working class bar called Hardpan near an algae and other plant protein processing plant.

The smell of the place is something else, but they hold it together. Otters stays in the van, ready to intervene as needed, and Aradia disguises herself and Heat in more appropriate workwear getup, as Creep sneaks in and keeps an eye from a corner. Aradia and Heat approach the guards, playing at a “oh hey surprise seeing you here!” The guards seems to agree they are owed some drinks after the team’s escalation of the facility defense left a number of their former coworkers dead.

Heat and Aradia agree, and suggest they could make a larger donation to a funeral and survivors fund, as well as throwing in to help cover their current period of unemployment, in exchange for some info. After some skillful talking from both of them, and some hemming and hawing from the men, they agree. Aradia sets up a silence barrier, and Heat jams any comms in the booth. The mean relate what they know- they were kept on for a few months, Vishala was a hard charger but otherwise decent, and they were let go with little notice after helping move all the boxed up lab equipment out.

They say there were a half dozen scientists, who they never saw leave or arrive at the facility, but who were friendly enough, though their comm numbers no longer connect. They also say they were warned sometimes not to bring anything magical or anything high tech (on different days each) near the second floor sealed lab, as some sort of astral or matrix “corruption” might interfere. After a little more haggling, they agree to let Aradia do a surface mind probe to get good images of the 6 scientists, which Aradia also mentally shares with Heat. The guards also remember overhearing Vishala on a comm call seeking info on some secret project at MCT, though they didn’t hear the name.

Having paid well for the info and the guard’s silence, the team leaves, though Heat sets ups a safe word in case they ever end up on opposite ends of the same job down the line, which the guards seem to appreciate. The team heads out and decides to go after the MCT boss next. Aradia calls up Carol Staples in Chicago (Maggie Goldberg’s former assistant and successor there), and after assuring her that the team doesn’t have anything nefarious planned, gets a meeting set for the next day with Shinju Sho.

The team rests and then heads to the Hub for the meeting. Otters stays outside, in case MCT security recognizes her, since she doesn’t know how she really left things with them and has missing time as well as memories from that period. The rest head in, checking their weapons as they go through an intense sec scan. They go about 2/3rds up the 30 story building, to the floor where Sho has his office, atop the HermeTech subsidiary wing, which focusses on providing mundanes training to counter mages- teaching security guards to notice mental manipulation or astral forms passing through them or similar.

They sit down, and Aradia launches into an explanation of what they are seeking. Creep and Heat both notice that Sho’s desk is a bit too big and prestigious for his office, almost as if he has been demoted and brought his old furniture with him. Sho makes a big production of rejecting their request to share info on past employees or other internal matters, but turns his chair slightly to obscure view of him from the hallway and quickly scrawls a note, which he palms to Aradia as they shake hands as the team rises to exit.

Back outside, Aradia gets a text from an unregistered number, providing a nearby location and time, and reading the note, sees that it says “I am open to an exchange of information and will be in touch.” They head over to the cabaret bar in the text, and get a booth and wait for a bit with some drinks. After a short while, they see Sho walk in with 2 corpsec types bodyguarding, but he says something to them and they hang back by the entrance. He makes quick eyes at the team as he walks through the bar to the stairs to the second level.

They wait a moment, then subtly follow, to avoid attracting the guards attention. As they reach the second floor, they see a private elevator door closing, ascending to one of the rental units behind the face of the clock tower above. They follow when the elevator is available again, and enter to find Sho just sitting down in a nicely appointed loft, with two gun drones pointing at them. He smoothly apologizes for the precaution but makes not move to dismiss them. Heat takes the initiative and smoothly approaches the low table and sits across from Sho, pouring himself a drink. They enter into negotiation, with Sho proving quite canny. Heat tries some different approaches, but the only option Sho will take is the return of all project data recovered from Vishala and their research to MCT, and only to MCT.

Heat skillfully lies that this will be done, planning to work with the Guide later to choose what info to send back, and Sho seems to accept this. He shares his experience with Vishala- she was a skilled and manipulative corp climber, not cruel but very willing to throw anyone who got in her way under the bus. MCT has been unable to locate her since she fled their employ and then left the PCC, with their “metahuman resources” teams all turning up empty-handed. He suggests this means she is either still working under deep cover for either MCT or the PCC, or that she has found a new patron and protector.

He explains that she had been tasked with recreating some experiments AZT had undertaken at a leyline confluence in Antarctica to open stable metaplanar portals. Checking timelines, the team thinks this AZT facility must have been breached at some point, as the timeline also checks out with when ARES began many of their portal weapon experiments. Vishala seemed like she was on a good track, filing early promising reports, before her reports started turning to corpspeak and buzzwords with no actual data.

As she already had a rep for getting opponents demoted, no-one wanted to take Sho’s concerns seriously since she hadn’t crossed into behavior that would fully justify an investigation, but she must have sensed his suspicion, as she fled to the PCC, taking the project data with her. She also seems to have wiped her personal records, though he never knew her to have friends or family. The team infers that Sho was also punished for this, explaining his demotion, and nice but too large desk. This seems to be the crux of his willingness to negotiate with them.

Heat tries to push him for more but Sho says the deal was the deal, and suggests they leave. He and Heat stare each other down while they finish their drinks and then the team leaves, with Sho giving them a hard stare but saying no more. With this, they think they’ve probably got enough to satisfy the Guide, and they don’t feel ready for a Foundation dive against MCT or the PCC, even with Heat’s new TM abilities to bring the team along more efficiently. But Heat wants to try one more thing, and calls up Prophetess at the GW TM “cult” The Followers of the Ancient Way.

She is excited to hear from him and asks if he is ready to join, but he demurs, saying he wishes to sample their Temple’s Resonance Well first. She agrees, after he offers to pay them with a secret from the Deep Resonance. He once again gets blindfolded and transported to the site in a jammed drone. He enters the Well smoothly and easily, and through a test of strength of Ego manages to overcome the Event Horizon, and travel forth into the Deep Resonance. It seems more composed of vibes and energy than the visual representations he is familiar with from the astral plane, and he summons a data sprite to help him navigate.

They spend some time searching but eventually locate the Endless Archive, and enter the endless halls and stacks and shelves of that place- a record of everything ever made digital, supposedly. Heat approaches one of the Sprites tending the place, but doesn’t understand the local customs, so has to fumble through an exchange with his Sprite translating. He eventually makes a satisfactory offering of previously offline info (everything the team learned about GW in the Seelie Court), and the librarian Sprite heads off to seek what he requested: anything they have on Vishala.

While they wait, Heat witnesses a fairly terrifying attack from Null Sect vines on the doors of the library, though they hold, for now. The Sprite eventually returns with two documents- one explains the work she was doing at MCT, though not why, and the other some of what she’s been up to since. It seems she pivoted secretly from opening large portals to opening multiple micro-portals at once, allowing mana transfer without full access. She also began attempting to replicate this in the matrix, seemingly recognizing some of the dualities present, opening micro-burst Dissonance connections inside of Hosts and the like to see what would happen.

It seems she decided to go freelance with these innovations, and has since arriving in Denver expanded her experiments into weaponizing these technologies, aiming to make mana corruption grenades and matrix corruption programs that would be easy to use, and quite valuable. It seems she has been selling some of her breakthroughs back to MCT, but is not officially working for them, and also does indeed have a new backer, though the documents do not state who.

Heat returns, and the team speculates who the backer could be, ruling out GW, who would be unlikely to want to pollute astral space, given all of his restorative work. With no other obvious answers in sight or places to search, they plan to share this info with the Guide, close out the job, and hope he can use it to figure out their next steps.

OC tells them he will leave it up to the team how they want to handle this but that he would be pretty worried about whatever info PPJ may be passing along to whatever power grants the crystal it’s abilities, and since PPJ has reaffirmed he is not willing to give up magical power to remove it, this is a bit of a dilemma.

OC fills them in on his speculations about the BL’s interest in Hansu’s Prophecies 1 and 2, and what he thinks they mean. He believes that the BL has interpreted them as a guide on how to dump a bunch of mana from the Seelie Court into the material plane- the exact type of scenario that the conservatives at Court fearmonger about. OC isn’t sure this is possible or true, but believes the BL is seeking to enact this and to control the power flux that results. However, he also believes that this influx could be disastrous, as raising the mana level suddenly like that could rapidly accelerate the coming of the Scourge and the Blood Eclipse.

He tips them off to the ID of the “missing prisoner” from Prophecy 1, who he got tipped off to by some Rogue Lodge informants, but says he doesn’t know where she is, and could use the team’s help finding her- she may be the key to resolving the whole thing. They agree to assist, and he also warns them that the BL may well be behind PPJ’s mystery crystal, since they were behind his crystal spine for sure. PPJ recalls that the Tan Robes used another crystal spine to attune to his and listen in when he was kidnapped into Temple of Terror on the Crystalline Entity island in Boston, and doesn’t know what this means.

He winks at Creep while quietly offering “anything” in return for his magical power returning, and is disturbed when he feels a voice inside him ask “ANYTHING?”. He responds by thinking “within reason”, and then hears an animal yelp of pain followed by an enraged growl, and finds himself being guided a little more towards anger and violence by Wolf as his power returns. But what does it mean…. The team have a heated discussion, with PPJ explaining further how he thinks the crystal works- it seems to only know what it actively observes and doesn’t seem to always be observing, and seems to need to request deeper access (to memories, actions, etc). But he does warn them that something weird happened with Wolf, which disturbs OC, as Wolf has been generally becoming more pack-oriented and calmer over time,

Heat starts a quick vote of whether they should kill PPJ as a liability, but includes a “maybe” option. PPJ obviously votes no, with Creep and Otters on maybe, and Heat on yes with Aradia undecided so Heat says “no hard feelings” and suggests they move on. PPJ reminds everyone that Heat is a liability with his Horizon ties, and Aradia sends a private message to Otters and Creep asking if the three of them should just split off, before remembering that Creep is a strong breeze away from death at any moment and Otters has her own strange liabilities. They all decide to keep working together to strengthen their bonds again.

They take their leave from OC and the Labyrinth, and meet up with Niall to travel out. He once again takes them out through the Passage Graves under the castle, and they walk a surprisingly smooth half day’s journey through the Broceliande woods, noticing various things slightly off their path though nothing interferes with them. Aradia examines a magical ring that Niall wears, and see that is a powerful item focussed on truth and travel, which may explain the ease of their journey.

He explains that they are headed to one of the castles of the Wild Fae, who are acting as a safe middle ground for a meeting with the Unseelie, as they won’t fully trust the team given their affiliation with a Court faction. They eventually reach a small fae village, overlooking a lake with ruined castle on an island in the middle. Creep takes a close look and jumps in the water, and figures out there’s a big illusion going on. Niall says he better take the lead, and guides them all down a ramp to the upside down reflection of an intact castle in the “water”, which thought illusory, seems capable of drowning them if they lose their hold.

Luckily they all manage to make it down with his guidance, and are ushered into the castle by a gatekeeper with a halberd. Niall takes them through, and they see that although the castle is attended, it seems somewhat underoccuppied at the moment. They reach their assigned meeting chamber and Otters and a few others have to keep their eyes closed or avoid all windows and portals, as they see both upside down fish and rightside up clouds outside. Aina Dupree, the Unseelie’s newish Guide waits inside. They begin the meeting by updating her on Harlequin’s status, and showing off how much they’ve figured out, earning her grudging respect, and a thanks to Niall for not wasting her time.

After explaining their plan and it’s problems, she says she should be able to manage Gwynplaine, but that and sneaking into the Castle may be the limits of her abilities to directly assist. She wishes them luck with Celedyr, who she doesn’t seem to think highly of, and tells them to stay in touch. She asks if any of them would have a boon of her for their service, and after confirming that PPJ can’t keep his full power while removing the crystal or blocking it in some way, they can’t really think of any other good ones, so they chat a bit about the Cornerstone, with Aradia fascinated to hear that it seems that possibly both Castle and Cornerstone have had a sort of magical bootstrapping to sapience, similar to an AI, after it was imbued with so much strange magic.

Aina shares what else she knows about it’s uses and abilities and they agree to stay in touch. Lacking any other pressing needs, Otters asks for a spell to make her a bit better at rigging, which Aina quickens to make it semi-permanent. They take their leave, and after a short debate, decide to return to Denver to report in on their progress thus far, and then return to the Mile High Realm later to do the second portion of their investigation there. Niall confirms their destination, and it seems like they will actually be returning to the same mountainous Wild Fae community that most of the departed from, in Castle Rock.

Niall makes a portal easily, and confirms that this is not a thing Aradia can learn, at least not without spirit assistance or a great ritual, which makes Creep and the others wonder how exactly Arcane manages it with such ease, and what this implies about who or what he is. Regardless, they step through the portal and find the same villagers they left the van with, awaiting payment and in a hurry to get the team out of there and on their way. Heat tries to charm them with one of his new sampled production from the Court, but they seem to dislike Courtly music, and also don’t seem to sell any souvenirs or knick-knacks, so the team gets on the road.

They are relieved that it seems the Shadow Cloaks worked and outside of some time distortion from the astral travel they seem to have been gone about as long as they thought, around a week. Their cloaks do seem to have degraded from the use though, with some crumbling entirely. Otters and Heat are thrilled to be back onto the matrix, and dive in. They get some assorted news updates, the most locally pressing of is that Perri discovered that the Null Sect seem to have infiltrated a Spinrad bot rental service that lets people telecommute to important meetings in Denver, suggesting that there may be some more embodied Null around.

Heat makes a few calls, checking in first with Johnny Silver about the state of the Shadow Chapters locally. JS explains that though the rep came down from Seattle as promised, there’s another ShadChaps rep in from Boston, who seems more “traditional” but is a slick talker. Heat asks how to be involved and JS tells him it would be best for him to actually stay out of it, due to his Horizon ties having been exposed in the infighting. Heat happily agrees, and asks JS if he can get on a show coming up, and JS offers him a slot that night, which Heat accepts, frantically starting to try to figure out some compositions from his various samples and notes from the Court.

He also calls Perri, and they set a digital meet to debrief, in one of the Nexus’ secure rooms. The team enters, and gives Perri the whole Ghostwalker debrief, including their latest intel that he is thought to possess a Silver Book, according to Aina. Perri shares that she has heard tell of certain powerful ancient individuals who had the ability to awaken sapience and/or magical ability in their chosen ones, and wonders if GW has either figured out how to or is promising to do the same for TM abilities, which could help explain his rapidly growing cult. She thanks them for the info, and sends Heat a data package with an updated Hitchhiker program that she and Cloudmaster/the Collector decrypted from their Silver Book.

She explains that this will allow non-matrix-y teammates to use a mental projection of their real like skills and abilities on Foundation dives, so long as Heat runs it all through his simulated cyberdeck. This is exciting news, as it will make the other team members a lot more useful on those missions, should they come across the need, and is a major breakthrough. She tells them they’ll need a lot of processing power to make it all work, but the Nexus is happy to provide in exchange for reports back on results. The team agrees, and heads back to the safehouse after getting the all clear from Cat, who has swept the building and unit thoroughly in their absence, after the corp hit squad gained access to their neighbor.

Heat decides to stay in and work on compositions, while the rest of the team calls Niki the Gardener back, who had left them a somewhat frantic message. They determine that she is just stressed about her rep (As well as the attempt on her life) after Vishala’s betrayal, and forgive her, this once, which she is thrilled to hear. She updated Otters on her investigation into ANIMUS, which has brought her to a burned-down lab and some strange rumors in the Lisbon sprawl outskirts, and says she’ll keep digging. For now, she tells them the The Guide reached out, hoping they were available for some work and some revenge.

They accept, and call the Guide back directly, setting up a meeting at a spot of his choosing in Broomfield after some initial scouting confirms it looks safe. They head there and have a thankfully straight-forward and threat-free meet, accepting a job to look into what Vishala was really up to, on the path to tracking her down for some appropriate revenge. They call Niki back, and she gives them the little additional intel she has: Vishala used to work for the PCC, and MCT before that, and there may be some bad blood there. Otherwise, she is what she seems- a put together femme fatale type with limited implants and no known magic or TM abilities.

They next call up the Collector, and set up another meeting at the Nexus (where he goes by Cloudmaster) in a secure room. After confirming that they are after Vishala for intel first and revenge second, and are willing to share some of that intel with him, and will do a job for him next, he shares his info. Vishala fled MCT to the PCC, claiming she needed protection from her former employers, but bringing some valuable skills and data with her, or so his former superiors in PCC intelligence thought. He worked adjacently to her but never directly with her, and was meant to be one of her points of contact in Denver, but she dropped off the grid and slipped her handlers almost as soon as she arrived.

This was meant to be her first foreign assignment after she spent a while proving herself, and as a metaplanar phenomena expert she was tasked with investigating all the rift/rip/portal weirdness going on in Denver. The Collector is very interested to hear what exactly she uncovered, if the team should find out, and why she went rogue/where her true loyalties lie. He passes a long the name of her former supervisor at MCT, if they need further leads, and wishes them luck. With this info, they ruminate a while on what they know of her and what next best steps will be based on the experiments that had seen her interested in.

But with Heat having a show coming up tonight, they decide to party and chill, with Creep choosing to stay in his Roseanne bot and watch trids while the rest attend.

Just One More Thing...
