However, she’s been missing since GW turned on the Nexus a few years back.

She calls Heat back, and says she goes by “Prophetess” now. She invites him to come see what they’re up to, and promises he can leave after if he doesn’t wish to join, though she insists he come alone without knowing where he’s going, as she doesn’t trust outsider, especially given the past activities of some on his team. Otters is concerned that anyone else knows about “that”, but Heat agrees to go along with it, though he is spooked, and makes the team promise to follow him and make sure he doesn’t disappear. PPJ stays behind to keep an on the hotel (which he considers the real job) telling Heat it was nice knowing him.

The next day, Heat gets a message with a pickup intersection and heads over, with Otters, Creep and Aradia stealthily trying to follow in the van. Unfortunately someone spots them and disables them with a massive Opium Den spell (TD is mad they stayed in the tub and missed it), and manages to clean up the astral sufficiently before they come to that Aradia can’t magically track the caster. Also, Heat is gone and not showing up on the TacNet.

Heat has been blind-folded, bundled into a Faraday’ed caged VTOL, and flown off. He has no idea where he is, but emerges in a battle-scarred ruin, possibly underground, with a bunch of TM’s wearing comfortable clothes seemingly working on clearing, cleaning and rebuilding. He is greeted by an older woman who introduces herself as Prophetess, and says she’ll give Heat a minute, which he quickly needs, as he detectds the cleanest, most stable Resonance Well he’s ever seen. Even his initial skepticism of this “cult” fades as he can’t find any trace of Dissonance or deceit- just pure, clean, powerful Resonance energy.

He doesn’t want to risk diving or trying to sneak a message out, and converses with Prophetess for a bit- she asks him to join the Temple of the Old Way, and though he is a bit tempted, he says he wants to think about it, and she says she won’t force him. He asks after their mission, and she says she must be a bit vague if he won’t join, but confirms that they have a powerful patron (“the Prophet of the Seed”) who ensures their safety and protection in return for them protecting his digital assets and ensuring their security. She says they will go more public once they finish rebuilding the Temple, and learn more secrets of “the Seed”, which perks up Heat’s interest further.

Heat asks to leave and is freely allowed to, with a mask in a VTOL again. ViralVixen sees him out, and confirms that she wants to be here, asking Heat why he’d want to keep risking his life out on the streets when he could be safe and nurtured here, with powerful Resonance stable and available at his fingertips. Once again slightly tempted, Heat still leaves. He gets dropped off near where he was picked up, still with no idea where he was, and reports in. The team pieces together that he was likely at one of the old abandoned Nexus sites, as they had heard rumors of a stable Resonance Well that Nexus TM’s called “the Temple”.

Calling it into Perri, she confirms that this may be the old Nexus Alpha site, and that the patron is almost surely GW, so the team is reassured that at least the target of their planned hit doesn’t have a massive TM force at his beck and call. Perri seems to consider the bonus part of the job done for now, and pays the extra fee as promised. The team stocks up on explosives and the like, and pays Silver Streak to smuggle their illicit gear into the Hub again. They set up on the 9th floor, with PPJ locking himself in the bathroom and going astral. Otters picks up a new stealth VTOL from Dracula, and Heat buys a squirrel suit to match Creep’s.

Aradia uses a new spell to cast a silence bubble over the ceiling, as Otters crashes a cheap rigged drone into the outside of the building, causing a large stunt explosion that’s visually impressive but not damaging. Creep blows the ceiling, which goes to plan, dropping the ceiling while the distraction explosion outside explains any errant noise or rumbling. The team is surprised as 2 stony-skinned large gargoyle-like entities crash through into the room where the team is waiting to breach, and everyone prepares to fight!

Blimp Heist

With Creep and PPJ joining the team onsite via remote control ride on Heat’s moto, Creep swaps out with Aradia, taking over the invisibility and levitation spells, and slowly ascending, with Otters racing Heat (in the VTOL) and the HERF roto-drone up to match at the last minute. The team is horrified as the opposition uses this chance and nearly destroys the van, and Aradia races Creep back up to magnet to the blimp after a stimpatch gets him back up.

Heat uses a grenade to blow a hole in the side of the blimp, which is descending faster, especially after the blimp gunners shoot out their tether cable (across the border) which seemingly forces the ZDF sniper team’s hands, who open fire on the blimp’s gas envelopes, broadcasting an announcement about an anti-smuggling action. Creep crawls in the hole, gets a quick look inside, shoots at one of the gunners, and then is quickly KO’ed again and kicked out of the side of the blimp.

Heat quickly dives after him, after the gunners also destroy the VTOL in midair (with Otters still out). Heat dives for Creep, firing a desperate final grenade into the blimp. The two sams jump free and everyone is free faaaaaalling. Heat really focuses and manages to deduce some sky-diving from first principles, and slams into Creep, catching hold of him and managing to barely hang on as Creep’s chute auto-deploys. Heat rotates to take the hit as they land, as Creep is nearly dead, and Otters scoops them up in the van. The ZDF tells them to clear the area, seemingly non-plussed as to how well their plan went off. The team heads back to the safehouse, with Heat really wanting to race off and seek out the other blimps before they can move or change security, but the team needs to rest.

PPJ and Creep almost get in another fight, halted by Creep passing out again from stimpatch comedown. They decide to call it a night and rest up, though Heat does some research as he can’t sleep. Reviewing the footage Creep got inside the blimp, he sees a rigger (unconscious or dead, likely from biofeedback), a mage (seemingly killed in the grenade and/or crash) and the two street sam gunners. There’s also a ton of weird electrical equipment, and some slots that look like they could hold manavoltaic rods, which are missing. There’s also what looks like a modded version of the SpriteLok boxes they saw in Detroit.

When everyone else wakes up, they take a look as well, and Creep confirms this looks like almost an electrical substation worth of equipment, but with some weird manatech components he can’t quite ID. PPJ goes off to astrally scout the blimp in Front Range, and finds a busy Uni campus, a mana-shielded blimp hovering overheard, plugged into some more large equipment that he doesn’t recognize when he briefly manifests. Returning to his bod, he describes it to Creep and the others, who confirm is it more electrical stuff- as a research campus, they probably have major power needs, and also are in a relatively isolated area. Otters and Heat also piece together that there is an old smuggler layover with a power and recharging tap into the Oceti Sakowin grid, which seems to be where the blimp near the border was tethered.

So all the blimps seem to be hooking up to “secret” or high-powered but isolated electrical systems, that are likely not under Shiawase’s control yet, as they slowly move to take over the grid. Otters also spots that the gear that the blimp crew was using is a combo of MCT and Renraku stuff, all recent model years and good quality, but fairly anonymized by virtue of wide use. PPJ also says the mage was similarly anonymous- classic Hermetic style, generic elemental spirits, little to distinguish. Aradia splits off to do her initiation with her order. The rest of the team heads over to Front Range, and finds a ZDF interdiction in place, with a “shelter in place” order on campus.

Heat approaches, fast talks past the guard trying to brush him off, and then manages to socially over-power the onsite negotiator, even after they recognize the team as the crew from the previous day, when they kinda blew it and nearly contributed to an international incident. Good thing Heat is good at this stuff! They agree to let the team take a shot at getting aboard, after Heat promises no explosion or crashes on campus. Unfortunately, the team hadn’t been counting on the ZDF to go along with this, so Creep had already snuck onto site and started scaling the invisible tether.

He quickly gets spotted by the blimp crew again once he’s ascended above any nearby objects, and he gets shot again before retreating. The blimp shoots off it’s tether again and flees, with ZDF spirits giving chase, quickly learning to attack from above, while ZDF gunners target enemy spirits that emerge before they switch to astral. Otters sends Morticia to stealthily follow and try to get any intel at all. The rest of the team takes public transit into the Hub, and finds that the 3rd blimp has already crashed in the big sports stadium. Thinking quickly, PPJ does some obnoxious distraction while Creep scouts it out and TD and Heat get into disguise and Heat does some digital forgery.

Posing as insurance adjusters, Heat and TD bluff their way in, agreeing to ZDF escort as long as they can assess the damage. They carefully use these distractions to pull ZDF guards and spirits away from Creep as he sneaks into the crashed blimp and looks around- similar, though subtly different, to the other one. Unfortunately fully powered down either intentionally or from the crash, so nowhere good to place a data tap, so he just cuts the head off the rigger and takes it out with him. He hides in a dumpster and scrapes as much flesh and bone as he can off the headware, which is looking gnarly but at least not a full head to carry back to the safehouse on the train.

TD and Heat manage to make their exit, but burn the SINs they used. PPJ just leaves after running out of conversation, he was also providing useful tips to Heat about how to talk to some of the ZDF spirits about manasphere stuff. Otters remains out in Front Range watching via Morticia as the ZDF spirits bring down the blimp short of the PCC border, and sees some large border patrol VTOL’s approaching. The team call The Collector to ask if this is a PCC/NAN opp, which he says it isn’t, as far as he knows, but he appreciates the intel.

Blackouts and Blimps
4/30/2024, 12:53:34 PM
Runners
The Detective
Otters aka Roadkill
Heat
Aradia

After some quick discussion, the team agrees to meet up with Peregrine and talk about looking into some strange blackouts which occurred the previous night. He tells them to meet him at a Hub club, and although everything on the way to the Hub seems to be powered and working, there’s a stressed out mood in the air as rumors spread. Aradia makes some calls, and gets ahold of some contacts in the Warrens who report that they’re on backup generators as the power is still out there. She also makes plans to initiate soon with members of her new Order. PPJ is still sorting through some feelings and figuring out his new “ronin” identity, so plans to sit this one out, not having the same traumatic Blackout response as some of the Detroit survivors. Creep goes the opposite route and stays behind to hide in a couch and fortify the safehouse.

Otters, taking precautions, stocks up on batteries on the way, finding the shelves a bit depleted as paranoia spreads. They find Peregrine waiting at the club, concealed in a booth inside an AR/trid whirlpool, while the stressed out owner berates staff about their “Blackout plan” in case things gets worse. It’s early enough in the day that there aren’t many patrons, and Otters hangs back to keep an eye out for trouble, clocking most of the club’s attendees as plain clothes security (whether for Peregrine or the club). Peregrine explains he’s been scratching the itch to travel since Detroit but has been in and out of Denver, amongst other places, and that he wants to know the who/what/why/how of the current blackouts and end them or prevent more from happening. Otter is still unable to figure out what exactly feels mysterious about Peregrine, or what it means.

The team agrees, and Heat as usual over-performs on negotiation, which Peregrine seems to find charming. He offers a bonus if they can keep whatever they uncover out of the news, and explains that he has word that there will be other teams also looking into this (unsurprisingly) so the team doesn’t have full informational control. He gives them two sites that seem good to start investigating, where there are still ongoing effects. They head back to the safehouse and load the van up with drones and mission gear, and head out, with Heat taking his newly tricked out moto (having finally let Otters remote drive it to her mechanic for some essential stealth upgrades).

They head to Archer Heights first- reviewing the team file, they find that this is the metaracist district in which the genocidal Paladin Health Clinic was based, before the old team helped shut that down. The medical facility has since been taken over by Catholic Hospitallers, who still run outreach into the Warrens, and a lot of the “official” Warrens services are provided through pipelines running through this district. They have an uncomfortable moment glancing around the van and seeing that the whole crew on this job is human, and realizing they can probably just blend in amongst the bigots, if they go along a bit. They plan to leave Otters in the van given her lack of skill at deception.

Upon entering, they see that the whole gated community is blacked out still, which locals are using as an excuse to grill out, with kids running around eating melting ice cream. Very Rockwell’s “America”. Aradia notices something is deeply weird and off with local mana space but can’t quite make sense of it, and given many human supremacists aversion to magic as well, doesn’t want to ask around about it here. She instead makes general conversation with a larger group of locals, pretending to be interested in moving to the area given its “unique culture”. She hams it up, going along with assorted levels of bonkers conspiracy theories about what causes the Blackouts (dragons, immortal Elves, aliens, and other more obscure options all being popular).

She does however get some intel about a blimp that was parked, hovering, near the Paladin clinic complex, that noone has seen this morning. It seems like it was lightly camouflaged, so Otters can’t fully determine which model and what mods are on it but narrows it down a bit. Aradia also spots a tiny, barely visible, stealth-coated cable in a high quality image a local teen snapped of it yesterday, and which none of the locals seem to have noticed. Heat has split off on his bike, and follows the cable in the image to a closed and barricaded side entrance to the hospital complex, and after digging around for a bit, finds a snapped tether cable, which looks to have some different filaments running through the center of unusually thick insulation. It also is still running into a freshly bored hole in the side of hospital next to the closed entrance.

Heat heads around front and fails to talk his way in from the main entrance, foiled by some elderly Catholics befuddlement at what he wants. Otters and the rest of the team arrive at the side entrance, stealthily, and continue investigating. Aradia is able to tell that one of the filaments in the cable is Orichalcum, and that this area is the center of the mana ebb she’s been feeling, though it doesn’t help her identify the other odd astral sensations. Otters slips some drones in through the cable hole, and they spot a freight elevator, follow the shaft down, and find one set of doors at the bottom propped slightly open by the thick cable running inside. Following it in, the drones spot what looks like a ruggedized commlink case bolted to a larger power grid panel- it seems this is the control room for the utilities running from this district into the Warrens.

Heat comes back around the side and touches the cable end, using it to hack into the device it is connected to in the basement, and sees that it’s just an off the shelf hardened comm anyone could buy for some off-road/back country style expedition, from an outdoors store. So no good clues there. But it is only running a single program and only has default defenses, so he hacks in. He barely manages to get a copy of the program before the device reboots and wipes itself to factory settings, having detected his intrusion. Analyzing the program, he sees that it seems to be a legal but ultra-specialty program for managing power grids/massive energy flows, and seems to have been left set in a “throttle” setting whenever the cable was cut or torn.

As he reports this, the team sees lights and power start to return in both the Warrens and Archer Heights, and make their way out of the district before anyone can ask any questions, heading for the second site to the south, in Co Springs. Arriving, they find a donut area of power outage, though when asking around, it seems like this was a power surge that blow out a bunch of overloaded small devices and transformers, rather than a lack. the center of the donut is a private school complex the this small, exclusive, primarily working class neighborhood is centered around. The school’s power is still on, so that seems like a good place to start looking, especially after a bunch of locals reports that some others were already asking around and headed there, and that there was until yesterday a blimp hovering over the school sports field, providing trid broadcasts, scores, replays and the like in exchange for a spot to tie up “for repairs”.

Noone seems to know whose blimp it was, who was doing the repairs, or who made the deal in the first place, it was a nice feature for a week and now it’s gone. The team heads over the school and drone scouts the area, finding that it’s empty (it being a sunday) other than maintenance staff. However, the maintenance seem unusually well-deployed, though not armed or cybered-up. They also don’t seem magical, though Aradia spots an artfully cast “eyes of the pack” spell on all of them. They eventually ID two plain-clothes people who do have some ‘ware, who seem to be in charge, and doing some matrix recon, Heat spots that they all seem to be rocking AZT brand gear, rare in these parts, given GW’s violent antipathy. Heat also sees there’s some sort of unusual local Resonance effect which seems to have attracted some Sprites and other e-life to the area.

Aradia reports that the magic is weird again, with some similar unidentifiable sensations, but with the opposite effect- this appears to be an area of strong mana flow, and Aradia reports that spellcasting might be a little wild or overcharged. She also calls Simonetta Chandra to ask about AZT opps in the area, trying to avoid an unnecessary fight. Chandra tries to stay dominant but Aradia decides enough is enough and lays it on thick with the intimidation, which Chandra seems to find perversely pleasing. She makes some calls, and tells the team to head in- their operatives on site have been told to cooperate, so long as the team also shares what they know. Aradia is able to big dog hard enough to leave those parameters somewhat loose.

They head in and meet the AZT plain clothes team, who show them where the cable was tethered, and some of the sharp-eyed team members spot a carefully patched hole that looks to have been recently drilled through the concrete floor and then repaired. They ask to see the basement, and the AZT guys insist on calling it in again, and then after it is approved, make the team give them some info and assurances first. Then they bring them downstairs, where they find the other two AZT plainclothes operatives- a techie working on some panels in a large control room behind a (formerly) hidden door in the otherwise normal school basement, and a mage named Aguilar who introduces himself as team lead. Aradia sees that a bloody mirror image spirit of him seems to be the entity maintaining the spell outside, and recognizes it as a fairly powerful blood spirit.

Otters is quickly able to tell from the control room that they are likely sitting on top of a secret, small nuclear reactor, and Aguilar confirms that AZT has left some various surprises around town after having been forced to “pull out”. Otters starts looking around with TD, and they find on the back of the power flow control panel where another one of the ruggedized commlinks was bolted on, though it’s gone now- this extraction seems to have gone a lot smoother. Aguilar is dissatisfied with the amount of info the team is sharing with his team, feeling they aren’t holding up their end, and tries to pry further, including having his blood spirit attempt some magical interference. Heat manages to shrug it off as Aradia defensively counterspells for the team jic. They also teamwork intimidate about the major AZT secret they now know and how it would be unwise to further start stuff with them. Aradia gets a hard look from Aguilar in response but he seems to have orders from above and lets them go.

To make a peace offering, they send a little data packet on some of what they uncovered in Archer Heights to Chandra, who seems perfectly happy with how everything went and warns Aradia she’s made an enemy out of Aguilar, texting some popcorn emojis as well. Reviewing what they know, the team starts to wonder if that shipment of manavoltaic rods they intercepted was either used in this experiment (or whatever it is), or if they were meant to be and that was part of why the two areas had such different results. Regardless, they got much better images of the blimp this time from the AZT team, and Otters is able to ID it, give them a rough idea of crew complement., and spot some mods. They head back to the safehouse to rest after a long day, and Otters sends Morticia out to stealthily fly around looking for more of the blimps, since with their best guess of timeline there must be at least two.

Heat wakes up the next day and spends much of the morning on deep matrix research, scanning MeFeeds and messageboards for any trace of blimps as well. Between him and Morticia, they locate three- one out near a DU Research Campus in Front Range, one out on the Oceti Sakowin border in Brighton, and one hovering over one of the main stadiums downtown, providing much boosted entertainment to the masses. They decide to go after the rural one first, and head towards the border. Doing some scouting as they close in, they spot a ZDF sniper/observation team watching the blimp, which is tethered juuuuuust barely on the Denver side. They spot some Wildcat teams on the opposite side of the border, similarly concealed and observing. Morticia is able to spot another stealthed cable running down to the OS side of the the border, though it’s unclear if either side’s forces are aware.

As they watch, some kileg lights along the border flicker and go out, before powering back on to the hum of some generators, so they decide they better do something before an international incident kicks off. Aradia manifest near the ZDF team and announces herself, which startles them though they play it off well. She says she has a team on hand who is going to deal with the blimp, allowing the ZDF some plausible deniability without them having to take action this near the border, and after sending out a comms relay drone and running Aradia’s street name and cred, they agree surprisingly quickly. Aradia heads back to the van, and they decide against trying to contact the Wildcats, hoping they can keep everything on this side of the border. They prepare their assault, with Aradia levitating invisibly and Heat loading into a fully stealthed VTOL to make their approach, while Otters and TD provide support from the van.

Next up: blimp heist!

One of the guards tells TD that cremation benefits are included in the job, so they stack the dead alongside the building, and a branded morgue van quickly arrives for pickup.

Heat calls Niki and reports in on the job, she tells them to hang tight and they’ll be by shortly to get everything back up and running. A limo pulls up out front shortly thereafter, and Niki and Vishala step out. PPJ, badly wounded and looking rough, casts an illusion to make himself appear clean and fresh, but Vishala seems to see right through him and he wilts a bit. Otters tries to subtly help pull other people’s bone fragments out of his face. Heat has better luck, lightly flirting with Vishala, though he fails to convince her to hang out.

However, she seems thrilled with the results, as is Niki, and the team gets paid, with an offer of an optional bonus if they can find out who hired the opps team(s). The team packs up and departs, with Otters pausing to pass some of her pay back to Niki, asking her to look into “Project: ANIMUS” on her behalf, and telling her to be careful while doing so. Back at the safehouse, Creep lays into PPJ for switching to lethal, as it put Creep in more danger without his consent. TD intervenes to dig deeper on Creep’s feelings and also on the efficacy of some of his proposed esoteric punishments.

PPJ defends his actions, in that he only has lethal area effect spells and he needed to get the mage distracted from bypassing the barrier, and it worked. Heat and Otters jump in on the side of professionalism. They all debate for a while, and Aradia does some forum research, finding a variety of different popular “runner codes” which seem to all lend support to the various different arguments being offered, though it seems that the team gets through, a bit, in terms of potential reputational costs, but PPJ doesn’t seem to have the same respect for other runners (over guards/corpos) and their social scene (such as it is) as Heat does, for whom it is a more going concern.

Regardless, they find a few points of agreement and plenty of points of disagreement before everyone abandons it for now, deciding against any new team norms for the time being. Heat tracks down the opps team TM, Sidle, and messages her requesting a meet. She proposes a neutral forum room in the Nexus, and Heat agrees. Perri is still laying low/off comms after Kludge’s death so he can’t easily check her rep. They meet up, and Heat smoothly offers some pay and a future favor in exchange for info on their Johnson, after they bond over how chaotic and egotistical mages can be.

Sidle says she would usually never do this, but Heat’s team seems pretty pro and like a good connection, and the Johnson seems to have been engaged in some general fuckery, so she shares their research on him. Reviewing the data, it seems like the Johnson was posing as an MCT fixer, but may have actually been an operative for the secretive “Asintmah Alliance”, though that is marked as unconfirmed. This team also got intel that the Johnson had hired a second team as well, which is why they went in fast and a little early compated to Niki’s tipoff.

A few people on the team also take a look at the pic of the Johnson provided, and confirm that it may by the man they met as “The Guide”, in disguise as a Japanophile corpo. Or maybe The Guide was the disguise? Tbd. They also research the Asintmah Alliance a bit, and don’t find much- seems like a very secretive and well-funded group, trying to disrupt intersections of tech and magic. Their operatives apparently all use old school devices which are hard to interface with and made of intentionally overprocessed materials to be hard to magically interact with. Other than that, not much is known it seems, though the group seems fairly new.

They pass this info on to Niki after deciding they don’t own any particular loyalty to The Guide as of now, and get some bonus pay. Resting up and patching up armor (only Creep and PPJ really took any damage), they awake to some disturbing news- there were some odd rolling blackouts overnight, and given recent events in the UCAS, people are concerned! They quickly get a call from their old Detroit contact Peregrine, asking if they’re up for some work and willing to look into these blackouts on his behalf- he wants to get ahead of it if it’s happening again. Otters races to the stores to buy batteries right away while the team debates getting involved.

While the rest of the team was off on the Metaplanes, Aradia got the group together to establish the Order of the Garter, with a half dozen interested locals, who have been cleared by Kobold of any Black Lodge connections, and examined further by The Detective for some additional psych profiles, in case Aradia needs them. Recognizing that name, Otters realizes that the man they just saw killed is none other than Cap’n Kludge, one of two surviving head admins of the Nexus. This explains all the subsidary security devices and private surveillance – he was notoriously paranoid.

She hasn’t heard about any open wetwork contracts or anyone moving on the Nexus, so she asks some questions about the enemy team, which Creep and Otters are able to give some good insight too based on comms traffic and gear.

They debate drugs and/or Mind Probe, but settle on Aradia and TD team-working intimidating her. They pull her back out of her matrix hole, and lean on her hard, and she cracks easily.

She does seem legit distressed, and was actually friends, or a sort, with Kludge. She was newer to the Nexus and was using the older man’s mildly creepy attraction to her as a means of gaining access to cooler projects and info- though she did also like him, just not in that way, which he seemed aware of. So a somewhat complex dynamic. Apparently someone ID’ed her as a weak link in Kludge’s paranoid security, as he had given her access codes to the building and his suite, and black-bagged her, threatening her and some other people she’s close to if she didn’t cooperate.

So she came over to “work on a project” and propped the alley door open. She thought based on Kludge’s skills and paranoia he’d be able to get them both out of it, and seems traumatized about the way things went down. Also she wasn’t making calls to tell people about Kludge’s death, she was checking in on her people who were also under threat and booking tickets to get out of town, not trusting what kind of response she’ll get from the Nexus and others if/when her involvement and presence get out.

Libraries and Hydras
4/1/2024, 10:37:32 AM
Runners
Creep
Otters aka Roadkill

With the team admitted to the library, they start looking around and getting a feel for the upsettingly massive scope of the place. Creep takes a thorough look around with some assistance from the others, trying to get a sense of the categorization system, and can’t make heads or tails of the whole thing. Heat detects a Resonance-based matrix equivalent of some sort, which seems very clean, almost sterile, and not quite like either material plane or Seelie Court matrices. Otters is mostly just vibing on it- great drone signal at seemingly any distance within the library’s confines. PPJ takes a look around magically and spots a number of magical books/tomes/scrolls/tablets and the like, though they are a minority amongst the overall collection.

Off in the distance, Creep spots a figure that seems slightly familiar, and heads over, eventually recognizing that one of the semi-spectral librarians seems to be ThinkTank, who Creep had only met a time or two in Seattle and who died in an explosion shortly before he joined the team. Creep tries to chat with him a bit- he seems to have some sense of who he “was” but not his full memories and seems dedicated to the library. He offers to guide them to someone who can tell them more, and they accept, brought up a series of staircases and down assorted passageways amidst the sprawling structure, til they find a small side room, where a bald, scarred ork is working on a terminal that looks curiously modern.

As the ork spins around and rudely acknowledges them, some of the team recognize him as former team member Test, an abrasive but skilled TM and researcher. He quickly confirms that none of them are scientists, but once Heat provides context for their connection and bona fides, as well as some flattery about Test’s major research discoveries on the Seelie Court, he gets more enthused and gives them some info. He explains that he is working with Masaru’s Great Library project in Alexandria, along with a lot of other TM’s who took shelter with Egyptian Coptic Church during the global TM-hunting high point a few years back. He also clears up StepMAMA’s archaic language problem, though the team isn’t quite sure how he did it.

They are attempting to connect the physical Great Library, as it is rebuilt, with this astral version, as well as hopefully linking both to the Endless Archive, as they are already having to keep a portal open to run cables and Resonance energy through to keep the local network stable and prevent everything from fully aligning to the local metaplanar translation/paradigm. When questioned further, he suggests that this is the next step in multi-realm theory- punching through the coin, and no longer needing “sides”. Unfortunately, he suggests that based on his knowledge of The Seed, it isn’t a book, and certainly couldn’t be contained in a single volume even if it was, plus he hasn’t encountered it in his cataloguing of the library, though he’s a long way from finished.

Along with everyone else they’ve spoken to, he suggests seeking out Mr. Bookbinder for more information/to become a member. They finally decide to do so, and head up another few levels, seeing a number of other TM’s working on running cable, setting up devices, etc- seemingly expanding the local net. They seem to be under the direction of an Australian dwarf named Barnaby Rudd, who the team is able to ID from the team file from some past contacts and references, and know is a longtime Library enthusiast from Egypt, under Masaru’s protection.

They reach Mr Bookbinder’s desk, a classic librarian at a classic “help” desk, and PPJ assenses him, worried of all the warnings they’ve had about not upsetting him, and is delighted to find that he appears relatively weak, especially compared to some of the relatively high force librarian spirits. Creep signs up for a membership and has to give a tiny drop of blood- this is seemingly their version of an ID confirmation. He sees the name he signed kind of magically flash as the blood seals into the book. Otters does the same, but is a little shocked to see the name “Roadkill” briefly flash as her blood seals the contract.

Finally deciding to look into her past a little, Otters asks Heat if he can get a line out to Jackpoint via all the Resonant energy. Heat manages to do so, though it is a bit unstable. Otters asks StepMAMA to just collate the number of posts with “Roadkill” as a keyword without reviewing content. Since PPJ has previously asked StepMAMA to report on any info uncovered about Otters’ past, she then confirms this instruction with him. Heat sees this conversation happening, and tells StepMAMA to first report to him with all the post results, and he will filter them. StepMAMA seems to be feeling some conflicted loyalties, but does so.

Heat is disturbed to find that “Roadkill” was apparently a relatively well-known trafficker in the Carib League, who often used animals to assist in her work. However, she was an elf. Heat decides to keep digging, and finds that Roadkill apparently went missing a few years back, right around the time that MCT scaled down their TM kidnapping efforts, and had apparently been transporting a lot of TM’s to MCT prior to that. However, this doesn’t line up with the timeline of Otters’ memories, in terms of when she recalls ARES using the AIMAD to erase her past and set her up in Detroit. Heat decides to keep looking one more time, and finds his birth name on a compiled list of missing TM’s from the region/era.

Quite upset, Heat then backs out, and sends along only the number of posts to Otters but starts giving her the cold shoulder. StepMAMA confirms to PPJ that something is going on when he inquires but is having some internal struggles with complex loyalty dynamics. Meanwhile, Creep, unaware of all of this, has begun researching furniture construction with Mr Bookbinder’s assistance, wanting to make some couches he can be inside of. After Heat refuses a few light attempts at conversation about his sudden mood shift, they continue searching the library, and with careful analysis and some targeted searches with Mr Bookbinder’s assistance, they are able to figure out that the collection seems to be magically sorted by order of acquisition- the library expands outwards and upwards as needed to accommodate new additions, with librarians seemingly magically knowing where each item ought to be shelved.

This does not bring them any closer to finding the book they seek, but they do spot a large section of the 4th floor, the size of a large building on it’s own, which has not obvious means of access. Thinking back to the conversations with Mr Bookbinder, they recall that although he rejects any destruction of knowledge, he acknowledged that some knowledge needs to be restricted, and they wonder if this hidden area is the restricted section. Otters is thinking hard on her past after Heat confirms that he found some stuff out from the matrix search, and is able to remember a feeling of being betrayed as one of her last conscious thoughts pre-Detroit.

After being completely unable to find a way into the hidden area, and confirming that it is warded with a masked alarm ward, they decide to just ask Mr Bookbinder. He confirms that the book they seek is in the restricted area, but says library members in good standing of course have access, though the book cannot be checked out or removed- it is the only known copy, and has yet to be translated, so is too precious to risk loss or damage. They get into an esoteric debate about what owning a book truly means, and eventually come to the conclusion that if it were successfully translated, Mr B would likely move it to the more public area, as the preservation of knowledge is more important to him than the physical object in and of itself.

With that in mind, Creep and Otters follow one of the librarians into the restricted section, feeling a sort of expansion of space as the librarian somehow opens a path between two shelves that didn’t seem to previously exist. They find themselves in a massive open chamber, with evenly spaced pedestals, stands, and the like each holding a single text of one form or another. As opposed to the library outside, the texts in here all have the look of magical objects, though neither of the runners are able to confirm for sure. After about a 15 minute walk, they find themselves at the pedestal of the Silver Book of Saeletra that they seek, and the librarian reaches forward, one finger transforming into a key to open the trapped mechanical lock, which causes the protective casing to flower open.

Gesturing invitingly, the librarian steps back a little bit but is keeping a close eye. Creep carefully examines the pedestal, case and lock, finding no other security mechanisms or traps, and then takes thorough images of the book itself- densely inscribed text in some sort of machine code on a silver plate encased in glass. After getting good enough looks at it for a passable forgery, he passes it to Otters, who is shocked to find that the text, when read into her RCC, seems to be some kind of program she can run. With StepMAMA’s help, she creates a partition and starts running the program, which seems to download a large text file in some unknown language into that area.

While this is happening, StepMAMA asks Otters how she feels about secrets, and explains some of her conflicted current loyalties, though she isn’t overly specific. She suggests that Heat learned some things about Otters’ past that could involve him as well, and this is why he is so chilly all of a sudden. When Otters asks if the info is “bad” StepMAMA says that although she can’t qualitatively judge, she believes many would interpret it as such, including Heat. With everything they are able to get from the book for now, they head back out of the restricted section, Otters trying half-heatedly to make conversation with the librarian, who seems similarly distant to ThinkTank. They once again feel the expansion of space as the librarian ushers them out, and find Heat and PPJ waiting.

After some tense interactions about what to do next, since they don’t think this file copy will be sufficient for the Johnson, Mr. Bookbinder offers them a private room to have a louder discussion in, so as not to disturb other patrons. They ignore him for now, while Creep continues to read up on furniture making. Heat takes a look at the data in Otters’ partition and has a bizarre slightly mind-breaking revelation from within, without being actually able to make sense of the language or symbols. However, he can feel that there is more power and more to learn in here…. for now, he asks PPJ and Creep to test out his new ability, which seems to allow him to use his skinlink and living persona to act as a kind of cyberdeck for someone he is touching.

Neither PPJ nor Creep seem to really appreciate or understand how cool and weird this is, as well as how unprecedented, and Otters, the one team member present who might, is the one person Heat doesn’t want to currently share this with. However, they decide this may be an option of how to proceed with the job. Heat suggests they take Mr. B up on the private room, and they all head inside except Creep, who continues his research. Heat confronts Otters with info about her past, with PPJ confirming that his own research had also discovered some of this. Otters is initially resistant, but starts having some weird dual memories, and decides to follow them, feeling that the whole point of erasing her memories was that noone knew, and if some people know now, she might as well.

She’s able to piece a little back together as they continue to process their conflict, with Heat attempting to explain his anger as well as his confusing situation, and Otters offering very “no regret” apologies and “explanations”, believing that not remembering excuses her from past action. Although PPJ was otherwise backing up Heat in terms of the veracity of info, he also kind of agrees with Otters on this, as he doesn’t want to be responsible for whatever he may have done on Dis when he was not in control of himself, in a time he doesn’t remember.

Otters eventually recalls that on her last job for MCT, she was supposed to bring them a promising tween kind, she remembers paying the parents for a “hiring bonus” for a prominent corporate position, and how desperate the parents were to believe all of this. She remembers that during the travel, she got a bad feeling and passed the kid off to a contact at Horizon instead, reporting to MCT that this one got away. However, at the meet with MCT, she got black-bagged and had some operations performed on her- both surgical and mental. Pushing deeper into her memories, with her photographic memory, she starts having an easier and easier time separating the full-sensory BTL style implanted memories of some years in Detroit and a deal with ARES, overlapping with some mystery missing time in some kind of black lab.

She is able to get a memory of two scientists discussing her over her while strapped to some kind of surgical table, and running the mouth movements through their lip reading program thinks they are saying something about how the “AIMAD is insufficient in this case, but she may be a good candidate from Project: ANIMUS”. Otters snaps back to reality and reports all of this to the team. PPJ is interested, and Heat is further frustrated that Otters seems to be getting some self-satisfaction and even healing out of these revelations while Heat gets more and more disturbed. He repeatedly tries to leave and gets called back in for more of Otters’ memories, with Creep finally joining them.

Otters excitedly re-explains everything to Creep, who suggests that her bad past behavior might have explained her addictions at the time- seeking an escape from the darkness of her actions at the time. With some peace made for now, and Heat still mad but announcing that he’ll get past it eventually, they decide to refocus on the job for now. Realizing that they can probably get a call through from here, Heat contacts Cat, who seems to notice his unusual downcast vibe and inquires, which he brushes off for now. However, she invites him to drink and chat in the back room of her bar when they get back, a social upgrade and mark of recognition of his growing status in the local shadows that he has long been seeking.

He gives her the info on the book and what they’ve learned/gained from the copy Otters made. Cat says she’ll check with the Johnson if that is sufficient, and reports back shortly after that it should be. Heat transfers her a copy of the file for now, but she says they’ll need to come by a bar for the meet when they return to confirm with the original copy on the RCC. They agree, and decide to head back. PPJ wants to do more research while they’re there, but Heat isn’t in the mood, and Otters, the only one without a shadow cloak, is getting very tired and hungry but is nervous about eating and drinking here.

They head out the front door and descent the step towards the tavern where Arcane waits, when all of a sudden a massive hyrda appears in the street in front of them. Seizing the initiative, they dash past it as it manifests, racing for the tavern door where a frantic Arcane is preparing the portal, though it successfully bites PPJ, damaging his armor, and also Otters, both damaging her armor and poisoning her. They all skid through the portal into Arcane’s study, and the portal snaps closed, severing one hydra head that followed them, which starts dissolving through the floor in a pool of acid. Arcane quips that this is why he never takes a job harvesting hydra reagents. He also suggests that this was almost surely the Black Lodge, which Creep confirms based on the summoning circle he spotted in the street.

After curing Otter’s poison with a combo of magic and an alchemically prepared bezoar, he sends them on their way, and they roll over to the One Up Arcade, with Otters taking a quick nap. They meet with the Johnson, who they’ve done a little research on, and confirm that “The Collector” seems to indeed be Thomas Whitefeather, though he is disguising both appearance and voice, who seems to also be the Nexus poster “Cloudmaster”. According to street rumors, he a semi-retired matrix war specialist from the PCC, who also works in the spycraft field, and seems well-respected if not always liked on the Nexus. They have a pretty interesting though short convo with him, and Heat promises to assist with further translation of the mysterious book. They warn him of the BL and he asks if the team has come to their attention of late, which they confirm, and he says that may be the connection, though he also suggests that the BL are not friends of GW, and so have to operate deep underground locally, if at all.

The Collector requests any future similar books they encounter, which Mr. B and Test have both also requested, and they get paid. PPJ also has a list of overdue books from the library, just in case he encounters any in his travels. After the meet, they crash at the safehouse, with Creep ordering supplies to start building some furniture, which is just as well since PPJ is living here full time now. PPJ gets a new alert that Rosalyn Hernandez has been forced out of leadership at the IOND into an emeritus position, and looks quietly stressed/unhappy about this in the press release. He also gets a message from Darien Black telling him that his personnel files have been purged from the IOND databases but that he still has secure copies “if they are needed….”

After conferring with the team, PPJ thinks this is probably a dual purpose offer to return and also a threat. He decides that although this is some forward progress, as requested, he still doesn’t belong at the IOND, and decides to continue as a free agent for now. Heat also gets a message from Perri with an invite link to a Nexus private forum related to deciphering the Silver Book text that they acquired. He’s also planning to write up an update on the library and it’s connections, in his ongoing investigation of magical/matrix crossovers in the metaplanes.

Rescuing the Refugee
3/25/2024, 1:08:32 PM
Runners
Creep
Otters aka Roadkill
Pepper Jack
Heat

Previously, in LA: while the rest of the team heads out to deal with GWB and investigate The Auditor, PPJ decides to finish his task for the Rat King. With Aradia’s assistance, he changes his appearance and outfit to look like a high end courier for a local service, and uses a fake SIN to take a cab to the motel that Ratcatcher is rumored to be staying at. He has a whole plan to bluff his way in but the teen working the front desk really doesn’t care. PPJ walks up to the room, knocks, and cons his way in, delivering the package. He then makes excuses to hastily leave, though not before seeing the edge of what looks like a Tarot card as Ratcatcher rips it open, seemingly thinking it might be his payment for the past job gone wrong.

As he waits for another cab in the parking lot, there is a pressure wave and a brief shriek from behind him, followed by some worried shouts and inquiries from adjacent rooms. He gets out of there, swapping clothes and cabs on Venice Beach, as well as rotating to another SIN. He gets back to the safehouse in town in time to ride back to Denver with Otters and TD, happy to have completed his task. Reviewing the footage and the team file, they conclude that this might have been one of the trap tarot cards that PPJ and other team members had heard of/seen once before, in Boston, some time back. It seems like whatever payday LBC held onto is safe for now.

The team hustles out to the mountains in the van, quickly switching to tracked mode as they head off the poor quality logging and mining trails west of town. Many of their plans involve needing to go back to town to buy supplies since they had rushed out so hurriedly, but they eventually come up with a plan with what they have on hand. Creep magnets to the VTOL while Heat rides inside, with PPJ overlanding on foot accompanied by the Mule drone (which he names Charlie, this is the one effected by metaplanar translation). Otters stays in the van to run drones, sending the swarm with the VTOL and keeping Morticia near the Mule, after doing a surveillance sweep of the area.

Otters, at the van, is about 3km from the safehouse, and first flies Creep over to a clear stone area about 1km the other side of the safehouse, which looks like the closest potential landing zone. Otters also sees large bear tracks and horn scratches on some trees, and warns of Piasma activity in the area, though they may be hibernating. She also spots some carnivorous pain pines and a snow owl nest, and warns the team to path around them. Creep rigs up det cord on a few medium size trees to block the landing zone if needed, then magnets onto the VTOL again, going stealth to match with his custom skin and armor, and Otters flies them up to the safehouse dooe.

Set into the side of a cliff face with no ledge and no other obvious entrances, Heat pops open the VTOL door and starts talking at the small quartz window set into the wood door. After a pause, he hears some muffled conversation, and then gets a response. Following some tense negotiation, he gets permission to enter so long as he leaves his weapons behind. After making an easy but perilous leap in, the troll guard shuts the door behind him, introducing himself as Slab. He seems like a skilled woodsman, wearing full area-appropriate camo and moving very softly for such a large man. He stands back but is keeping an eye on Heat, and though Heat still has comms to Creep with all his boosters, has lost comms to the rest of the team.

The safehouse is very low-tech- Slab has a cooler and some battery powered cookers and other small devices, but not comms and good magic and signal masking as it is all hewn out of the stove of the mountain-side, with the door made of thick local wood, still sprouting some green branches and small leaves. Sitting in the corner is a tall, thing, gangly individual with incredibly smooth skin and wide set eyes, who appears human. Heat introduces himself, explains the peril, and offers assistance. The figure seems to be assessing for a moment, and almost seems to be refining his use of cityspeak English as he replies.

He introduces himself as “the Refugee” as he has been referred to by Slab, but suggests that after some consideration he can be called Ersatz. After some questioning about the team’s hirer and the other Johnsons on offer and their terms, Ersatz agrees that the ASPS seems the best option and will go along. Creep gets invited into the help Heat take a look at the strange cuffs that Ersatz is bound by, but recognizing that they contain some components they can’t make sense of, as well as at least one anti-tamper system, they decide to leave them be.

They load Ersatz up into the VTOL, and Otters triangulates a path out of the mountain to maintain signal as she drives and flies simultaneously back to town. Slab rigs up an explosive trap to the door, as he throws down a rope to assist Heat and Creep down the 200m drop from the door to the scree slope below. Creep easily scrambles down, with Heat struggling but not falling. They both notice the altitude, being almost twice as high as Denver, though Creep is easily able to compensate with his internal air tanks by adjusting the balance. PPJ and the Mule are a little past halfway there, as Creep spots a stealthed chopper heading for the landing zone.

He blows the det cord, dropping trees, and see the chopper veer clear at the last moment. Otters and Morticia, on their way to meeting the VTOL, spot an invisible flying carpet steered by what looks like a spirit, with 4 others aboard, also headed towards the safehouse. Slab heads off into the woods, offering to attempt a distraction, while Heat and Creep aim to rendezvous with PPJ and the drones. PPJ goes astral to check on the flying carpet, and is instantly spotted- it’s a powerful spirit of man, 2 mage and 2 adepts, so he assumes this is the IOND team, though the presence of a bound spirit suggests they could indeed be Black Lodge, as the team worried.

PPJ frantically manages to counter a fireball, and the stealthily repositions, heading deeper into the woods to cover his tacks. Heat and Creep hear an explosion behind them from the safehouse- it sounds like someone found the trap Slab left. They close in on PPJ’s concealed position just as one of the corpo-soldier types from the chopper spot Heat, not a natural woodsman himself, as he bashes through the woods a little loudly. Heat raises his hands while stepping into a little cover, and tries to talk his way out, complicated slightly as Creep clumsily chucks a grenade. Luckily, it was only a thermal smoke so Creep pops it right away to allay suspicion as cries of "grenade! grenade! echo from a few flanking positions in the woods.

PPJ, still concealed, uses some carefully applied magical dread to lend weight to Heat’s argument, which is essentially: the target’s already gone, we don’t know where or have comms right now, and there’s another magical team en route that seems inclined towards lethality, so why don’t we all walk away? PPJ also manages to mostly blunt the effects of an area thought browse spell that seems to effect both groups (Creep further embraces cyberpsychosis to fight it off). Combined with the magical dread, this seems sufficient, and the corpo team backs off, at least visibly. Figuring this is as good as it will get for now, they head on foot back towards the logging trails, and follow them back towards the edge of town, getting a room at one of the Front Range casinos on the west edge to ditch any tails.

PPJ quickly gets kicked out after being waaay too interested in how to repair slot machines- although this is legit it seems incredibly sketchy to casino security, even more so when he presents an obviously fake SIN after getting face-scanned. He takes a cab to a mechanics bar in Elbert and drinks and talks about tinkering, getting some MeFeed channel recommendations. Heat parties with some strangers he befriends while losing money, with Creep crashing from Jazz and hiding under the bed. They left the strange Mule buried in the woods a little ways away from the casino lot for Otters to retrieve later.

Meanwhile, Otters has made contact with Louis Belaran of the ASPS, and set up a meet at DocWagon’s main hospital complex in town. She is given a room number to meet in, and makes some simple disguises for herself and Ersatz, using the large pocket of his hooded jacket to hide his cuffs. They walk in and Otters heads straight for the room, not pausing to talk to anyone, and finds what looks like a high end street clinic hidden inside what looks like a regular suite from outside. After being ushered in by a nurse, they find Belaran waiting.

He confirms that this is a street clinic of sorts, for people in their line of work, and converses briefly with Ersatz before paying Otters out for the team and confirming he owes them some favors as well. Belaran confirms that it was good not to attempt to remove the cuffs as it could have triggered an astral beacon of sorts, suggesting that Ersatz may be fleeing from multiplanar captors. Otters heads out and returns to the safehouse after some evasive driving, marking the clinic in the team file for future use. She also analyzes the small data chip that Ersatz slips into her hand as she exits.

It contains some data that seems sort of like simsense recording but not quite, and seems to confirm that Ersatz had previously been a captive of the Black Lodge, and that they were in touch with Rosalyn Hernandez, the founder and head of the IOND, who was one of the Johnsons seeking him. The team all links up the next day and goes over the data, as well as doing further research, and determine that Hernandez def fits the BL portfolio- a human and magic supremacist, though she has always espoused an “all-magic welcome” philosophy counter to the BL’s human supremacy. In the footage provided, she seems to fit the role of a powerful person outside the org chart, so it seems like she may either be a past members or a high level contact.

PPJ stays up all night writing it all up and then delivers it to Darien Black. After an intense conversation, PPJ ends up leaving the IOND- though he believes Black will do the right thing, in his way, Black makes the argument for slow, careful, political movement to remove the BL from the IOND, whereas PPJ desires action. Black pulls the “not sad, just disappointed” card amongst other classic parental moves, but PPJ is finally unswayed, and tells him to be in touch once he actually does something about it all, as he doesn’t want to continue working for an organization that may even tangentially benefit the BL. He sees on his way out that onsite security has already restricted his access privileges.

He heads back to the safehouse, now his only residence, and sadly watches OrkDadToolTips repair videos for the rest of the day, while Creep tries to talk to him, not realizing he is on mute. Creep ends up down a couch reupholstery rabbit hole, given his recent fixations. After regrouping the rest of the day, the team wakes up the next day to a call from Cat, offering another job, telling them to come meet them in the private back room in her bar. They head over, and get the spiel: a Johnson called “the Collector” wants a special book. The pay is good, but it involves long distance travel, which is being arranged. Cat won’t give more details until they agree on taking the job and pay, so Heat negotiates, once again feeling like he has maxed out the encounter.

Cat gives the deets: there is a book called the Silver Book of Saelatra, colloquially known as “the Seed”, that the Johnson wants retrieved from the astral Library of Alexandria, a hyperplane of the plane of Kindred. Arcane will portal them there once again, but how they get the book from the library is up to them. Excited for another metaplanar voyage, they head back to the safehouse to do some research, and Heat gets confused by rumors of a digital/Resonant library also existing, and becomes convinced they are meant to go there instead. The team convinces him to check the astral one first, and they can’t think of other required gear, so they head to Arcane’s. He confirms that the library is a sort of tripartite structure, with astral, material and Resonant representations, somewhat validating Heat.

Most of the team still has intact Shadow Cloaks, so they wear those, but Otters just goes in raw. Arcane once again has set up the appearance of a ritual to open the portal but seems to just gesture it open. They see a cobbled street and some sort of Victorian-era structures, and head in. Arcane accompanies this time as the portal closes behind them. He points to a tavern, and say to find him there when they’re ready to return, and suggests not making enemies of the librarians. Otters seems to have taken on a distinctly steampunk vibe, with lots of gears, pistons, steam vents and the like on her and the drones. Charlie is looking especially weird now.

Turning around, they find that Arcane has dropped them right in front of an entrance to the massively wide, towering library hyperplane. They spot some cloaked figures watching them from a nearby alley, and Heat approaches, causing them to fall back out of sight before he can speak to them. Creep approaches the library door, curious about “the Seed”, as he found some interesting references to it in the team file, from a past team’s time on the Seelie Court. He knocks on one of the massive bronze doors, and it is opened by a tall, bowed librarian, who when questioned moans at them to speak to Mr. Bookbinder. They see a massive array of shelves in book heading up out of sight and off into the distance on either side. More interestingly, Heat is able to detect some kind of network here, seeming like something halfway between the material plane matrix and the strange equivalent of the Seelie Court- more useful paydata for Perri!

Dark Waves in the Green Bay

Runners

The team mostly rests up while Heat and Pepperjack do some research, and compile some questions for their friends to post/look up on Jackpoint in the morning. PPJ splits off to go deal with Ratcatcher as promised to the Rat King on ZCorp.
